# Decisions

## D-374: wizard actions and proximity belong to the dialog's layouts

**Approved by the owner, 7 November 2026 at 11:24.** No Cancel beside a close ×. Back is secondary, absent on the first step; Next or Create project is primary. These are the only two footer button types, right-aligned at the bottom, outside the scrolling body. The close control uses the existing dismissal command, preserving cancellation of tracked creation rather than silently removing that action.

Labels and controls are the closest pair, items are next, sections are furthest apart. The dialog owns one content inset across heading, body and footer; parent Inline and Stack layouts own the gaps. The project mark and title use the approved mock's aligned row, never directly stacked without space. No button geometry or unrelated component styling changes.

**Affects:** [New project](../experience/projects.md#creating-and-closing), IA-VIEW-PROJECTS, the project mock, Dialog's optional footer slot, and the wizard rule in AGENTS.md. Owner screenshot references are retained on the project page.

## D-371: captions use only confirmed image-capable connected integrations

**Decided (implementation, 6 November 2026; IMG-09).** Caption capability is explicit server metadata, never a model-name guess: Ollama's saved endpoint reports `vision` through `/api/show`; OpenAI-compatible model lists report `image` in the selected model's `architecture.input_modalities` or `input_modalities`. Missing metadata is unsupported, not a silent fallback. This initially covers Ollama and compatible services such as OpenRouter that publish modality metadata; other integrations remain available for rewriting. The caption choice never changes the Writer.

Generate sends only an instruction and a renderer-decoded, metadata-free PNG, at most 2,048 pixels on either edge and 5 MiB. The main process validates those limits again and constructs only the provider's image request. It never sends note text, names, paths, other assets or location metadata, uploads a public URL, starts a model or adds a destination. Capability checks contact only the person's saved integration.

The dialog retains the image and existing caption. Stop cancels its own bounded AI stream, not a rewrite. The editable plain-text result is applied only after rechecking source bytes and the mapped immutable image node; changed, replaced or deleted images retain the result for copying and require reopening before regeneration. Use caption is one ordinary Undo edit and never changes the description. Capability and generation errors have explicit retry/manual recovery.

**References:** [Ollama model details](https://docs.ollama.com/api-reference/show-model-details.md), [OpenRouter model modalities](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api/api-reference/models/list-all-models-and-their-properties.md).

**Affects:** [Auto caption](../experience/images.md#auto-caption-using-ai), [Writing Help](../help/write.md#write-an-image-caption-with-ai), [caption waits](../architecture/perceived-performance.md#other-image-waits-planned), WP-9.5.

## D-370: image clipboard and Save image never disclose the source location

**Decided (implementation, 6 November 2026; IMG-03, IMG-11).** Copy/Cut writes decoded pixels plus an HTML data image containing escaped description and caption, with no asset path. Ancillary location/camera metadata is stripped using the existing public-image sanitizer. Cut and Replace track the captured image through editor transactions; deleted targets are refused, never redirected to another image with the same source. Replacement imports exactly one new managed file and keeps the current words and display choices.

**Save image…** exports the decoded raster as PNG through the native Save dialog with overwrite confirmation. The default name is `image.png`, not a note or source path. Non-PNG destinations are refused; a temporary sibling and atomic rename preserve any existing destination on write failure. Remembered Rounded corners / Limit height are serialized device-state writes, separate from each image's Markdown and normal Undo. A failed defaults write keeps the last saved defaults.

**Affects:** [Image menu](../experience/images.md#image-menu-and-selection), [Writing Help](../help/write.md#copy-replace-or-save-an-image), [image waits](../architecture/perceived-performance.md#other-image-waits-planned), WP-9.1.
## D-372: Image-only editing, handles first, original preserved

**Status:** Approved by the owner, 6 November 2026 at 20:56: “The image edit design is approved, you can go ahead an dimplement it.” **Affects:** [Images → Crop and rotate dialog](../experience/images.md#crop-and-rotate-dialog), IMG-04, IMG-10, CP-FLOW-01, CP-SAFE-01, SC-IMG-03, UC-IMG-04; supersedes D-327 and the earlier destructive-save interpretation.

The owner's source says “Opens a dialog/modal”; the 14:17 feedback asks to rethink the entire editing task flow and benchmark established UX. Keep the image-only modal, but put direct crop handles first, followed by Free/Original/1:1/4:3/16:9, rotate, flip, Reset, Done/Enter and Cancel/Esc. The [source benchmark and failures](../experience/images.md#benchmark-established-crop-flows) distinguish verified help from Word's 404 and Apple's 429.

Done stages a new bounded managed image copy and changes the note's relative link as one ordinary Undo transaction. Never overwrite the original. Ctrl+Z and Restore original recover the retained reference even without Git; other references keep their pixels. Keep every unused copy: no automatic deletion. Each copy obeys 20 MiB / 40 MP limits; Git projects version the note and referenced asset together, offline files retain them locally. Storage growth is the explicit trade-off for data safety and plain Markdown interoperability, rather than inventing portable crop directives unsupported by other readers.

**Image-move integration, 6 November (coordinator):** the one persisted provenance key is `original=encodeURIComponent(Markdown-relative-src)`, not `originalSrc=`. The in-memory image attribute remains `originalSrc`. Shared destination scanning, same-root rebasing, cross-root media copies and sidecar duplication retain this outer encoding; the file path's own percent escapes are decoded separately. Copies validate and copy both the displayed asset and its kept original before publishing the note, strip metadata from both when becoming public, and leave the source assets untouched. Restore and Undo must show the correct pixels after each operation.

The gallery demonstrates fake-data state transitions only; persistence, normal editor Undo, source-version guards and asynchronous saving remain a post-approval implementation. The modal is grounded in the owner's actual source, so no unwanted in-place alternative is added.

**Owner revision, 15:44:** "Image editing icons are well established, ples use icon btns and fit fucntionalities in a toolbar approach at the bottom. Should also be able to specify exact height and width." Use one bottom icon toolbar, established Fluent crop/rotate/flip/reset glyphs, tooltips and accessible names. Exact pixel width/height and a ratio-lock manipulation toggle share the toolbar. Typing updates the crop, locked dimensions follow its ratio, and out-of-range sizes clamp with an inline explanation. Drag inside the crop to move; remove the centre square. Done/Cancel, copied result, preserved original, Undo and Restore original remain unchanged. Approval is still pending.

**Owner revision, 16:43, word for word:**

> Height and width should be just H an W inside the input filed wrapper.
> - Wrapper with the visual style of the input
> - H or W
> - Value (This filed should not be highlighted on focus, the wrapper border should be highlighted instead.)
> These are very well established patterns, should not require me to micro manage these stuff.

Use the design system's shared TextField prefix, not mock-only styling: W/H inside the one input wrapper, full accessible names Width (px)/Height (px), wrapper-only focus, no visible labels above. Arrow Up/Down steps one pixel; Shift steps ten. An aspect choice only shrinks and centres within the current crop (600 × 700 → 600 × 600 for 1:1), never restores full-image bounds. Keep lock state explicit, typing ratio-preserving, out-of-bounds feedback inline, tooltips on every icon and all compact controls aligned. [The crop section's self-review](../experience/images.md#proposed-task-flow-and-copy-policy) records verified Figma/Photoshop/Google Docs comparisons and the unverified Canva help fetch. Adopt standard controls without waiting for owner micromanagement; design approval remains pending.

## D-373: Gallery crop evidence under the unchanged strict CSP

**Status:** Design-preview correction (6 November 2026), not desktop runtime approval. **Affects:** [Image editing gallery and limitations](../experience/images.md#gallery-review-and-limitations), the gallery build, ImageCrop tokens and fixtures.

The initial link had accessibility-tree evidence but inadequate rendered evidence. Main reproduced mouse/stability timeouts and two blocked style tags. Independent normal-mouse headless Chromium isolated the CSP reports to Fluent Icons' Griffel stylesheet insertion. Use the installed library's documented atomic headless imports and external CSS in the gallery build only; never loosen the CSP or modify the desktop's icon styling. Budget the crop stage separately from a note image, preserving source geometry as it shrinks. Reinspect the complete path in light/dark/narrow and take the owner-requested screenshots before another review handoff. Automated tests and runtime wiring still await explicit design approval.

With the owner's D-372 approval at 20:56 on 6 November 2026, shared `packages/ui` imports now use Fluent's headless exports and external CSS directly; the gallery-only transform is removed. App and gallery keep the same SVGs under the unchanged `style-src 'self'` policy.

## D-367: Disabled preserves colours; Switch labels have a gap; Off parents disable dependents

**Status:** Accepted (the owner, 6 November 2026, 10:39). **Affects:** [Foundations → States](../design/foundations.md#states), [UI states audit](../design/ui-states-audit.md#disabled-controls-6-november-1039), [OFB.25–OFB.28](definition-of-done.md#owner-feedback-batch-6-november-ofb), IA-VIEW-SETTINGS, IA-CMP-SWITCH, DSY-15, AGT-02.

- **Same value, same colours:** disabled = the actual enabled colours at the shared `opacity.disabled`, never a hue swap. A Switch keeps its On accent/On thumb or Off track/Off thumb. Its whole row, including label and description, fades once. Remove Switch and menu disabled-colour tokens, menu descendant fades and DatePicker's disabled hue override.
- **Forced-colors menu exception:** Windows high contrast cannot rely on opacity alone for disabled content. Only under `(forced-colors: active)`, disabled menu labels and shortcuts use system `GrayText` through `cmp.menuButton.forcedDisabledText`; normal contexts retain their actual colours and whole-row fade. The scoped lint exception rejects this token outside forced-colors and arbitrary disabled hues inside it; the existing GrayText journey assertion is unchanged. Landed in `ca6ae179`, exact-green CI `42179605825`, Preview ready at 13:12 on 6 November.
- **One clear gap:** `cmp.switch.gap` aliases `space.inlineItem` (8 px at default text size), owned by the primitive's layout, not a label margin or a screen override.
- **Dependent rows:** an Off parent disables its dependent controls, removes them from keyboard focus and prevents activation, keeping the reason. Agents already provides the disabled binding and command guard; the missing visible distinction was the Switch's disabled paint, not missing permission wiring. Reuse that row-level disabled contract rather than layering another opacity wrapper.
- **Enforcement:** extend the existing button geometry/paint check, reject disabled hue declarations in the existing spacing/style lint, and assert computed gap, colours, opacity, focus skipping and ineffective activation in built Settings → Agents. Pending locks remain a distinct state that preserves focus.
## D-368: writing-window toolbar modes remain icon buttons

**Decided (the owner, 6 November 2026: "Toolbar was the exception where we have to use buttons to toggle modes... Chage the toolbar back to what it was exactly.").** Toolbar mode toggles (Focus mode, light/dark and other writing-window toolbar modes) stay icon toggle buttons with `aria-pressed`. The Switch rule covers binary *settings* only. This amends D-355; it does not change Settings Switches. The writing window is sacred, so a rule change never alters it without the owner's direction.

**Affects:** AGENTS.md, Toolbar and Trigger, `ui:check`, [OFB.5–OFB.7 and OFB.30](definition-of-done.md), [gate evidence](gate-evidence.md).

## D-366: Preview and development run beside the Store app

**Status:** Accepted (owner bug report, 6 November 2026). **Affects:** desktop startup, PKG-03, PKG-12, D-202, D-122.

The plain **Focus Notes Preview** shortcut must stay open while Microsoft Store Focus Notes is running. The scheduled builder produces an ordinary unpacked release and its launcher sets no channel or profile flags: both previously used the release identity and the same logical default `userData` path before requesting Electron's single-instance lock. Windows' Store filesystem redirection does not provide a separate lock namespace.

| Channel | App identity | Default profile and lock |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Store and ordinary release | Unchanged; `io.github.prabinpebam.focusnotes` | Unchanged |
| Preview | Focus Notes Preview; `io.github.prabinpebam.focusnotes.preview` | Legacy default path plus ` Preview` |
| Development | Focus Notes Development; `io.github.prabinpebam.focusnotes.development` | Legacy default path plus ` Development` |

Startup resolves the identity **before** restoring a project profile and taking the lock. The Store always stays stable. Unpackaged development is distinct by default; `FOCUS_NOTES_CHANNEL` can explicitly select `stable`, `preview` or `development` for builds and native tests. Existing Preview executables under `focus-notes-work/preview/builds/<commit>/`, `app/` or `candidates/<commit>/` are recognized without changing or running the owner's updater. Preview and development set their own Windows AppUserModelID; only stable releases register as the default `focus-notes:` handler, so Preview does not take over the installed app's links. Explicit `FOCUS_NOTES_USER_DATA` paths (project windows, fresh profiles and tests) stay exact.

**Existing Preview settings carry over without a move.** Until its completion marker exists, and only when no explicit profile was supplied, startup snapshots the legacy unpackaged app's inventoried durable data into a temporary sibling, then fills only missing files in Preview's separate profile. This includes settings, keybindings, OS-protected data, project profiles and recovery drafts. The last-project pointer is adjusted to the copied project profile; notes remain at their original paths. Chromium caches, single-instance locks, transient agent locks and project lifecycle SQLite files are not copied; lifecycle metadata is rebuilt by the existing project store. Store package data is never read. Exclusive file creation prevents overwriting even if a concurrent process creates a file during the copy. The original profile is never edited or deleted.

**A failed copy never blocks Preview** (coordinator direction, 6 November). It logs the cause, starts with its own usable profile, and shows one must-read notification: "Couldn't bring over your Preview settings. Focus Notes will try again next time." No completion marker is written on failure. The next launch retries only missing files, never replacing settings or notes changed since the failed attempt. `preview-carry-over.json` marks completion only after all missing files were filled; its data inventory row contains no note paths or content. Close an older Preview before first opening the updated one so the snapshot includes its latest saved settings.

**Regression coverage:** identity units compare Preview, development, release and Store, including Store's precedence over a channel override. Carry-over units prove source preservation, no overwrite, project pointer remapping, draft retention and exclusion of transient files. The native journey uses two invisible built-app processes with the same synthetic legacy default, confirms both retain their native locks and writing windows under different channels, and confirms a third process in the Preview channel is refused. It never launches or stops the owner's Store app or Preview.

Explicit profiles are resolved without querying Electron's default path: first-run tests and project subprocesses may deliberately isolate the OS profile folders, where the default path is unavailable. Native window-opacity assertions follow the shared journey policy (invisible locally, visible on CI), without changing how app instances are opened.

## D-pending: Project replaces workspace everywhere

**Status:** Accepted (the owner, 5 November 2026: "The project + Workspace design is approved. We can implement this design."; re-confirmed, the owner, 5 November 2026, 19:46: "Removing workspace and having only Project, the design is approved." and "Delete dialog looks good, it's approved." then "Go ahead and implement all of these."). The on-disk and template renames in (c) stay a separate owner decision; implementation changes user-facing words only. **Affects** [One concept: Project](../experience/project-model.md), [Projects](../experience/projects.md), [Images](../experience/images.md#assets-markdown-and-encrypted-sharing), CP-SCOPE-01, SC-PROJ-04, SC-PROJ-05, UC-PROJ-07, UC-PROJ-08, IA-VIEW-SETTINGS, IA-VIEW-FRE, [Foundations → Words](../design/foundations.md).

The owner wrote: "Project and workspace are the same thing. Why have two separate concepts. It's confusing. … eliminate the term workspace completely from the app. all projects have version control with git. Projects can be completely offline or linked to a GitHub repo." The full words are on [One concept: Project](../experience/project-model.md#the-owners-direction).

- **One noun.** "Workspace" leaves every message, menu, dialog title, Help page, CLI message and accessible name; **Project** is used instead. The inventory with its proposed wording is on the spec page. `migrate.notice` keeps the one sentence that explains the rename.
- **Every project has Git.** A project is either **on this computer only** (local Git) or **linked to GitHub**. Linking is a later, optional step; offline is a complete state.
- **Settings loses the Workspace tab.** Its contents become **Where it's kept** at the end of Settings → Project (mocks `pnw.linked` and `pnw.offline`).
- **Standalone files are the only thing outside a project:** edited in place, no version history, no images for types such as .txt, and an image insert creates a folder beside the file (mocks `pnw.fileMd` and `pnw.fileTxt`).
- **For the owner to decide separately:**
  - (a) Local notes becomes an offline project, as recommended, or is removed;
  - (b) flatten standalone images to `<file>.assets/<id>.<ext>`;
  - (c) rename `.focus-notes/workspace.json` to `project.json` and the template repository `focus-notes-workspace` to `focus-notes-project`, with a migration that reads both.

  Nothing on disk is renamed until then.

Rejected: keeping "workspace" as the word for the folder and "project" for its identity (two words for one thing is the confusion the owner named), and a silent alias in the UI. The orchestrator assigns the number.

**Built in four slices** on `wp-project-not-workspace`: the words; the merged Project tab; Git for every project (Link to GitHub follows after a security review); This file Settings for standalone files. Slice 1 changes text only except the `--project` CLI spelling, mapped to the existing `workspace` argument key with `--workspace` kept as a silent alias. No data, repository, IPC, message-key or on-disk rename.

## D-pending: Delete a project: hand-off to GitHub's Danger Zone

**Status:** Accepted (the owner, 5 November 2026, 19:46: "Removing workspace and having only Project, the design is approved." and "Delete dialog looks good, it's approved." then "Go ahead and implement all of these."). Wired with fakes only: no real repository is ever deleted by an agent, and any real-account test asks the owner first. **Affects** [Deleting a project](../experience/project-model.md#deleting-a-project), CP-SCOPE-01, SC-PROJ-08, UC-PROJ-13, IA-VIEW-SETTINGS, DD-PROJ-09 to DD-PROJ-17, PRJ-23, WP-P.23, MP.34.

The owner's full words, feasibility citations, task flow, screens and recovery table are on the experience page. Recommend **(b)**: after consequences, preservation and typed project-name confirmation, open `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/settings` through the safe external-link path. GitHub owns Danger Zone and its own confirmation/sign-in. The app observes each chosen repository, then finishes the explicitly approved local cleanup and returns to Open. The success pill is inside Open because toasts drop under dialogs.

Reject **(a)** in-app step-up: GitHub supports deletion, but GCM, gh and pasted fine-grained tokens do not share a universal permission-upgrade path; normal sign-in does not include deletion rights. Reject the **hybrid** API/browser flow: two confirmation/failure paths for one action, depending on incidental credential permissions.

**Safety:** a 404, even with `GET /user` returning 200, can mean lost repository access rather than deletion. It triggers verification and explicit confirmation that the person deleted the repository on GitHub, not automatic local deletion. Retained private notes must be preserved outside a parent folder before it is recycled. The build keeps both safeguards: the 404 asks for that confirmation before the Recycle Bin step, and a retained private repository is moved out before its parent folder is recycled. The orchestrator assigns the number.

## D-pending: Destructive actions use the danger button, from semantic tokens

**Status:** Accepted (the owner, 5 November 2026: "Delete project should be semantic danger/critical, red color btn. Use proper token implementation for this."). **Affects** [Deleting a project](../experience/project-model.md#deleting-a-project), [Components → Button](../design/system/components.md), `packages/tokens` (`color.danger`, `color.onDanger`, `cmp.button.danger`), `packages/core/src/contrast.ts`, DD-PROJ-09 to DD-PROJ-17.

The existing danger family is reused, not a parallel one: `color.danger` is the fill and `color.onDanger` its text, defined in every theme, light, dark and High contrast. Button's `danger` variant reads `cmp.button.danger`; its hover and pressed are the shared `color.hover` and `color.pressed` overlays drawn on the red fill (they were the fill itself, so a hovered danger button didn't change). Focus is the shared focus ring and disabled the shared opacity, the same as every button. The contrast guard now checks `color.onDanger` on `color.danger` at rest, hovered and pressed, 4.5:1 (7:1 in High contrast), in every built-in theme. Views pick `variant: "danger"`; they never style it. Only the action that destroys (**Delete project…** and its final **Delete project** confirm) is red. Cancel and the GitHub hand-off are not. The orchestrator assigns the number.

## D-pending: article entries and reader controls match the public-site preview

**Status:** Accepted correction; live-site control design only (owner, 5 November 2026). **Affects:** [Published website](../experience/published-site.md#owner-corrections-5-november-article-entries-and-reading-controls), desktop site preview, share/publish mocks, ProjectScope and reading token scopes; SHR-06, SHR-20 to SHR-22.

The owner's three corrections are recorded verbatim on the experience page. Articles have no trailing chevron; this does not remove section or app-card navigation cues. The actual generator already emits plain article links without chevrons. Preview article titles, excerpts and body follow its reading font and size, without changing the writing app's appearance. Light/dark/system use icons only, with accessible names and tooltips, in the existing preview and published-site design.

Reuse SegmentedControl's opt-in native icon-radio variant in the preview and existing share/publish mocks. Main clarified that a control absent from the generator may be added only after implementation approval. No such approval is recorded: remove the initial ReaderPage control and root theme selectors; preserve the generator's existing script-free device theme behavior. Live controls, persistence and real publication remain later work, not implementation in this correction. The orchestrator assigns the number.

## D-pending: GitHub Actions runs are batched on accumulated work

**Status:** Accepted (owner, 5 November 2026). **Affects** [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md) (Modes, gates and merges), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `nightly.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `pages.yml`, D-113, D-206, D-255.

The owner wrote: "Do we need these many and this frequent push to actions... can be optimized with much lesser number and frequency by batching it on accumulated work." Each push to `main` started ci, nightly, codeql and Deploy app, and every work-branch push, `vq-*` included, ran the full ci. Now:

- **nightly** runs once a day and by hand, never on push.
- **codeql** runs weekly, by hand, and on pushes to `main` that change `apps/`, `packages/`, `tools/` or the root manifests and lockfile; the `pull_request` trigger is gone (only Dependabot opens pull requests here).
- **ci** skips pushes that change only `documentations/**` (Help, the docs' manifest and index stay in, because the site build and its journeys read them), root `*.md`, `.github/skills/`, `.github/agents/` and the kit pointers. `vq-*` branches no longer run it: designs the owner hasn't approved run no tests, and review-captures stays. Concurrency still cancels an older run of the same ref, and `workflow_dispatch` gives a skipped candidate head its exact-head run.
- **Deploy app** runs on pushes that change what it deploys: the web app, `packages/`, the docs, schemas, Slate, the product site, notes and the root manifests; its nightly schedule and dispatch stay.
- **Working rule:** push a work branch only as a landing candidate, bundle ready branches on one `fix-merge-candidate`, send docs-only and agent-file commits to `main` without CI, and don't move `main` with follow-up docs or test-only commits while another branch's gate is in flight, because every in-flight branch then merges again and reruns.

The gate that protects `main` is unchanged: desktop runtime changes still land only on a green Windows CI run of the exact commit. The orchestrator assigns the number.
