## D-364: AI actions open the approved prompt; no chains, scripts or plugins

**Status:** Accepted (the owner, 6 November 2026, 02:07: "The improvement spec and framework for "Actions" look good. Implement this. I'll validate it by actual user testing."; the spec's recommended defaults on its five open questions are taken as approved). **Affects** ACT-10, ACT-11, ACT-13, ACT-14, UC-ACT-04, UC-ACT-09, RWR-70, D-256, D-303.

- **AI-backed rows open the existing inline prompt** on their anchor, pre-filled for a preset, showing integration, model and where it runs; nothing is sent from Actions, and only the anchor and prompt are sent. "Presets never send" (UC-RWR-01) holds. No model runs in Focus Notes (RWR-70).
- **No chaining or macros**, no scripts or shell commands, no plugin surface. Agents keep MCP (AGT-01 to AGT-06).
- **User-defined actions are a later stage, waiting for the owner's yes:** saved prompts and text templates only, in `actions.json` in the app's data folder (never committed, because a project can be public), described by `schemas/actions.schema.json` version 1.
- Rejected: running presets directly from Actions (sends without the disclosure the owner approved); "ask AI about my notes" (sends private notes beyond a selection); project-stored prompts (R-59).

## D-363: One anchor per action, grouped and named by it

**Status:** Accepted (the owner, 6 November 2026, 02:07: "The improvement spec and framework for "Actions" look good. Implement this. I'll validate it by actual user testing."; the spec's recommended defaults on its five open questions are taken as approved). **Affects** ACT-04 to ACT-08, ACT-15, UC-ACT-01, UC-ACT-03, UC-ACT-05 to UC-ACT-07.

From the owner's "independent actions" and "actions that need an anchor… explicit… implicit", and "enough distinction for each action so that it's not confused with anything else":

- Each action has exactly one anchor: app, project, current note, a selection kind, or a chosen result. Results are grouped *This selection*, *This note*, *This project*, *App* (and later *Yours*).
- Context is captured when Actions opens; an action whose context changed refuses and says so.
- Labels are unique, start with a verb, and name their object when a verb repeats (*Rename this note…*, *Rename project…*); the active row shows a one-line outcome.
- Actions that can't apply are hidden; fixable ones are blocked with their reason and the fix as the next row; `>` browses the whole catalogue with what each needs.
- Content is the files and folders the Files panel shows in this project (D-273); headings and body lines are reasons on a file's row, not groups of their own (owner question 1).
- Rejected: one *Rename…* that asks what to rename (an extra step and an ambiguous row); showing every action disabled with a reason in the normal list (the dump the owner described); a blocked row that runs its fix itself (two meanings for one row).

## D-362: Spotlight is called Actions, "Find and act"

**Status:** Accepted (the owner, 6 November 2026, 02:07: "The improvement spec and framework for "Actions" look good. Implement this. I'll validate it by actual user testing."; the spec's recommended defaults on its five open questions are taken as approved). **Affects** ACT-03, IA-VIEW-SPOTLIGHT, [Actions](../experience/actions.md#words), Help, the cheat sheet.

The owner wrote: "We don't want to call this Spotlight as that's a direct name that MacOS use. The purpose of this feature is "Find and act", and we will simply call this "Actions"… The tagline will be "Find and act"". User-facing words only: the dialog is *Actions*, its field placeholder *Find and act*, the per-result panel *More actions* (so it isn't "Actions inside Actions"). Keys, command IDs and code names keep `spotlight`, as *workspace* did in its first slice.

## D-361: Actions lists only catalogued actions

**Status:** Accepted (the owner, 6 November 2026, 02:07: "The improvement spec and framework for "Actions" look good. Implement this. I'll validate it by actual user testing."; the spec's recommended defaults on its five open questions are taken as approved). **Affects** [Actions framework](../architecture/actions-framework.md), [Actions](../experience/actions.md), KEY-01, SPT-04, D-111, ACT-01, ACT-02, ACT-09, CP-REACH-01, UC-ACT-08, WP-A.2.

The owner's Spotlight note (5 November): "We don't want to dump all actions and then figure out which ones don't work and remove it. Instead we want to put actions confidently and only the ones we know for sure makes sense and actually works." Today Spotlight lists every registered command not on a deny-list (D-111), about 200 rows including dialog steps and duplicates.

- **Allow-list.** Only actions in a catalogue in `packages/core` are listed. Each entry passes Q1 to Q7 (intent, starts here, one anchor, distinct, proven, works here, reachable elsewhere) and names the check that proves its outcome from Actions; an entry without a passing proof isn't listed. Rows whose command is missing or unproven wait as Candidates until their blocker is closed and recorded here.
- **Commands are unchanged.** Every command still runs from its key, menu, dialog, agent or deep link. KEY-01 becomes "every action is a named command; the catalogued ones are listed in Actions". D-111's "lists every action the app has" is superseded.
- Rejected: pruning the deny-list (the next dialog's *Cancel* arrives with it); a per-command `palette: false` flag (opt-out repeats the failure; VS Code's `menus.commandPalette` shows the cost).

## D-360: images go ahead in normal Git, 20 MiB each (I1 accepted)

**Decided (the owner, 6 November 2026: "Image addition is still not implemented and you stop work. I've already told you all of these needs to be implemented, why stop before it's done.").** This is the separate execution go-ahead the D-pending image approval above waited for. Open question I1 takes the spec's recommended default: images stay in normal Git, each at most 20 MiB (IMG-01), with no Git LFS. Slice 1 of [Images](../experience/images.md) (D-349: paste, drop, "/" Image and Insert image) is built in production-ready against mock 9. **Affects** [Images](../experience/images.md), WP-9.7, OFB.17 to OFB.19.

## D-359: "/" lists every block before anything is typed

**Decided (the owner, 6 November 2026: "While typing "/" what do you mean "No blocks match. I should see the list of available blocks to pick and insert like in Notions or MS Loop. This feature is not working at all. I've no way to insert a table today.").** An empty query shows every block, grouped (Text, Lists, Media, Advanced) with its icon and name, as Notion and Microsoft Loop do; typing filters the list, and "No blocks match" appears only when a typed query matches nothing. Table is one of the blocks and inserts a 3 × 3 table with a header row. **Affects** the editor's block menu, OFB.13 to OFB.16.

## D-358: the project colour border is a Switch, on by default, 2 px

**Decided (the owner, 6 November 2026: "Make the Project color border, a togglable option with Switch toggle, on by default and the border thickness should be 2 px.").** Settings → Project gets a "Project colour border" Switch, on for new and existing projects. The writing window's outline width is a 2 px token. Turning it off removes only the outline; the project pill and watermark are unchanged. **Affects** [Projects](../experience/projects.md), IA-VIEW-SETTINGS, OFB.8.

## D-357: notifications dismiss themselves unless the person must read them

**Decided (the owner, 6 November 2026: "Most notification should auto dismiss. We make a notification a manual dismiss only if we want to force the user to read it. I just deleted a file and the confirmation of the delete is a manual dismiss. It doesn't warrant a manual dismiss. Audit all types of notifications and make sure it's the right type and fix all notifications.").** Auto-dismiss is the default for every notification, including confirmations that carry Undo ("Deleted"). A notification is must-read (manual dismiss) only when the person has to read it or decide something: data at risk, or an error whose recovery needs their choice. Every notification is listed in one inventory in the [UI states audit](../design/ui-states-audit.md) with its type and reason, and a test fails on any notification missing from it. This narrows the earlier rule that every notification with an action is hard-dismissed. **Affects** the notification host, OFB.11, OFB.12.

## D-356: one pending pattern per need; no overlaid spinners

**Decided (the owner, 6 November 2026: "Pin/unpin loading state has at least 2 variation, one with animation at the border and another with a circular spinner in the middle overlaid. We don't want multiple variations where the same thing would have done. So, de duplicate redundancies and minimise number of superfluous componenet variations without losing the core need and fucntionality.").** A compact control that saves (pin, icon button, Switch, menu button) shows only the own-border pending arc (dashed under reduced motion), then the success or danger outline. The overlaid centre spinner is deleted, and every primitive keeps one variant per state a person needs. **Affects** [UI states audit](../design/ui-states-audit.md), [components](../design/system/components.md), OFB.9, OFB.10.

## D-355: on/off settings are Switches, never buttons

**Decided (the owner, 6 November 2026: "Why do you use btns for toggles, who does that. It's so confusing. Use proper components, use the swtich toggle for AI agent connecting options. If you are using btn for toggles anywhere else, change it to Switch toggle.").** Every binary setting uses the Switch primitive (`role="switch"`). **Amended by D-368:** writing-window toolbar mode icons retain `aria-pressed`; formatting toggles (Bold, Italic) and segmented choices of three or more remain exceptions too. A check fails on other binary buttons. **Affects** Settings → Agents and every Settings section, [components](../design/system/components.md), OFB.5 to OFB.7.

## D-354: buttons keep one geometry and never lose their padding

**Decided (the owner, 6 November 2026: "No left padding "Create new notes instead" btn. I can't be pointing thes out one by one. The design system should fix this in a robust manner." and "Hover state is different from rest state and is misaligned for "more options" btn. How can this even happen. What unnecesary complaexity you are adding here. Bts are the simplest and basic component, get these right at least.").** Every button variant has a token-driven inline padding greater than zero on both sides that no view can override. Rest, hover, pressed, focus, disabled, pending and selected share one box: same size, padding, border width and label position; only colour, ink and the focus ring change, painted on the button itself, never on an offset layer. A design-system check renders every variant in every state and fails on zero padding, a literal value or any geometry difference. **Affects** `packages/ui` buttons, [components](../design/system/components.md#button-geometry), OFB.1 to OFB.4.

## D-353: loading placeholders are translucent, so they take any surface's hue

**Decided (the owner, 5 November 2026: "loading Shimmer needs to work on different bg hues, so shimmer can't be grey. It has to get the subtleness with opacity of black and white, else the lack of hue in the shimmer area stands out. I noticed this issue in Open dialog modal in the Project card. This should be a general fix and it should get this fixed automatically.").** Every skeleton's fill and passing light are now black or white at an opacity, set once at the token level, so a placeholder on a project-tinted card, a sunken panel or any theme's surface takes that surface's hue:

- **Tokens.** Two semantic colours, `color.skeleton` and `color.skeletonHighlight`, over new reference families `ref.color.shade` (black at an opacity) and `ref.color.glow` (white at an opacity). Light themes use black at 8% with white at 55% for the light; dark themes use white at 8% for both. High contrast light uses black at 16%, and high contrast dark uses white at 20% with a 14% light. `cmp.skeleton.fill` and `cmp.skeleton.highlight` read them instead of the opaque `surfaceSunken` and `surfaceRaised`.
- **Automatic.** No view or component overrides anything: every `Skeleton` use gets it, project scopes re-declare the theme's values with their tint (`[data-theme-scope]`), and custom themes inherit their base theme's values. Reduced motion (still) and forced colours (a GrayText shape) are unchanged (D-208).
- **Kept true.** `tools/test/shimmer.test.mjs` fails if any theme's skeleton colours stop being translucent black or white. The gallery's **Mocks: Open → Loading shimmer on project tints** shows the placeholder on six project tints in this window's theme, Paper, Ink, Sepia and both high contrast themes. Each theme uses a full-width group so project names and paths remain legible; this gallery-only layout does not change product cards.

Affects [components → Skeleton](../design/system/components.md), [perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md), D-208.

## D-352: the Open dialog's project cards are approved and wired

**Decided (the owner, 5 November 2026: "The open dialog is approved, implement it.").** The approved design is `vq-open-project-card` at `9965a7f4` (mocks `mock.open.view` and `mock.open.cardStates`). It's merged into the real Open view with no change to Open's behaviour (D-318): each project card shows its size line and its GitHub line from real data, as D-346 describes. The repository is read from the folder's own Git config; the counts come from an asynchronous walk in the main process, kept for the session, shown at once on the next Open and recounted each time. A count that fails leaves the line out. Pressing the GitHub line opens the repository through the safe link path, and if it can't, Open's status row says so with **Retry** (`project.open.repoFailed`).

Affects [Projects](../experience/projects.md#a-project-cards-github-link-and-size), PRJ-20 to PRJ-22, WP-P.21.

## D-351: publishing a note shows its deploy as one honest wait

**Proposed (worker, readiness review of 5 November 2026; ordinary behaviour, reversible).** When a note is published or unpublished, the app reports the GitHub Pages deploy the same way a share's readiness is already reported (R-53, D-344):

- **Ready means live.** "Published" is claimed only when GitHub reports the Pages deployment for the commit that carried the change as succeeded, then the page answers at its address. Until then the note's state is **Publishing…**. No new network destination: the GitHub API the app already uses, and the workspace's own Pages origin (D-344).
- **One long, leavable task.** Steps: **Saving to GitHub…** → **Publishing… usually about a minute** → **Published.** with **Copy link**. It runs in the task tracker as a bottom notification pill (the approved Publish… toast, D-335), never a new bar or badge in the writing window (SHR-24, Rule 7). Writing continues while it runs.
- **Failure keeps the choice.** Offline, signed out or a failed deploy says so in the pill with **Retry**; the note stays public in the workspace and the state stays **Not live yet**, never "Published". A deploy still running after 10 minutes says **Still publishing. GitHub is taking longer than usual.** and keeps waiting in the background.
- **Unpublish is the same wait in reverse.** **Removing from your site…** until the next deploy succeeds; the page then returns **This page isn't available**. Git history keeps the earlier text (accepted, D-274, D-299).
- **Copy public link** before the page is live copies the address and says **Copied. The page will open once publishing finishes.** (UC-SHARE-02).

Affects [Published website → Publishing a note](../experience/published-site.md#publishing-a-note), [perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md#project-and-sharing-waits-planned), SHR-26, UC-SHARE-02, WP-P.22.

## D-350: links from a published note to an unpublished one become plain text

**Proposed (worker, readiness review of 5 November 2026; records built behaviour).** On the website, a link from a published note to a private, shared-only or missing note is written as its plain link text, with no address, title or path of the target (as built in `packages/core/src/public-page.ts`, covered by `public-page.test.ts`). The approved Publish… dialog gets no extra warning or list (Rule 7, D-335); Help says it in one line: **"Links to notes that aren't published show as plain text on your site."** Why: the owner's rule that a public note never reveals private content, without adding anything to an approved dialog. Rejected: a per-link warning step (not asked for), publishing linked notes automatically (makes private content public).

Affects [Published website](../experience/published-site.md#publishing-a-note), SHR-04, SHR-25.

## D-349: images and publishing are built in small visible slices, in this order

**Proposed (worker, readiness review of 5 November 2026).** The owner asked for both features to be brought to execution readiness. Each slice gives the owner something to try within about an hour of build time, and any new surface is mocked in the gallery first (strict design) before wiring:

- **Images:** (1) add an image by paste, drop, **Insert image…**, `/image` and Spotlight into `assets/images/` with relative links (WP-9.7 with the storage core of WP-9.3); (2) **Edit description…** and **Add caption**; (3) the rest of the image menu (Cut, Copy, Replace image…, Save image…, remembered defaults; WP-9.1); (4) print and Save as PDF with images (WP-9.8, D-348); (5) images in published notes (WP-9.9, after publishing slice 2 and the owner's answer on publishing images); (6) Auto caption with AI (WP-9.5); (7) moving and copying notes with images, standalone sidecars (rest of WP-9.3); (8) encrypted images in shares (WP-9.6, after WP-P.10).
- **Publishing:** (1) gallery mocks for the states the approved mocks don't have: publishing, live, failed, unpublish, the site's empty, missing-page and failure pages; (2) Publish… and Unpublish on today's workspace with the deploy status (WP-P.22, D-351); (3) the site's own theme on the deployed site (WP-P.12); (4) the public home with sections and counts (WP-P.14); (5) images in published pages (images slice 5).

The work graph's statuses are corrected to what's on `main`: WP-9.1 (display modes, menu, missing-image state) and WP-9.4 (crop and rotate) are **building**, with their remaining items named in their summaries. Publishing slices 2 to 4 assume the owner answers yes to publishing on today's workspace ([owner decisions](../experience/published-site.md#readiness-assessment-5-november)); with a no, they wait for projects (WP-P.4) as planned.

Affects [Images](../experience/images.md#readiness-assessment-5-november), [Published website](../experience/published-site.md#readiness-assessment-5-november), [work graph](work-graph.json), [implementation plan](implementation-plan.md#later).

## D-348: print and Save as PDF include a note's images

**Proposed (worker, readiness review of 5 November 2026; amends D-230's "images, which notes don't show yet").** Now that notes show images, printing shows them too:

- Every local image prints in the note's flow at its display mode, capped to the printed text width (Wide and Actual never run past the margins) and to the printable page height, keeping its aspect ratio. An image never splits across pages; one taller than the space left starts the next page.
- Rounded corners print as shown; colours are the image's own, never theme-inverted, on Paper's colours (D-231).
- A caption prints under its image. A missing, unreadable or remote image prints its description in the body's muted italic, or nothing when it has none; nothing is fetched.
- The system print dialog opens only when every image in the note has decoded or failed (a waiting step, **Preparing images for print…**), so no page prints a blank box.

Why: an owner's printed note shouldn't silently lose its pictures; the system's Save as PDF is the export (SHR-12). Rejected: a separate PDF exporter (D-230 already relies on the system's Save as PDF).

Affects [Print](../experience/print.md), [Images](../experience/images.md#print-and-save-as-pdf), IMG-12, WP-9.8.

## D-347: image display options are written as a hidden comment line

**Proposed (worker, readiness review of 5 November 2026; records the built format and amends D-310 and IMG-10).** The built image slice writes non-default display options as an HTML comment on the line before a standard Markdown image, the same convention tables already use (`<!-- table: … -->`, `packages/markdown/src/index.ts`):

```markdown
<!-- image: wide rounded unlimited -->
![A desk beside a window](../../assets/images/q7m4k2.png "Morning light")
```

Words in the comment: `wide` or `actual` (Column is the default and isn't written), `rounded`, and `unlimited` (Limit height off). A default image has no comment. GitHub and every other viewer hide the comment and show just the picture, which is better for CP-OWN-01 than D-310's trailing `{display=wide rounded fitHeight=false}`, which plain viewers show as stray text after the image. Nothing was ever written in the `{…}` form, so no reader for it is needed; an unknown comment or attribute text stays byte-for-byte (EXT-05). Rejected: keeping D-310's attribute block (visible clutter in plain viewers).

Affects [Images → Assets and Markdown](../experience/images.md#assets-markdown-and-encrypted-sharing), [content model](../architecture/content-model.md#planned-image-attributes-and-asset-transactions), IMG-10, IMG.9.

## D-346: a project card shows its GitHub repository and its size

**Decided (the owner, 5 November 2026: "Project cards should show the GitHub logo and the name of the github repo if it's linked to a github repo. Clicking it opens the repo in the default browser. It should show total size, number of files, media files and folders in the project.").** Open's project cards (PRJ-20 to PRJ-22, UC-PROJ-12, WP-P.21) gain two quiet lines under the folder path, designed in the gallery before any wiring:

- **Which repository.** The link is read only from the project folder's own Git config (`remote.origin.url`, then any other remote on github.com), locally. `https://github.com/owner/repo(.git)`, `git@github.com:owner/repo(.git)` and `ssh://git@github.com/owner/repo(.git)` are recognised; any other host, a local path or an owner or repository name outside GitHub's allowed characters shows no line. No GitHub API or network request is made to show it.
- **Opening it.** The line is its own button, named "Open owner/repo on GitHub", never nested in the card's open action. The main process rebuilds the address as `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>` from the remote it read for that project (the renderer passes the project's ID, never a URL) and opens it through the existing safe external-link path (`shell.openExternal`, https only). No other URL can be opened this way.
- **Counting.** Total size, files, media files and folders are counted asynchronously in the main process (an async directory walk that never blocks the event loop or the renderer), cached per project folder, and recounted in the background each time Open shows, the cached line staying until the new count replaces it. The card shows and opens at once; the line fills in. A count still running after 300 ms shows a short placeholder line in its place; a count that fails (a missing or unreadable folder) leaves the line out without an error, since the card still works. The wait has a row in [the wait inventory](../architecture/perceived-performance.md#open-action-recovery) (`fn:project.stats`).
- **What counts.** Files counts every file; media files counts those among them with an image type the app already recognises (`.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.webp`, `.gif`, the types [images](../experience/images.md) read). The app recognises no audio or video types yet; when it does, they count as media too. Folders counts the folders inside the project, not the project's own. Hidden folders and files (a name starting with `.`, such as `.git` and `.focus-notes`) are left out of every number. Size is the files' total bytes in decimal units (B, KB, MB, GB), one decimal below 10 ("2.4 MB"), whole numbers above ("37 MB").
- **Words.** "2.4 MB · 37 files · 5 media · 6 folders", with correct plurals ("1 file", "1 folder"); "media" rather than "media files" keeps the line whole on a card in a small window. The size line comes first under the folder path and the GitHub line last, so the two links' targets never overlap (SP-6). No new border, fill, badge or enclosure inside the card (D-345); both lines use the folder path's quiet text and icon style. An empty project (no files, hidden folders aside) shows **"No files yet. Start your first note."** in the size line's place instead of "0 B · 0 files · …" (the owner, 5 November, verbatim: Empty projects should say "No files" or something quirky to encourage users to start working on that project.). Following [foundations → Words](../design/foundations.md#words) and ui-copy, name the empty content plainly, then invite one first action in short, warm, sentence-case copy: the same quiet secondary text, no new icon, border or fill. While counting, the placeholder itself and its wrapper are exactly one size line tall, so nothing shifts when the line arrives.
- **Card-state mock.** The blue pin to the left of Field notes belonged to the pinned recent note in the neighbouring column, not to the card. Only the card-state mock clears the recent list's pinned flags; the ordinary Open mock and real recent-note pins stay unchanged.

Affects [Projects](../experience/projects.md#a-project-cards-github-link-and-size), [requirements](../product/requirements.md), [user can](../product/spec/user-can.md), [perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md) and [the implementation plan](implementation-plan.md#mp).

## D-345: no visual noise; approved components keep their look

**Decided (the owner, 5 November 2026: "We already have a good toolbar design, do not change that. To many wrapping borders adds visual clutter.").** A toolbar customizer mock drew a border around every icon button inside the already-enclosed toolbar pill. The owner had asked to "add a border to each slot" for drag to customize; that cue was applied to every button at rest instead of only during a drag. The skills had no rule against it, so the gap is fixed in the kit (agentic-craft `7459800`):

- **`spacing`** rule 13: members of an enclosed group (toolbar, segmented control, card, menu) get no border, outline or resting fill of their own; hover, press, selection, focus and drop-target cues show only while active, and transient affordances stay transient. One enclosure level per boundary. The audit adds the `nested-enclosure` finding (S2), and the spacing probe reports it.
- **`visual-noise`**, a new skill: an ink budget, one cue per meaning, the cue order space → alignment → type → tone → border → shadow, enclosure depth of two at rest, transient affordances, decoration, colour, icon and text noise (with the [visual vs verbose score](../design/visual-score.html)), approved components never restyled as a side effect, and measurable checks with N1 to N4 severities. `craft` and `design-system` route to it, and the `design-system-auditor` checks it.

The approved toolbar stays as it is. The owner clarified (5 November, 08:22): "Yes, border was only for the drop target, only when it's active as a drop target with the accent color border." In drag to customize, only the slot that is the active drop target gets a border, in the accent colour, while a dragged item is over it; at rest, nothing in the toolbar has a border. Both skills use this as their worked example. AGENTS.md Rule 6 carries the rule for this project.

Affects [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md) (Rule 6), [the main UI](../experience/main-ui.html) and [the spacing audit](../design/spacing-audit.md).

## D-344: confirm publication against the workspace's own Pages site

**Decided (orchestrator for the merge, under the owner's "Rest you do as you see fit", 5 November 2026).** After publishing through Git, the main process may read the workspace's configured GitHub Pages site to check that the published access metadata has arrived. This is a new, explicit network destination beyond the GitHub API: only the configured site's origin, no renderer-selected host, no credentials, no redirects and no cached success. Each request has a ten-second timeout and follows cancellation. A missing page remains pending; offline or refused responses remain an explicit recoverable failure, never a successful publication. Agent evidence uses local protocol fakes only, as D-343 requires.

Affects [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md), [Sharing](../design/views/sharing.md) and [gate evidence](gate-evidence.md).

## D-343: the owner tests against real GitHub; agents never do

**Decided (the owner, 5 November 2026, 02:43: "Testing against my real github, I'll do manually, you don't do anything.").** Testing against the owner's real GitHub account is done manually by the owner. Agents never sign in to, write to or publish through a real account. Their journeys, review candidates and evidence use fakes, local bare repositories and the sample projects. The one exception is pushing this repository's own branches and `main`. Evidence that needs a real account is recorded as the owner's to try, never claimed by an agent.

Affects [gate evidence](gate-evidence.md), [Definition of Done](definition-of-done.md) and [UI states audit](../design/ui-states-audit.md).

## D-342: compact setting actions keep the toolbar open

**Decided (orchestrator for the merge, under the owner's "Rest you do as you see fit", 5 November 2026).** A compact setting action in the toolbar keeps the toolbar shown until its own-border commit feedback has finished and the person moves on. Examples are Focus mode, docking Files or the contents, and Reading size. This lets someone make several adjustments in a row and see each confirmed. The toolbar still closes on Escape or an outside click, and a command such as Find, Share or Settings… still closes it as before. In the More menu, a choice closes the menu unless it's pending, in which case the item stays inert and the menu stays open. No element is added to the writing window. Built in `b6375b3a`; [evidence](definition-of-done.md#phase-2-compact-focus-and-docking-b6375b3a).

Affects [UI states audit](../design/ui-states-audit.md) (F2) and [the main UI](../experience/main-ui.html).

## D-341: withdrawing a public share keeps its confirmation, not Undo

**Decided (orchestrator for the merge, under the owner's "Rest you do as you see fit", 5 November 2026).** Withdrawing a share invalidates its link and passcode, and the access format refuses to reactivate it. Sharing again makes a new link. Undo would therefore promise something the app can't do, so withdrawal keeps its confirmation. The confirmation says plainly that this can't be undone and that sharing again makes a new link. Cancel keeps the share. This settles audit finding F10 by its own rule ("if the link can't come back, keep the confirmation and say so in it"). Built in `46c0cb31`.

Affects [UI states audit](../design/ui-states-audit.md#f10-withdraw-asks-first-instead-of-offering-undo) and [Sharing](../design/views/sharing.md).

## D-340: Themes follows this window's theme scope

**Direction accepted from the original main, 5 November; numbered D-340 at the merge to `main`.** A Themes-panel choice updates whichever setting currently decides this window's colour theme: a project's fixed theme changes only that project; a project following App default changes the app's chosen slot, as before. Never release a fixed project to App default or change the app default as a side effect of that project's choice. No new controls or labels are added.

Preview, persisted selection and Revert use that same scope. A refused write leaves the acknowledged theme applied; Revert restores the opening project theme without rewriting app theme slots. Another project's settings remain unchanged, and a sheet belonging to a previous project cannot save after project navigation. Built in `bc45a4e1`; [evidence](definition-of-done.md#phase-2-themes-project-scope-bc45a4e1) covers real computed tokens and process restart, not only selected tiles.

Affects [project appearance](../experience/projects.md), [Theme manager](../design/views/theme-manager.html), [UI states audit](../design/ui-states-audit.md) and [waits](../architecture/perceived-performance.md). This corrects selection/application within the existing per-project theme intent.

## D-338: choose a folder in today's workspace

**Decided (implementation coordinator, 3 November 2026).** Ship New note in…
and Mod+Shift+N against today's writable Public and Private workspace folders,
without waiting for WP-P.4. The approved picker stays inside that workspace,
preselects the current folder, then the last used folder, then Private, and
creates folders inline. Cancel creates no note. Local-only windows keep immediate
New note. When project windows land, the same picker takes their scope; it never
lists or creates projects. New folder moves to Ctrl+Alt+N / Option+Command+N.

**Trace:** CP-ORDER-01 → SC-FILE-07 → UC-FILE-12 → IA-CMP-PLACEPICKER → FIL-17 / KEY-09 →
WP-P.7. Affects [Files](../design/views/files.html), [keys](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md),
[requirements](../product/requirements.md), [implementation plan](implementation-plan.md)
and [work graph](work-graph.json).

## D-267: one Settings list, one remembered default

**D-265/D-266/D-267 production boundary, 1 November:** owner approval of exact
`d40b78a345b7fe79262116b270b3a908c0966eac` at 19:59 supersedes the pending-approval
status of the historical feedback revisions below. Production hardening retains
the accepted layout and adds no controls. At 480 x 360, diagnosis measured
276.1875 px natural prompt content against 194 px usable room: normally only
the textarea scrolls, but when the chrome itself cannot fit, the complete
existing prompt must scroll to keep Generate reachable. Edge-collapsed content
is remeasured synchronously on resize. Return scroll rounds toward available
room so fractional line coordinates cannot keep it pinned by less than a pixel.
Normal/short light/dark, both Return directions and reduced-motion journeys pass.
Default's fixed tracker targets and view-only menu command classification are
kept explicit; no network, capture, saved-note or consent behavior changes.
The [current gate evidence](gate-evidence.md#current-production-candidate-checks)
records commands, results and remaining full-gate/security/assistive-technology
limits. This is a responsive defect correction to D-265, not a new design.

**D-265/D-266 correction, owner screenshot 1 November 18:12; design mode, feedback is not approval.** The supplied Discard prompt confirmation shows cut-off descenders in both **Keep editing** and **Discard prompt**. The earlier cap-to-alphabetic trim excluded the font descent while Button's `overflow: hidden` clipped it; the prior claim that this box centered complete visible text was wrong. Preserve the full token line box, center it with flex, use token minimum heights that accommodate natural content, and ellipsize only inline without vertical clipping. This shared Button/PillButton/MenuButton correction preserves symmetric padding, opaque floating hover, all Rewrite loss-protection/default semantics and the Settings layout. Static source guards replace the unsafe trim rule; real `Agjpqy` gallery specimens cover variants, sizes and icons in normal/floating light/dark contexts. The screenshot is evidence of the regression, not acceptance; build/types/ui:check and narrowly authorized ordinary-UI element images are the only verification here. Documentation rendering and all suites remain unapproved.

**Accepted, built on own-ai-default-list-build; integration and owner acceptance pending (owner, 1 November 12:03).** Settings → AI is one connections list with a directly selectable row and **Default** badge, not a separate Writer dropdown or **Use as Writer** action. Retain connection identity, icon, model/locality, key and Test state; Test/Change/Remove remain independent sibling controls. Checked radio-like semantics, arrows and Space, token-only selected styling, named pending/failure feedback and no invented fallback are required. One **Find on this PC** button says **Scans this PC for running AI services you can use.** No separate **On this computer** section; unavailable discovery stays honest.

**Default means the existing Writer ID.** Settings row choice calls the existing Writer command; Rewrite's remembered integration picker changes that same device-local ID. Reopening either surface reflects it; there is no new default store or sync. Removing the default clears it. Choosing/discovering sends no notes. D-267 changes Settings anatomy, not the later D-265/D-266 removal of consent/private blocking; serial integration must preserve those changes.

**D-265/D-267 revision, owner feedback 1 November 17:29, not approval.** Fix the defect classes in shared design-system defaults: native-control block padding/line-box metrics and trimmed label centering, opaque composited floating hover paint, related-versus-section spacing, and selected badge emphasis. Settings Find and its explanation are one semantic row above the list; Add is last. Default saves preserve mounted rows, editor, focus, scroll and input: native `disabled` had dropped radio focus, and transient progress/default text changed list height despite stable root/row identities. Replace native disabling with inert `aria-disabled` choices and use mounted off-layout progress plus announcement-only success; no app reload, remount or cache workaround. Remove redundant Copilot-ready and local-empty prose while retaining errors, privacy/locality and setup. Static source invariants and gallery fixtures accompany generated contracts in the same commit. Only build/types/ui:check and ordinary synthetic review UI are authorized; docs remain unchecked and acceptance is pending.

**D-265 revision, owner feedback 1 November 15:36 (UX/UI verdict pending).** This replaces the earlier viewport-clamp/hidden-popover behavior in the historical row below. Prompt starts exactly two text lines high, grows and shrinks with content to the existing token maximum or viewport room, and alone scrolls beyond it; no dialog scrollbar. Keep one initial x, side and retained fragment while scrolling, using frame-attached transforms without per-scroll panel-size reset/reflow. Selection or dialog leaving either edge morphs into one top/bottom directional return button; returning scrolls the retained selection into view and restores the mounted prompt/draft/focus. Both directions are reversible and respect reduced motion. RWR-87/88 and UX-POP.2/3 are revised; RWR-89/90 loss protection remains unchanged. [Design](../design/rewrite-with-ai.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md), [DoD](definition-of-done.md#inline-prompt-popover-definition-of-done).

**D-266 revision, 1 November 15:36 integration preservation.** The compact prompt and edge button retain the same disclosed Writer ID, icon, service/model/locality, remembered integration and direct Generate behavior; morphing never chooses an integration or sends text. No consent/private blocking is reintroduced. D-267's shared default Settings list and the combined CLI work remain included. Only build, typecheck and static view checks are authorized for this UX iteration; prior tests/captures do not verify it and feedback is not UX/UI acceptance.

**Trace:** CP-RWR-01 → SC-RWR-04 → UC-RWR-05 / UC-RWR-08 → IA-VIEW-REWRITESETTINGS / IA-CMP-REWRITESTATUS / IA-CMP-AIDISCOVER → DD-RWR-07 / DD-RWR-08 → AIS-16 → WP-AI.15 / MAI. Affects [Settings AI](../experience/ai-settings.md#single-list-default-choice-d-267-planned), [Rewrite design](../design/rewrite-with-ai.md), [Help](../help/connect-an-ai-service.md#choose-which-one-rewrites), [waits](../architecture/perceived-performance.md), [DoD](definition-of-done.md#settings-ai-single-list-stage).

Every assumption and design choice these docs make beyond the literal [seed spec](../seed-specs.md), in one place, so any of them can be revisited deliberately.

> [!NOTE]
> **TL;DR.** 90 decisions. D-01 to D-44, D-56, D-57, D-73, D-78 to D-80, D-84, D-85, D-87, D-89, and D-90 are settled by the owner. D-45 to D-55, D-58 to D-72, D-74 to D-77, and D-83 are proposals (D-81, D-82, and D-88 are superseded by D-90) that stand as the plan until changed. **D-56/D-57**: a strict, layered design system, and an interface defined entirely as data, with no HTML or CSS in the app. The newest owner decisions: **D-41/D-42**: private sharing with named audiences through magic links and passcodes, in the first release. **D-43**: download first, browser second. **D-44**: agent-friendly through MCP, a command-line tool, deep links, and AGENTS.md. Key owner decisions: **D-14**: 20 px corners on Windows (and Linux) by drawing the window and rebuilding window management; macOS uses its native window. **D-16**: Spotlight is <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd>, and <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>K</kbd> is the link key. **D-19**: support every platform possible. **D-21**: no OAuth app, no registration, and no hosting by the project. Sign-in goes through Git's own sign-in tools that the user owns (with device codes), and each user's Focus Notes is an isolated instance in their own GitHub account (**D-38**). **D-90**: the browser keeps its own notes and reads the instance's notes; only the desktop app writes to GitHub, and private notes reach the browser sealed, as a share with an audience of one. The owner accepted every remaining proposal on 2026-10-13.

## Status legend

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **Decided (owner)** | Stated or confirmed directly by the project owner. |
| **Decided (accepted)** | Proposed in these docs and accepted by the owner ("go with what you proposed", 2026-10-13). |
| **Proposed** | Proposed in these docs to close a gap. Treated as the plan until the owner changes it. |
| **Superseded** | Replaced by a later decision, kept for history. |

## Implementation updates

**Compact backup-card icon and spacing, 17 October 2026 (D-132 implementation; owner request).** The status tile inside the backup card is 36 px square at the default UI scale, using a compact `StepMark` variant. Internal padding is 16 px on all sides, using the popover's comfortable-padding variant. Setup's larger hero marks, their responsive sizing and other popovers' padding are unchanged. Affects [User can](../product/spec/user-can.md) and [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html).

**Save indicator, 17 October 2026 (D-132 and D-161 implementation; owner request).** Remove the desktop corner's username, retain an always-visible circular save control, and show backup details in an accessible hover card. Its border spins while backup runs, with live status in the card; reduced motion keeps the ring still. Reuse the existing recorded backup timestamp without inventing a historical time when it is unavailable. The control follows the pinned Files edge, falling back above the sheet when the window has no side room. Browser vault controls remain unchanged. Affects [Requirements](../product/requirements.md), [User can](../product/spec/user-can.md) and [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html).

**Viewport interaction, 17 October 2026 (D-160 implementation; owner request, built; corrected by the owner the same evening).** Treat the visible document canvas as a click target, not only its narrow text column. A plain left-margin click selects the aligned row's block in the existing block-selection mode, the caret at that row's start, as Word's selection bar does; a right-margin click places the caret at the aligned row's end and selects nothing; a click below the note goes to its end without adding content; Shift+click extends text selection. A drag that starts in a margin or above or below the note selects text from where the press landed to the row under the pointer, scrolling the note past its edges. (First built with the right margin selecting the block; the owner said they'd mixed up the sides.) Controls, scrolling and touch gestures are excluded. Affects [Editing](../experience/editing.md), [User can](../product/spec/user-can.md) and [Requirements](../product/requirements.md).

| Update (17 October 2026) | Status | Implementation | Affected pages |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Window input (D-147) | Proposed; built | Window move and resize pointer events are captured before a surface can stop them from bubbling. Tests cover moving and all eight resize handles while an account check is pending. | [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md) |
| Bounded account checks (D-149) | Proposed; built | Desktop GitHub API requests have a 10-second deadline, including response bodies, while preserving caller cancellation. A stalled account check shows the existing offline problem and can be retried, instead of waiting indefinitely. | [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md), [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md) |
| Portable IPC identity (D-147, D-149) | Proposed; built | The portable's short temporary path exposed a raw URL comparison: Chromium's literal `~` did not equal Node's `%7E`, so both setup and window IPC were rejected. Compare the decoded path with the exact renderer entry file, preserving Windows path case rules and refusing every other file or protocol. Verify packaged setup and frame commands from a tilde-containing path, not just development journeys or CLI smoke tests. | [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md) |

## Owner decisions

| ID | Decision | Status | Affects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-01 | **Editor engine: Tiptap (ProseMirror).** Only MIT-licensed Tiptap packages are used. The paid Tiptap bundles (Collaboration, Content AI, Conversion, Documents/Cloud) are excluded. Markdown comes from either `@tiptap/markdown` (MIT) or our own remark bridge, whichever passes the round-trip corpus in the M0 spike. | Decided (owner) | [Editor engine](../architecture/editor-engine.md) |
| D-02 | ~~Desktop sign-in through the app's own GitHub OAuth device flow.~~ The wish for device sign-in at the CLI level is kept, delivered through Git's own sign-in tools (D-21). | Superseded by D-21 | [First run and sign-in](../experience/first-run.md) |
| D-03 | **Trigger size: a 10 px *diameter* red dot inside a 30 px *diameter* frosted circle.** | Decided (owner) | [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html) |
| D-14 | **Window corners by platform.** Windows (and Linux, where a compositor allows it): a transparent, frameless window with 20 px corners, and **all window management rebuilt by the app** (moving, resizing, maximize and restore, edge and corner snapping, a Snap Layouts picker, the system menu, the shadow, and multi-monitor scaling). **macOS: the system's native window**, with no 20 px requirement. | Decided (owner) | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html#window-management-on-windows-and-linux) |
| D-16 | **Spotlight opens with <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd>.** <kbd>Ctrl+K</kbd> in the original request was a typo. <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>K</kbd> is therefore the link key everywhere, as in other editors. <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd> also opens Spotlight on every keyboard layout. | Decided (owner) | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md), [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html) |
| D-19 | **Support as many platforms as possible:** macOS, Windows, Linux, and iPadOS and iOS with a hardware keyboard (web build). | Decided (owner) | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#platforms) |
| D-20 | **License: the app is MIT.** Every shipped dependency must be under an MIT-compatible permissive license (policy below). | Decided (owner) | [Editor engine](../architecture/editor-engine.md#licensing) |
| D-21 | **No OAuth app, no registration, no hosting by the project.** Each user's Focus Notes must work with only things the user owns. Desktop sign-in goes through **Git's own sign-in helpers**: Git Credential Manager or the GitHub CLI, both with device codes, or a **fine-grained token** the user creates from a pre-filled GitHub link. The credential lives only in Git's helper, so the app and the terminal share it. The only hosting is GitHub Pages (each instance's own site) and GitHub Releases (D-87). | Decided (owner) | [Desktop and Git → Sign-in](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#sign-in), [First run](../experience/first-run.md) |
| D-24 | **Desktop window:** a custom title bar on every platform, with 20 px rounded corners where D-14 allows. | Decided (owner) | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html) |
| D-25 | **Themes:** Light, Dark, and System (auto). | Decided (owner) | [Theming](../experience/theming.md) |
| D-26 | **Toolbar:** at most 6 entries. Anything beyond that goes into a ⋯ (more) menu. | Decided (owner) | [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html#the-minimal-toolbar) |
| D-27 | **Keyboard:** a thorough shortcut strategy that follows the most popular apps, from basic to niche pro shortcuts. | Decided (owner) | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md) |
| D-28 | **Find, find and replace, and a Spotlight-style search-and-act panel** (on <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd>, see D-16). | Decided (owner) | [Find and replace](../experience/find-and-replace.md), [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html) |
| D-35 | **Every UX and UI decision is grounded in a chain:** customer promise → scenario (job to be done) → user can → view or component → design decision → requirement. | Decided (owner) | [The articulation chain](../product/spec/chain.md) |
| D-36 | **Tables are edited the way Microsoft Loop tables are, and text is formatted with a simple contextual toolbar.** Theme management is designed around when and why people theme, what they bring, and presets, randomizing, or manual editing. | Decided (owner) | [Tables](../design/components/tables.html), [Text formatting](../design/components/text-formatting.html), [Theme manager](../design/views/theme-manager.html) |
| D-38 | **Every user gets an isolated instance in their own GitHub account:** a public notes repository (created from the project's template), a private repository as its submodule, their own GitHub Pages site, and their own share-link keys. The app creates all of it on their computer. The project's repository holds only the source, docs, releases, the template, and the reusable publish workflow. | Decided (owner), amended by D-87 | [Repository and publishing → Who owns what](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#who-owns-what), [Creating a workspace](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#creating-a-workspace) |
| D-41 | **Sharing with audiences.** A private note or folder is shared with an *audience* identified only by a **name**, through a **magic link**, a **custom passcode**, a generated passcode, or a combination. This follows the access model of the owner's personal site. Readers enter their name and passcode, or open the link. The content stays in the private repository and is published only as ciphertext. **3 November:** the owner left "who you're sharing with" out (no server can track it); the name parts are superseded by D-285 (proposed), and links, passcodes and ciphertext stay. | Decided (owner); name parts superseded by D-285 | [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md) |
| D-42 | **Private sharing is part of the first release (P0)**, not a later addition, because the owner asked for it as a core capability. | Decided (owner) | [Roadmap → M5](roadmap.md) |
| D-43 | **Getting started: download first, browser second.** The product site's Get started page leads with the desktop download, and the app runs setup. *Start in your browser* (M6) is a secondary path that uses only GitHub's own pages and a token. | Superseded by D-80 | [Get started](../design/views/get-started.md) |
| D-44 | **Agent-friendly by design:** a local MCP server, a command-line tool with JSON output, `focus-notes://` deep links, an `AGENTS.md` in every workspace, published JSON Schemas, and `llms.txt` on the product site. | Decided (owner) | [Agents and automation](../architecture/agents.md) |
| D-56 | **A strict, layered design system:** tokens, primitives, layouts, compound components, fragments, and views, each using only the layers below it. Every visual value is a token. | Decided (owner) | [Design system](../design/system/overview.html) |
| D-57 | **The app's interface is data.** Every view is a JSON document mapped onto design-system components, so the app has no HTML or CSS of its own. Markup and styles exist only in the design system's primitives and layouts. | Decided (owner) | [Views as data](../design/system/views-as-data.md) |

## Accepted proposals

| ID | Decision | Status | Affects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-04 | **Focus mode default:** on for new installs, since it's a signature feature. | Decided (accepted) | [Focus mode](../experience/focus-mode.md) |
| D-05 | **New notes are private** by default, so nothing gets published by accident. | Decided (accepted) | [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md) |
| D-06 | **Save state UI:** saving is invisible. Only failures or unsynced offline edits show, as a status arc on the trigger plus a toast the first time. | Decided (accepted) | [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#save-and-sync-status) |
| D-07 | **Submodule pointer cadence:** the notes repository's pointer to the private repository moves only for publishing-relevant private changes (share grants, shared notes), plus once a day. Since D-90 every private note is in the vault, so every private change is publishing-relevant: the desktop app moves the pointer after each private push, batched so a writing session makes at most one public commit every few minutes, and the browser's vault follows within one deploy. | Decided (accepted), amended by D-90 | [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html) |
| D-08 | **Public URL stability:** renaming or moving a public note leaves a redirect at the old URL, found through the note's front-matter `id`. | Decided (accepted) | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#public-urls) |
| D-09 | **Private mount path:** `private/` (the reference `personal-site` uses `private-pages/`). | Decided (accepted) | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md) |
| D-10 | **Desktop first.** The Electron app is the primary writing client and ships in the first release. In that release the web build serves public pages and share links, and browser editing follows in M6. | Superseded by D-80 | [Roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| D-11 | **Component syntax:** generic Markdown directives (`::name{}` / `:::name`) for future components. | Decided (accepted) | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#components) |
| D-12 | **Markdown-only formatting:** no underline, color, or highlight, because GFM can't represent them. | Decided (accepted) | [Editing](../experience/editing.md#inline-formatting) |
| D-13 | ~~**Browser editing (MW) uses a fine-grained token the user creates**, limited to their notes repositories and kept in the browser (see D-81).~~ The browser holds no GitHub credential and never writes to a repository; only the desktop app does (D-90). | Superseded by D-90 | [Architecture overview](../architecture/overview.html) |
| D-15 | **Windows and Linux window buttons** are drawn by the app and shown on hover. Hovering maximize opens the app's own Snap Layouts picker, because the native one isn't available to transparent windows. | Decided (accepted) | [Desktop app → Title bar](../experience/desktop-app.html#title-bar) |
| D-17 | **Focus mode shortcut:** <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>D</kbd>, with <kbd>F8</kbd> as an alternative (Typora). <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F</kbd> is kept for "find in all notes" (VS Code convention). | Decided (accepted) | [Focus mode](../experience/focus-mode.md#turning-it-on-and-off) |
| D-18 | **The six toolbar actions:** New note, Open, Search, Save, Focus, Theme. Everything else is in ⋯. | Decided (accepted) | [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html#the-minimal-toolbar) |
| D-22 | **System Git on the direct downloads; the Store package carries its own (amended by D-228).** The installer and the portable run the user's own `git`, so configuration, hooks, commit signing, and credentials are the same as in the terminal; their first run checks for Git and helps install it. The Microsoft Store package brings its own Git and sign-in helper and never offers to install anything (D-228). | Decided (accepted), amended | [First run and sign-in](../experience/first-run.md), D-228 |
| D-23 | **Save and sync:** edits are written to disk within 1 second. A commit and push happens when the writer presses <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>S</kbd>, switches or closes a note, quits, or stops typing for 60 seconds (configurable). | Superseded by D-133 (when it backs up) and D-132 (what saving shows, and Save to cloud); writing within 1 second stands | [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync) |
| D-29 | **Fonts may use the SIL Open Font License (OFL-1.1).** Bundled fonts: Inter, Source Serif 4, JetBrains Mono, Atkinson Hyperlegible, OpenDyslexic. The licenses are confirmed in CI. | Decided (accepted) | [Design foundations](../design/foundations.md#type) |
| D-30 | **A note's file name follows its title until the note is first published**, then stays fixed unless renamed on purpose (with a URL warning). | Decided (accepted) | [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#names-and-titles) |
| D-31 | **Appearance has a light slot and a dark slot.** System swaps between the two chosen themes. Reading settings are separate from color themes. | Decided (accepted) | [Theme manager](../design/views/theme-manager.html#the-model) |
| D-32 | **One icon family: Fluent UI System Icons (MIT).** | Decided (accepted) | [Design foundations](../design/foundations.md#icons) |
| D-33 | **Undo instead of confirmation**, except for changes of audience, deletes at scale, deleting public notes, and discarding text in a conflict. | Decided (accepted) | [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md) |
| D-34 | **Recently deleted is built from Git history** (30 days shown), not a trash folder in the repository. | Decided (accepted) | [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#deleting-and-restoring) |
| D-37 | **Working personas** (Writer, Power user, Tinkerer, Comfort-sensitive writer, Reader) are used as stated, and are checked against real users once there are any. | Decided (accepted) | [The articulation chain](../product/spec/chain.md#personas-used-in-scenarios) |
