| D-92 | **The local workspace model (WP-2.3, proposed).** (1) **Layout before setup:** the desktop's notes folder is the workspace. With `.focus-notes/workspace.json` it has the repository's roots (*Private* `private/notes/`, *Public* `notes/`, D-84); without it, it's one root of its own, so everything written through M2 stays where it is until workspace setup (M3). The browser's root is its own notes, with the instance's `public:` and `private:` notes as read-only roots (D-90). (2) **Format:** a missing `workspace.json` is writable and isn't created by the app; a newer or unreadable one makes the workspace read-only with a message and a *Read only* pill, and the desktop's main process and the browser refuse such writes too; an older one is read-only until the person chooses *Update* (in the message or the pill), then migrates one version at a time. The browser keeps its notes' format in IndexedDB `meta` → `workspaceFormat`. (3) **File names:** letters, marks, and numbers in any language are kept with `.` and `_`; any other run of characters (spaces, punctuation, symbols, emoji) becomes one hyphen, so names read well in links and URLs; reserved Windows names get `-note`; at most 80 characters and 240 UTF-8 bytes (Linux allows 255), cut at whole characters. A name follows its title when it's the title's name, a numbered clash of it, or untitled, decided when the note opens: a name chosen by hand isn't replaced, and a note without an H1 keeps its name. Only notes in the workspace's roots are renamed. *Published* is a per-note flag that sync sets from M3 (pushed at least once); until then desktop notes aren't published, and the browser's own notes never are. (4) **Watching:** Node's built-in recursive `fs.watch` instead of chokidar (no dependency; recursive on Windows, macOS, and Linux in the Node that Electron ships), batched for 30 ms. An open note with unsaved edits keeps them and a message says it changed; choosing between versions is the conflict resolver (FIL-12). (5) **Redraw (PRF-11):** view models declare `reads` (state paths, or `"*"` for command states, kept while equal) and `shows` (workspace content); the renderer re-renders per region (each child of a view's top node), not per node, and a hidden region reads only its `when` until it first shows. Splitting view code per view and `VirtualList` paging arrive with the Files panel (WP-2.4). | Proposed, amended by D-102; the file name following the title superseded by D-281 | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#the-workspace-model), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#external-changes), [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#names-and-titles), [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md), [Views as data](../design/system/views-as-data.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory) |
| D-94 | **The Files panel as built (WP-2.4, proposed).** (1) **Components:** `Sheet` is a layout, not a compound, because it needs its own positioned, frosted element and its closing behavior (<kbd>Esc</kbd>, a click on the note). `FileTree` is one primitive that draws its rows (no separate FileTreeItem yet) on the WAI-ARIA tree pattern, a flat list of rows with `aria-level` and `aria-multiselectable`, rather than React Aria's Tree, which renders a treegrid and doesn't host an inline name field. Its code loads when the panel first opens (PRF-11). Move to… is composed in the canvas view from Dialog, SearchField, and ResultList, like D-76 and D-91. (2) **Opening:** *Open…* (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>O</kbd>) is the Files panel wherever there's a workspace, in both apps; the browser's in-app list of notes becomes *Quick open* (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd>) until Spotlight (M4). The system's open dialog stays only for a shell without a workspace. (3) **Batches:** each operation, however many items it covers, is one `workspace.apply` batch (create folder, move, delete): all or nothing (the desktop's main process undoes earlier steps; the browser writes one IndexedDB transaction). It shows at once, the toast comes only after the write, and a refusal puts the tree and selection back and gives the reason (PRF-09). From M3 each batch is one commit. (4) **Deleting before Git:** the desktop sends deleted items to the system trash (`shell.trashItem`) and says so; the browser keeps them, marked `deleted`, for Recently deleted. There's no Undo until commits (M3); when to ask first is as designed. (5) **Visibility:** a move between the Public and Private roots is refused with a reason until WP-3.4 makes it a confirmed move between repositories; Move to… shows those folders as unavailable. (6) **Titles:** the desktop's scan and watcher read each note's first H1 from its first 16 KB, and the browser from its stored text, so rows show titles. Renaming a note in the panel edits (or adds) its H1, and the file name follows unless the note is published, even a name chosen by hand, because Rename is on purpose. (7) **Keys:** key scopes `files` (the sheet), `filesTree`, `filesEdit` (a name field, which hides the scopes around it through `data-key-scope-stop`, so <kbd>Delete</kbd> and <kbd>F2</kbd> edit text there), and `moveField`; New folder is <kbd>Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N</kbd> in browsers. (8) **Later:** sort and Duplicate (FIL-11), each row's time and ⋯ menu and the context menu, cut and paste, the drag ghost's count, Recently deleted and Undo (FIL-10), link updates (FIL-09), remembering open folders, the full-width compact sheet, VirtualList paging, and writing a new note only once it has text. | Proposed, amended by D-99 and D-102 | [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#whats-built), [Components](../design/system/components.md#compound-components), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#changes-shown-before-theyre-saved), [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md#the-files-panel), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#files-panel), [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Information architecture](../design/information-architecture.html) |
| D-95 | **Link updates as built (WP-2.5, proposed).** (1) **Planned in core, on the parser's positions:** `packages/core/src/links.ts` plans link updates as pure functions over a note's text. Core now depends on `@focus-notes/markdown`, whose `linkDestinations` reports where each inline link, image, and reference definition destination is written, from the parser's own tokens, so nothing in code, HTML, or front matter is touched. The rewrite is text-level: only the characters of a changed destination change, and the note is never written again from the editor's document. (2) **One batch:** `WorkspaceOp` gains `write`, a note's new text at its path after the batch's moves. Moves and folder renames in Files, renaming a note in Files, and a file name that follows its title as it's typed (FIL-07) send the move and the link writes as one `workspace.apply`. The desktop writes each note atomically and undoes it by writing the old text back; the browser applies the batch in its one IndexedDB transaction. Renaming a note in Files now sends its new title in the same batch, instead of a write followed by a rename. (3) **Which notes:** every note in the writable roots is read (the open note's text comes from the editor, after pending edits are written). A note that isn't moving is parsed only if its text names something that is, after undoing escapes and percent-encoding and ignoring case, because a link into a moved file or folder always spells its name. The instance's read-only notes are never read. (4) **Matching and spelling:** paths match ignoring case and Unicode form, as the stores and file systems do. Segments that didn't change keep their spelling; new ones are percent-encoded where a destination needs it (spaces and parentheses stay as they are inside `<…>`). Left alone: links that climb out of a root, site-root links (`/…`), absolute URLs, and `#…`. Not handled yet: character references (`&amp;`) inside a destination, HTML links, and wiki links. (5) **The open note** reloads with its new links; text typed while the batch was written is kept, with the same rewrite applied to it. (6) **Saying so:** the move's or rename's toast adds "Updated links in N notes." A renamed note that changed no other note is only announced, as before. (7) **Later:** Undo (M3), and a shell-side index so a rename in a large workspace doesn't read every note. | Proposed | [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md#links-that-follow), [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#whats-built), [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#changes-shown-before-theyre-saved) |
| D-97 | **Save and sync as built (WP-3.3, proposed).** (1) **Where:** the Git adapter is in the desktop's main process (`apps/desktop/src/main/git/`), not a package, because only the desktop runs Git (D-90). It runs the `git` on `PATH`, at least 2.31. The workspace folder must be its repository's top level (never a parent repository it sits in), and `private/` is the private repository when it's its own top level; otherwise, or without Git, saving stays local with no Git UI. Git opens after the caret is ready (or after 3 s), and a note written before then is still committed. (2) **Running:** argument lists, `-C`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`, `LC_ALL=C`, `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1`, and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` for reads, with `GIT_DIR` and its kin removed. Paths go through standard input (`--pathspec-from-file=-`) and the message through a file in the Git folder, so no note name, message, or credential is ever a process argument (SEC-03). (3) **Cadence (D-23):** the main process keeps the 60 s timer, restarted by every note write (writes follow typing within a second). <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>S</kbd> and switching notes ask through `sync.now`, after the editor hands over text it hasn't sent yet; quitting syncs once more after the window has saved, waiting at most 15 s per network call. `FOCUS_NOTES_IDLE_COMMIT_MS` and `FOCUS_NOTES_POINTER_GAP_MS` set the delays until Settings does. (4) **Commits:** a save commits only the paths the app wrote or renamed since their last commit (`git add --all` and `git commit --only` on those paths), leaving the person's own staged changes and other programs' files alone. Each Files batch is one commit per repository it touches, made before `workspace.apply` resolves, with pushing in the background; deletes still go to the system trash as well. A batch whose commit fails (a hook, signing, no name and email) isn't refused, because its files have changed: they stay, wait for the next commit, and the problem is shown. (5) **Messages:** one change says what it is (`Update`, `Create`, `Delete`, `Rename`, `Move … → …`, `Create folder …`); several are counted (`Update 3 notes`, `Move 2 items to archive`, `Delete 2 items`, `Change 2 items`) and listed below, with "Update links in N notes" for a batch's link updates; the pointer is `Update private notes to <commit>`. (6) **Pointer (D-07):** moves only to a pushed private commit (or when neither repository has a remote), at once the first time in a session, then at most every 5 minutes, in any notes commit already being made, and on quit whatever the gap. The app sets `push.recurseSubmodules=check` when it opens a workspace with `private/`. (7) **Push and pull:** `push.default=upstream`; no upstream means local only. A rejected push fetches (`--no-recurse-submodules`), rebases the app's commits without an automatic stash, and pushes once more. Fetches run when the window becomes active (at most every 30 s) and every 5 minutes, and pull only when tracked files are clean; saves wait while a rebase runs. A rebase that stops is aborted: the app's commits and files stay, the remote's version stays in its remote-tracking branch, and the problem shows until the resolver (WP-3.5). Offline, commits stay and a retry runs after 30 s, doubling to 5 minutes. (8) **Lock:** `focus-notes.lock` in the notes repository's common Git folder holds `{"pid": …}`, taken when Git opens and released on quit; a dead or unreadable owner is replaced in one rename. While another running process holds it, nothing is committed and the problem shows. (9) **Status cache (PRF-07):** as specified, with each repository's first status asked when Git opens so the key knows its refs; the benchmark records 2 status processes per refresh and a cached answer in about 0.5 ms (budget 5 ms). (10) **Shown:** only problems, as the trigger's problem dot ("Couldn't sync") and one toast when a problem first appears, with [Retry]; <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>S</kbd> says "Saved and synced" or why not. Problems: offline, auth, conflict, locked, identity, signing, hook, failed. (11) **Later:** the arcs and the toolbar's status row, the last-sync tooltip, [Details], [Sign in again] (WP-3.2), the conflict resolver, Recently deleted and Undo (WP-3.5), moves between Public and Private (WP-3.4), Settings for the delays, and logs kept per device. | Proposed, amended by D-102 | [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync), [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html), [Feedback → Save and sync status](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#save-and-sync-status), [Files panel](../design/views/files.html), [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md#links-that-follow), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md) |
| D-99 | **Public and private roots as built (WP-3.4, proposed).** (1) **Visibility:** a note's root says who can read it: `notes/` is public and `private/notes/` private (D-84). `visibilityOf` in core returns private, public, or shared, where shared is a private note with an audience, so grants (M5) only add a test. (2) **Changing it:** *Make public* and *Make private* act on the open note (the ⋯ menu's *Notes* group, until Share arrives in M5) or the Files selection (the header, shown only where it applies); a drop onto the other section and the other section's folders in Move to… (now offered, described "Public · changes who can see it") are the same change. All of them confirm first (DD-FILES-08): "Make public?" says who will read it, "Make private?" that history keeps what was public. Make public and Make private keep each item's folder path in the other root, making missing folders; a clash is numbered. The delete-confirmation dialog became one confirmation dialog for both (`files.confirm`). Lifts D-94 (5). (3) **One batch, two commits:** the move is one `workspace.apply`; the main process allows a move between the roots only in a workspace and makes the target's folders (removed again on undo). The Git adapter makes it a delete in one repository and an add in the other, the private one committed first, said as `Make private: notes/a.md` or `Make 2 items public` in both, with the pointer following the pushed private commit as D-97 says. (4) **Nothing private in public:** links are rewritten only where their target stays in the note's root (enforcing D-95's "links that climb out of a root are left alone" for the desktop's absolute paths), so a public note never names a private one; and nothing under `private/` is ever committed to the notes repository: where `private/` isn't its own repository, private notes stay uncommitted (WP-3.3 would have committed them to the notes repository). (5) **New notes:** with the Files panel closed, a new note goes next to the open note only when that one is private, otherwise into the Private root, even from a public note (SHR-01); a folder chosen in Files is still used. (6) **Import notes (FRE-10):** on the desktop, ⋯ → *Notes* → *Import notes…* uses the system's open dialog, and only the main process reads the file (refused unread over 64 MB, then `parseBundle`); a dialog asks where the notes go, Private chosen. Paths are made safe for every file system (`safeWorkspacePath`: no dot segments, reserved Windows names suffixed), names are compared ignoring case, nothing is replaced (`wx`), identical notes are skipped, all or nothing, and one commit (`Import 2 notes`). The first run (WP-3.2) runs the same `notes.import`. (7) **Synced settings (PRV-03):** the synced settings are the appearance, the light and dark themes, and the content font and its size (`settings.schema.json`'s `appearance` and `reading`); the last note, focus mode, the interface scale, and the web welcome stay per device in `state.json`. Syncing is opted into, as THM-05 says: *Sync appearance* in the ⋯ menu's Theme group writes `private/.focus-notes/settings.json` with the current look, and turning it off keeps that look on the device and deletes the file. While the file exists, it's read over the device's state at start and the synced settings are written there (the main process writes only what the schema allows), committed with the next save in the private repository. It's offered only where the workspace has `private/`; D-46's fallback to the notes repository isn't built, so settings stay on the device instead. Changes pulled from another device apply at the next start. Custom themes are WP-8.2's. | Proposed | [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md#changing-visibility), [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#whats-built), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#moving-between-public-and-private), [Theming](../experience/theming.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md) |
| D-102 | **Conflicts and Recently deleted as built (WP-3.5, proposed).** (1) **Finding conflicts:** the main process lists, private repository first, the paths both sides changed since they parted (`git merge-base`, `git diff --raw` each way) to different results, and files with uncommitted changes the other side changed too; this computer's version is the file as it is, the other comes from the remote-tracking branch through `git cat-file --batch`, with its time from that branch's log. No note name is a process argument (SEC-03). (2) **Settling:** the app's unsaved paths and the conflicting files are committed first; the result is built in a temporary index on top of the other version (every change only this computer made, each conflicting path as chosen, the copies) and committed with `git commit-tree` (signed when `commit.gpgsign` is on; like a rebase, no commit hooks), then the branch moves there in one `git reset --keep`, so the repository is never half-resolved and history stays a line on top of the other version, with no merge commits. *Take the other version* keeps yours as "⟨title⟩ (your version, ⟨date⟩ ⟨time⟩)", added in that commit and deleted in the next, so it's in Recently deleted; *Keep both* keeps "⟨title⟩ (conflict copy)" next to the note; a copy's H1 is its title and its file name follows it. (3) **Decide later** waits per repository, not per note: its commits stay on this computer and its push waits until settled, while other notes keep saving and committing (the spec's "only that note stops syncing" would need history that isn't a line). (4) **The resolver** is composed in the canvas view from an `xl` Dialog (`layout.dialogXL`) and a new `VersionPane` primitive per version (labelled region, plain-text blocks, changed ones highlighted with `color.findMatch`, marked, and said as "Changed:"), not the planned Split and DiffView compounds and without a merged preview; the choices sit above the panes; several notes show one after another and are settled together. It opens from the toast's [Resolve] (a conflict's toast and <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>S</kbd>'s offer it instead of [Retry]) or when a note in conflict is opened; the toolbar's status row is later. (5) **Changed outside with unsaved edits** (D-92's overwrite risk): saves to that note wait, so the file is never saved over; a toast with [Resolve], the trigger's dot ("Changed in two places"), and saving, opening another note, or closing open the resolver. Every choice keeps the version not kept at the note as a copy, made and deleted at once (Recently deleted with Git, the system trash without) or, for *Keep both*, kept; `workspace.apply` gained `create`, which never writes over a file. (6) **Recently deleted** is on the desktop with Git only (the browser's notes marked deleted come later): `git log --diff-filter=D -M --no-merges --since=30 days` in both repositories, notes under `notes/` only, newest per path, leaving out notes back at their path and moves between the roots (the commits the app says as `Make public…`/`Make private…`). The Files sheet gained a `footer` slot holding a toggle, *Recently deleted* (its count once listed: opening Files starts no Git call), that shows the list in place of the tree, grouped by day. *Restore* puts a note where it was, or in its root if that folder is gone; *Restore to…* is Move to… limited to the note's own section, so restoring never changes who can see a note; a taken name is numbered; one commit, `Restore notes/a.md`; the note then shows in the tree, selected and focused. (7) **Undo** in the toast (DD-FB-01) for moves within a section (each item back, one batch, links updated again) and, with Git, deletes (the deleted folders made again, then their notes restored in place); not yet for renames, new folders, changes of visibility, or deletes without Git; Spotlight's `> Undo last file change` is M4. Toast actions carry arguments. (8) **Found on the way:** the file watcher read files while a rebase had written conflict markers into them, so the editor could show (and then save) a half-merged note; it now waits for a running pull or resolution. A toast that appeared after <kbd>F6</kbd> took focus; now only a request made while it shows does. (9) New bridge calls `sync:conflicts`, `sync:resolve`, `history:deleted`, `history:restore`, each checked in the main process; temporary `FOCUS_NOTES_RESOLVE_INDEX` and `FOCUS_NOTES_RESOLVE_MSG` in the Git folder; no new network destination. As built it has **Account** and **Workspace** only; the other six categories join with their work packages, and its search field with Spotlight. The view models are `vm.settings`, `vm.signOut`, and `vm.removeWorkspace` (`packages/core/src/settings-app.ts`). It's desktop only: `settings.open` (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>,</kbd>, ⋯ → Help group → *Settings…*) is offered only where the shell has an account (`bridge.firstRun`). It reopens at the category it was left on. No avatar yet. (2) **Sign out asks first, with a choice** (FRE-07's "optionally"): *Remove it too* (the default, as before: `git credential reject`, and `gh auth logout` for the CLI) or *Keep it for the terminal*, which leaves Git's credential and writes only `account.json` (no login or token) so the main process offers Git's account without using it until *Continue as @login*. Because the workspace syncs with the user's own Git (D-21), Git keeps saving to GitHub in that case, and the dialog says so; the spec's "signs Git out too" is the default, not the only choice. The account pill's *Sign out of GitHub* opens the same dialog. (3) **Sign in and Switch account reuse the first run's Sign in step**, shown alone (`firstRun` scope `account`, no step numbers), with the note saved and put away meanwhile; *Continue as @login* finishes it and <kbd>Esc</kbd> leaves it. Switching first takes the credential out of Git's helper and keeps it in the main process's memory, putting it back with `git credential approve` if no one signs in; with the GitHub CLI as the helper nothing is taken out (`gh auth login` switches accounts itself). (4) **Run setup again** forgets setup's progress (the engine's `reset` now deletes `setup.json`) and shows the first run from its Git step (scope `setup`); <kbd>Esc</kbd> there goes back to the note. Setup's folder is the open workspace's when there is one. (5) **Removing the workspace** needs both repositories to have nothing their remote lacks (status with untracked files, the submodule's own changes counted once, `git log --branches --not --remotes`, stashes, and a remote at all); the dialog offers *Sync now* until then. The main process checks again, stops the watcher and Git, deletes the folder, and the window starts again at the first run's *Your workspace* (`firstRun: "workspace"`). A folder that isn't a Focus Notes workspace (no `.focus-notes/workspace.json`) or isn't a Git repository of its own is never removed. (6) **The data inventory is code** (`packages/core/src/inventory.ts`): `DATA_FILES` (the only names the main process joins to the data folder), Chromium's folders, Electron's single-instance lock files, the recent-item keys, the browser's database, stores, and `meta` keys (the web app reads its names from it), and what removes each; a test checks it against Security and privacy's table. Signing out deletes `setup.json` and `first-run.json` and the recent note (`lastNotePath`, by the renderer, which owns `state.json`); removing the workspace also deletes `workspace.json`; both empty Chromium's caches and web storage. As built there's no search index or log file on disk to delete. (7) **Chromium's working files** move into `Chromium/` in the data folder (Electron's `sessionData`), so the inventory names one folder instead of a dozen Chromium-version-specific ones; the preload's code cache stays in `Code Cache/`. Nothing migrates the old locations, since no release has shipped. (8) Uninstalling keeps the data folder (electron-builder's default), so the inventory says "deleting the data folder", not "uninstalling". (8) **Chromium's crash database (CI, 2026-10-14):** on macOS and Linux Electron makes a `Crashpad` folder in the data folder, which the inventory didn't list. Its path (`crashDumps`) is now inside `Chromium/`, with the rest of Chromium's working files, so the inventory stays one item; nothing in it is ever sent. | Proposed | [Secondary views → Settings](../design/views/secondary-views.md#settings), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [First run](../experience/first-run.md#account-settings), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#signing-out), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html), [Views as data](../design/system/views-as-data.md), [Components](../design/system/components.md) |
| D-104 | **The Windows and Linux window as built (WP-2.1, proposed).** (1) **Where:** the geometry is pure functions in `packages/core` (`window-geometry.ts`: resize hit zones, resizing with Shift keeping proportions, snap zones and Snap Layouts, restoring under the pointer, displays and their scale, remembered bounds checked against today's displays, keyboard Move and Size); the window's state, the commands (`window.minimize`, `window.maximize`, `window.restore`, `window.toggleMaximize`, `window.close`, `window.move`, `window.size`, `window.snap.*` for the nine zones, `window.systemMenu`, and the frame's own `window.drag` and `window.key`) and `vm.window` (which reads `window` and `ui.toolbarOpen`) are `window-app.ts`; the main process applies them in `apps/desktop/src/main/window/` (`custom-window.ts`, the controller; `placement.ts`, `window.json`; `frame.ts`, the Electron side and the `window:*` channels, each checked to come from the app's page). (2) **Components:** the window's shape is AppRoot's new `frame` prop (native, rounded, square), not WindowFrame's: the page is transparent and the window is drawn inside it, inset by a 16 px margin, with `window.radius`, `shadow.1`, and a hairline border. It's the containing block for fixed layers and, through React Aria's portal provider, the container for menus, tooltips, and dialogs, so all of them are clipped to its corners (`react-aria`, already installed under react-aria-components, became a direct dependency of `packages/ui`); Positioner and TableControls move the editor's viewport geometry into it. **WindowFrame** (host) is the behavior: moving by the band (pointer capture, one move per frame, in screen DIP), double-click to maximize, right-click and <kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>Space</kbd> for the system menu (a CommandMenu), the resize handles (in the margin, outside the clipped window), and the keys of keyboard Move and Size. The buttons and the Snap Layouts picker are one primitive, **WindowControls**, instead of the planned TitleBar and WindowControls compounds and SnapLayoutPicker, placed by the canvas view in a Layer at the new `topEnd` position. The **snap preview** is drawn by the shell: a zone lies outside the app's own window, so it's a second, contentless, click-through window whose background is `cmp.windowFrame.snapPreview` at `window.snapPreviewOpacity`. (3) **Tokens:** `window.margin` (16 px), `window.resizeEdge` (6), `window.resizeCorner` (14), `window.minWidth` (480), `window.minHeight` (360), `window.buttonWidth` and `buttonHeight` (46 × 32), `window.layoutWidth` and `layoutHeight` (56 × 36), `window.snapLayoutsDelay` (400 ms), `window.snapPreviewOpacity` (0.3), exported resolved as `windowMetrics` for the main process; `cmp.windowFrame.*`, `cmp.windowControls.*`, and `cmp.appRoot.window*`. There's no `window.shadow`: `shadow.1` fits the margin. (4) **Behavior:** a snap zone starts within 8 px of a display's outer edge (an edge shared with another display lets the window cross), and a corner within 64 px along it; thirds are offered on work areas at least 1920 px wide; dragging a maximized or snapped window restores it after 4 px; a snapped window resized by hand becomes a normal window where it is; Move and Size step 10 px, or 1 px with <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>; the system menu is Restore, Move, Size, Minimize, Maximize, Snap left, Snap right, and Close (<kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>F4</kbd>). Moving sets only the window's position, and after a move to a display with another scale the window's size in DIP (about the same size on screen) is put back if the system changed it. Full screen is the app's own too, because Electron doesn't track it for transparent windows: the whole display, square, with no margin. Clicks in the margin pass through: the page reports the pointer moving within `POINTER_EDGE_PX` (46 CSS px) of the window's edges, and once more as it leaves that zone (`window:pointer`, at most once a frame, carrying nothing), and from the moment the caret is ready (PRF-02) the main process checks the system's pointer against the hit zones and turns ignoring the mouse on or off. On Windows an ignoring window still forwards pointer moves to the page, so nothing polls. Linux can't forward them, so there, and only while the pointer is in the margin of an ignoring window, the main process checks it every 50 ms until it leaves the margin (back onto the window, which takes the pointer again, or out of the window, where the window takes it again too so it sees it come back) or the window hides or minimizes. (After the merge with WP-2.6: this was a check every 50 ms at all times, which PRF-04 forbids; `apps/desktop/test/idle.test.ts` now holds each pointer watch to that rule.) `window.json` in the app's data folder remembers the window on every platform, macOS's native window included. (5) **Not as planned:** a Linux desktop without a compositor isn't detected; `--opaque-window` (or `FOCUS_NOTES_WINDOW=opaque`) gives the square, opaque window. The full-screen band that hides until the pointer reaches the top is WP-2.2's. The window buttons have no tooltips, since hovering maximize opens the picker. macOS keeps its 360 × 320 minimum. The system's <kbd>Win</kbd>+arrow keys, Aero snap, real monitors with different scaling, the click-through margin, and <kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>Space</kbd> not also opening the system's menu can't be driven headless (journeys give screen positions to the frame directly), so they're checked by hand in the M2 demo. Supersedes D-72. (6) **Accessibility, after the merge with WP-1.11 (D-100):** a menu opened from the keyboard starts on its first available item (CommandMenu), so the system menu opens on Move while Restore is unavailable (WP-1.11's unavailable items stay focusable, which had made it start on Restore). Snap Layouts' zones are at least 24 × 24 px (WCAG 2.5.8): a layout is `window.layoutWidth` × `window.layoutHeight`, 84 × 56 (it was 56 × 36, with 15 px zones), and the picker wraps in a narrow window. <kbd>Tab</kbd> from the open toolbar reaches the window buttons, and <kbd>Esc</kbd> on one closes the toolbar and puts the caret back in the note (WindowControls' `dismiss`). The accessibility audit's 320 px reflow measures the window as drawn (D-100 (8)). (7) **Journeys never take the computer's focus** (the owner's request, 2026-10-14): the desktop journeys, the window spike, and `pnpm bench` start the app with `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E=1`, a test seam a packaged app ignores like the others. Every window then opens with `showInactive()`, stays off the taskbar (`skipTaskbar`), and is never focused, raised (`moveTop`), or restored with activation (`apps/desktop/src/main/window/journey-window.ts`); the snap preview never takes focus anyway. Such a window is never the system's active one, so the app's "active" comes from the window's own `focus` and `blur` events, which it raises where the system would (shown or restored: active; hidden or minimized: not) and which the journeys raise to make it inactive (`setWindowActive`), as switching to another app would; the system's own focus and blur are ignored there (someone may click the window, or another app, while the suite runs), and macOS, which reports no `show` for a window shown inactive, gets it from `showInactive` itself. The pages are told they have focus through Chromium's focus emulation (`Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled`, every window the journey sees); Playwright's keys and pointer go through the DevTools protocol and need no system focus. The checks that ran only when a CI display happened to make the window active (window controls hidden at rest, the traffic lights hidden after the toolbar closes) now always run, and the reveal for an inactive window is checked by making it inactive (`m2-window.spec.ts`, `m2-titlebar.spec.ts`). The web journeys run headless, locally too. A journey run leaves the foreground window where it was (checked with `GetForegroundWindow` through a whole run); the person's real pointer over a journey's window can still move it, as before. (8) **A CI runner's small display (2026-10-14):** a window bigger than its display is fitted to it when restored from maximized, which is right, but DSK-03's journey grew the window by 180 × 120 px from a window that already filled the runner's 1024 × 768 display, so the restore didn't put it back where it was. The journey first makes a window that leaves no room smaller, by its own resize handle; the restore is still checked to the pixel. `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_WINDOW=⟨width⟩x⟨height⟩` opens a journey's window at a given size to reproduce such a display. (9) **What CI's displays showed (2026-10-14):** Chromium on Linux (X11) keeps a window asked to cover its whole screen exactly 1 px narrower and shorter (a bigger or smaller one is as asked; measured with plain, frameless, and transparent windows), and a CI display has no panels, so a maximized window's work area is its whole screen there: the journeys expect that pixel on Linux only when the bounds asked for are a whole screen (`placed`). A journey's window ignores the computer's own pointer (`setIgnoreMouseEvents`, always), whose rest position over the window had hovered menus and moved the pointer's focus; the journeys' pointer reaches the page through the DevTools protocol. <kbd>Esc</kbd> pressed on maximize just after <kbd>↓</kbd> opened Snap Layouts, before focus had moved into the picker (it moves on the next frame), was lost; it now closes the picker there too. | Proposed | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html#window-management-on-windows-and-linux), [Components](../design/system/components.md), [Tokens](../design/system/tokens.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory) |
| D-106 | **The title bar, full screen, and the macOS menu bar as built (WP-2.2, proposed).** (1) **The band:** the title bar band is the window's top 44 px (`layout.titleBar`, a new layout token with the value of `window.titleBarHeight`), a strip Layer draws under its content when `dragBand` is set. The trigger keeps D-78's place, 40 px below the top edge and centered across the band, so it sits just under the band rather than vertically centered in it as DSK-02 first said. `layout.topBand` (110 px) stays the trigger's band and the note's top clearance, and is no longer the drag area. Every layer's content is `no-drag`, so on macOS the trigger is never dragged. The window controls' `topEnd` position is now the 44 px band (it was 110 px tall, which put the buttons below the band). (2) **Full screen:** WindowFrame's new `fullScreen` prop (`vm.window.fullScreen`): on the band the controls stay hidden until the pointer comes within `window.revealEdge` (2 px) of the top edge, then show while it stays on the band; keyboard focus still shows them. Full screen has no checkmark, in ⋯ or the menu bar: a checked item would make the whole View section of ⋯ checkboxes. (3) **Traffic lights:** at `window.trafficLightX` × `window.trafficLightY` (16 × 14). The system's drag region gives the page no pointer events, so the page reports the pointer within `BAND_APPROACH_PX` (24 px) under the band, and leaving the page (`window:pointer`), the window reports focus, blur, showing, and full screen, and the main process (`window/mac-title-bar.ts`) checks the pointer against the band at each and calls `setWindowButtonVisibility`; the renderer also reports the toolbar being open (`window:reveal`). Only while the pointer is on the band or in that strip, in the active window, is it checked every 100 ms, until it leaves: a quick move onto the band can skip the strip, and leaving through the band's top the page sees nothing. (After the merge with WP-2.6: this was a check every 100 ms at all times, which PRF-04 forbids.) They show while the window is inactive and in full screen, and can't take keyboard focus. (4) **The menu bar:** core's `menu-bar.ts` lays it out (`MENU_BAR`: the app menu, File, Edit, Format, View, Go, Window, Help) and names every command that isn't in it, with a reason (`NOT_IN_MENU_BAR`: values and events, keys of one focused control, a view's own buttons, the browser's commands, the app-built window's commands, and Spotlight until M4); a test fails when a command is in neither list or in both. The renderer builds the menu (words, macOS chords, checked, radio, enabled) once the first note is open and sends it on `menu:set` when it changes, at most every 100 ms; the main process (`window/menu-bar.ts`) checks it (roles from an allowlist, IDs, words, size), turns chords into accelerators, rebuilds it only when its shape changes and otherwise updates the states in place, so an open menu stays open, and sends a chosen item back on `menu:run` to run through the command runner. Undo and Redo are the system's items, named and keyed as `edit.undo` and `edit.redo`, so they work in text fields too; cut, copy, paste, Paste and Match Style, Select All, About, Services, Hide, Quit, Close Window, and the Window and Help menus are the system's. macOS can't show a key without also registering it, and the page has keys first, so the page notes every key pressed with a modifier or a function key: a menu item chosen by its key within 500 ms of one isn't run again (the page already ran it, or left it to the field with focus). Keys of one focused control aren't in the menu bar, so they never act outside it. After the merge with WP-1.11 (D-100), ⋯'s items whose feature comes later are in the menu bar too, unavailable, with their reason as the item's tooltip (the model's `reason`, checked like the words): *Share and visibility…* in File, before Make public and Make private, and *Help and docs* in Help, after Keyboard shortcuts. ⋯'s groups and the menu bar's menus have their own message keys (⋯'s *App* group is `menu.app`; the app menu's title is `menu.appName`, and Help's `menu.help`): the merge had left `menu.app` twice in the catalog, the second silently winning, so a test now fails on any repeated message key. (5) **Not as planned:** keyboard focus can't show the traffic lights, and neither does a pointer that comes onto the band straight from outside the window without passing the page, until the window is clicked or the pointer passes the page. The traffic lights with a real pointer, each key running once on a real Mac keyboard, Help's search, and the full-screen band with a real pointer at the top of a display are checked by hand in the M2 demo. | Proposed | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html#macos-menu-bar), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#toolbar-and-menus), [Components](../design/system/components.md), [Tokens](../design/system/tokens.md), [Foundations](../design/foundations.md) |
| D-105 | **The recovery journal, the parsed-note snapshot, the warm-up, and the resource measures as built (WP-2.6, proposed).** (1) **The journal** is `recovery-journal.jsonl` in the app's data folder (`DATA_FILES.journal` in the data inventory), one JSON record per line: `text` (a note's unsaved text, its FNV-1a hash, and the hash of the note on disk under it), `saved`, `moved`, and `gone`. The main process appends each record and flushes it to the disk (`datasync`), one writer at a time. Replay keeps the latest entry per note and skips any line that isn't a whole, valid record, so a write cut short by a crash costs only itself. Once nothing is unsaved the file is emptied rather than deleted (re-creating it mid-session can fail while Windows antivirus holds it); past 4 MB it's rewritten with only what's unsaved; a clean quit deletes it. (2) **Pace:** while typing goes on the editor hands its text over at least every 750 ms (before, only after a 150 ms pause), and the app keeps it in the journal at once and then every 750 ms (`JOURNAL_INTERVAL_MS`), more often than the spec's "every second", so a crash loses at most about a second: 0.27 s at the median and 0.64 s at worst over 40 random kills. (3) **Restoring** happens in the main process before the window reads any note: into the note when it's as it was under the text (its hash matches) or gone; otherwise beside it as "⟨name⟩ (recovered).md", so neither version is lost. A note that can't be written (a read-only workspace, DST-05) stays in the journal for the next launch. The renderer shows one information toast. Restoring is idempotent. *Close without saving*, signing out, and removing the workspace forget the journal. (4) **The browser has no journal:** the note itself is saved every 750 ms while typing goes on (an IndexedDB write is atomic), so QLT-03 holds there with nothing new stored. (5) **The snapshot** is the main process's: each note it reads or writes (text, title, and hash; 32 MB) and the Files panel's titles (4 MB), keyed by path, size, and modification time, least recently used first; a write hands its result to it and a rename moves it. The editor's parsed document isn't cached: reopening an unchanged 1,500-word note takes about 17 ms with 10,000 notes, well inside 50 ms. (6) **The warm-up** is `app.warmUp` in the app core: once the caret is ready, tasks run one at a time at idle priority, after the keys have been quiet for 500 ms, and report their progress for a view that opens early. Its one task today loads the Files tree's code; Spotlight's index (WP-4.1) adds itself, and its budget (typing during warm-up, done within 10 s) is measured then. It's off in end-to-end tests: `FOCUS_NOTES_WARMUP=0` on the desktop, `navigator.webdriver` in the browser. (7) **Measures:** `pnpm bench` generates a 10,000-note Git workspace and records `PRF-04.memoryMB`, `PRF-04.idleCpuPct` (every process over 10 s of idle, budget 1%), and `PRF-10.reopenMs` (median of 7), after scripted use rather than 10 minutes. Resident memory is measured on Windows as every process's private working set plus the largest shared working set, so Electron's code, mapped into each of its four processes, counts once; summing whole working sets counts it four times (about 400 MB even with 10 notes). macOS and Linux don't report memory yet. A test checks the code has no `setInterval` but the window's two pointer watches, each started by an event and run only while the pointer is where the page can't see it (D-104 (4), D-106 (3)), and that the fetch stays every 5 minutes. (8) **Fault injection:** `apps/desktop/e2e/fault-injection.spec.ts` kills the main process at random points while typing, saving, and committing, relaunches, and checks the note is a prefix of what was typed and at most 1 s was lost, each round continuing from the last crash. It kills the main process by its own ID: on Windows, Playwright's `app.process()` is a launcher, and killing it leaves the app running. `FOCUS_NOTES_FAULT_ROUNDS` (3 by default) and `FOCUS_NOTES_FAULT_SEED` run the Definition of done's 1,000 rounds and replay a failure. (9) **After the merge with M3, WP-2.1, and WP-2.2:** the M1 demo's byte-for-byte reopen found undone text coming back. Replace all, then undo before the replacement was saved, left the journal's text (the replacement) unmatched by the save (the original), so the entry stayed with the note on disk as its base and the next launch put the replacement back. The app always saves its latest text, so a save now supersedes every text the journal was sent before it: the main process takes `mark()` as the save arrives and writes `saved` with `clean: true` when no newer text has arrived since, which clears the entry whatever its text; text sent after the save still stays, with the saved text as its base. The recovery journeys had assumed that typing without a pause is never saved, but the editor's handover every 750 ms is saved 400 ms later, so on a busy computer the crash sometimes came after everything was saved and there was nothing to restore: they now crash as soon as the journal holds text the note doesn't (`journalAhead`), and fault injection still crashes at random moments. `pnpm bench`'s note timings (PRF-03) launched without a set-up state, so since M3's first run they waited on the Welcome step and never measured: they now start set up, as the start times do. The window's pointer checks became event-driven (D-104 (4), D-106 (3)). (10) **The pace, tied to the keys (CI on 2026-10-14):** a Windows runner lost 1.15 s in fault injection, and a renderer slowed fourfold lost 1.02 s. Two waits added up: the editor's handover, up to 0.75 s after the first unsaved key, ran on a timer that a busy renderer runs late, since it handles keys first; and the journal then waited 0.75 s from its *last write*, so a handover arriving just after one (a short pause in typing) waited almost a whole interval more, up to about 1.5 s in all. Now the editor hands its text over at most 0.5 s after it first changed, and a keystroke that finds the handover overdue hands it over itself, so typing that never pauses can't hold it back; the app keeps each handover in the journal at once, or within 0.25 s when it was just written (`JOURNAL_INTERVAL_MS` = 250), never on a timer when it's due. A key is kept within 0.75 s, and the rest of the second is room for a busy computer: 0.43 s at worst over 20 rounds with the renderer slowed fourfold (`FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_CPU_THROTTLE=4`, seed 872843), 0.52 s sixfold. Saves follow the handovers as before (400 ms after each), so while typing goes on a note is written about every 0.5 s rather than every 0.75 s; the browser saves within 0.25 s of each handover. The 1 s budget is unchanged. Fault injection crashes any relaunch a failed round leaves running, so a failure no longer stalls the worker. (11) **For the owner (2026-10-15):** the memory reading in (7) was one sample 3 s after use, which landed before or after the renderer's collection at random (275 or 531 MB on CI); D-120 proposes reading it once memory has settled, with the same metric and budget. (11) **Faster still (D-152, DEF-14):** the editor hands text over at most 0.25 s after it changed and the app keeps each handover within 0.15 s (JOURNAL_INTERVAL_MS = 150), so a key is kept within 0.4 s; the main process keeps only the latest of a note''s texts waiting behind a slow write. | Proposed | [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#reliability), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync), [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#save-and-sync-status) |
| D-107 | **The search index as built (WP-4.1, proposed).** (1) **Where:** `packages/core/src/search/`, exported as `@focus-notes/core/search`: the typed API (`types.ts`: `SearchQuery` with `scope` `titles` or `text`, `kinds`, `roots`, `within`, `phrase`, and `boost` for frecency, context, and the current folder; `SearchHit` with its `tier`, `score`, `highlights`, heading, and snippet; `IndexStatus` for "Indexing n / m"), the engine (`engine.ts`, MiniSearch 7.2, MIT, a new dependency of `packages/core`), the worker (`worker.ts`, `@focus-notes/core/search-worker`, which the shells import with Vite's `?worker&url` and build as an ES module file), its messages (`protocol.ts`, `serve.ts`), and the feed (`feed.ts`: `startSearch(app, createSearchWorker(url))`, which returns the `SearchService` Spotlight and Find in all notes use). Commands and settings are given to it by the app (`setItems`, WP-4.2). Frecency is Spotlight's to keep (WP-4.2); the index takes it as a ranking input. (2) **Two indexes:** titles (title, file name, folder, front-matter `id`; headings, without the title's own H1; folders; commands and settings with their synonyms) and full text. Paths are relative to their root. Ranking is tiers (exact title or file name, its start, the start of a word in the title, file name, `id`, or synonyms, fuzzy or folder-only matches, headings, full text), then MiniSearch's score times `1 + frecency` and `1 + context`, and 1.25 for notes in the current folder; ties go to the shorter label. Every word must match; in titles every word may be a prefix, in full text only the last. Typos: one edit for 4 to 7 characters and two from 8, as the spec says, and also **two neighbouring letters swapped as one** (MiniSearch counts Levenshtein edits, where a swap is two). Case and accents don't matter (NFD, marks removed). `"…"` is `phrase: true`. (3) **Incremental, never in the way:** the feed sends every note's title, path, and folder at once, then reads texts four at a time; the worker indexes texts in 8 ms slices between messages, so a query waits at most one slice, and only the newest text of a note waiting is indexed. A node that is the same object at a new path moved (the workspace model's own rule), so its text moves without a read. The app's saves are indexed from the store's text; the watcher's reports are read again. MiniSearch's background vacuuming breaks if the index changes meanwhile, so it's off; the worker cleans up in one go when idle and enough has been removed. (4) **Starting:** the shells call `startSearch` once `app.start()` has opened the first note; WP-2.6's warm-up can call the same function at idle time instead. (5) **In memory only, not cached in IndexedDB** as the spec planned for the web: the index is rebuilt each start or page load, so there is nothing on disk to delete when signing out, removing a workspace, or locking, and the data inventory gains no row. The web reads public notes from its site to index them. (6) **Private notes in the browser:** indexed only while the vault is unlocked; locking sends `removeWithin private:` before anything else, so no later query sees them, and a read that finishes after the lock is dropped. (7) **Trusted Types:** a worker's address is a script URL, so the Content Security Policy's `trusted-types` gains `search-worker`, a policy that passes only the worker the build made, on the page's own origin; `script-src 'self'` is unchanged and no script has a policy. The worker runs with no network code. (8) **Measures:** `SPT-07.openMs` is the index's share of opening Spotlight (the first answer from a just-built index; the panel's keypress-to-visible time is WP-4.2's), `SPT-07.titleQueryP95Ms`, `SPT-07.fullTextP95Ms`, and `SPT-07.indexBuildMs` (reported only), on a seeded 5,000-note fixture (`tools/bench/fixtures.ts`), through the worker's messages on a Node worker thread (same code and engine as the Web Worker). (9) **After the merge with main (2026-10-14):** with the index, `PRF-04.memoryMB` (D-105 (7)) on CI's Windows runner was 534 MB against 525 MB (350 MB and CI's margin), up from 293 MB: it is measured while the index is still being built, and reading 10,000 notes one call each through `note:read` filled the parsed-note snapshot and grew both processes. The index now reads its first pass 32 notes per call, one call at a time, through `readNotes` (`notes:read` in the desktop's preload: at most 64 paths, Markdown files only, from the app's page only, read from disk past the snapshot, which is for notes people open); a single note (a change the watcher saw, often the app's own save, already in the snapshot) still goes through `readNote`, and the browser reads one note per call. A note's row is put again only when its text changes what it shows. Locally the measure went from 512 MB to 397 MB; once the index is built and the heaps have shrunk (about 30 s after start) everything takes about 285 MB. | Proposed | [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#performance-budgets) |
| D-108 | **The theme manager and the contrast guard as built (WP-4.5, proposed).** (1) **Where:** the guard is `packages/core/src/contrast.ts` (the pairs, WCAG 2.2 ratios with translucent colors composited over the canvas, `fixColor`, and `checkBeforeSave`) with OKLCH in `color.ts`; the token build resolves every built-in theme's colors into `themeValues` for it at run time, and a unit test fails if any built-in theme fails a pair. The manager is `theme-manager-app.ts` (`ui.theme`, `vm.themeManager`) and an overlay view, `theme.view.json`, hosted beside the canvas on the desktop and in the browser; overlay views may now hold side sheets as well as dialogs. (2) **The pairs as built:** body and muted text on the canvas and every surface, placeholder text, links, danger text, code, and text on the accent at 4.5:1 (7:1 in High contrast); tooltips, which are inverted, their text and their shortcut at `opacity.muted` (added: editing *Text* changes them); the selected text on the selection and on both find highlights; and 3:1 (4.5:1) for the accent, the focus ring, and the trigger dot on its circle. **Control borders and the frosted circle's edge aren't checked:** the built-in themes draw them as hairlines at 1.2 to 1.9:1, controls are told apart by fill, label, or icon, and the High contrast themes draw them at 7:1 or more; raising every border to 3:1 would restyle every view, so it's the owner's call. The guard found the dark themes' current find highlight at 2.5:1 under the text: the dark themes now use amber washes at 20% for matches and 35% for the current one (`ref.color.amber.a20`). (3) **The one-click fix** searches OKLCH lightness in steps of 0.001, nearer first and darker first at the same distance, reducing chroma only to stay in sRGB, for a color that makes every pair its tokens are in pass; *Fix* is offered only where one exists. (4) **The gallery** is a list box whose selection follows keyboard focus (not a grid): `aria-selected` is the theme showing, a tile's name is what it shows ("Paper Light ✓") with its kind and badge as its description, the arrows move by the columns as laid out, and <kbd>Esc</kbd> while previewing goes back to the chosen theme and its tile without closing the sheet. Choosing runs `theme.<kind>.<id>`, then `appearance.<kind>` when the other kind is showing, so choices are remembered and synced as the ⋯ menu's are. Tiles are sampled in their own theme's colors by `[data-theme-sample]` blocks the token build writes for component tokens named `cmp.*.sample*`. Not built yet: search, filter chips, the Yours and Classic schemes groups, the tile's ⋯ menu, the empty note's sample paragraph, right-click or long-press on the toolbar's Theme button, and the first run's step. ⋯ → *Theme…* is the Theme group's first item; MenuButton now splits a group's plain commands from its checked choices so it isn't announced as a checkbox. (5) **Reading settings:** line spacing 1.3 to 2.0 in steps of 0.1 (default 1.5, the token and D-78, not the spec's 1.6), reading width 50 to 100 characters in fives, paragraph spacing Compact 1em, Comfortable 1.5em, or Airy 2em (token modes), and focus dim 15 to 60% in fives, unset meaning each theme's own `focus.dimOpacity` (so High contrast keeps 55%). AppRoot overrides `font.lineHeight`, `layout.measure`, and `focus.dimOpacity` on the root at run time, as it does the text size. They're `lineHeight`, `measure`, `paragraphSpacing`, and `focusDim` in `state.json`, and in `settings.json`'s `reading` while *Sync appearance* is on (`paragraphSpacing` joins its schema); a key sent with no value now removes it from either file, so *Reset reading settings* clears the dim everywhere. The font is a menu of the existing Font commands, not each in its own font and with no custom stack yet; focus dim doesn't preview with focus mode on. (6) **Edit colors** edits the five key colors of the theme showing, each setting the tokens beside it exactly (Background: canvas; Surface: surface and raised surface; Text: text, selected text, and code; Muted text; Accent: accent, link, and focus ring) until generated themes bring derivation. The copy, "⟨Theme⟩ (custom)", is shown through AppRoot's `themeColors` (color tokens and `#rrggbb` values only, UNSUP-THEME-01) and kept in memory while the app runs and its base fills a slot; it isn't saved. Saving custom themes, *Low contrast* tiles, and the "Save anyway?" confirmation come with WP-8.2, which saves through `saveWarning` ("This theme has 2 hard-to-read pairs. Save anyway?"). Colors are typed as hex in **ColorField**, committed on <kbd>Enter</kbd> or on leaving the field; the OKLCH picker, eyedropper, *All colors*, the name field, <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Z</kbd>, and the trigger dot's limits come with WP-8.2. (7) **Components:** three primitives, **ThemeGallery**, **Slider**, and **ColorField** (`cmp.themeGallery`, `cmp.slider`, `cmp.colorField`); list kinds `themeTiles` and `colorValues`; AppRoot's `lineHeight`, `measure`, `paragraphSpacing`, `focusDim`, and `themeColors`; number props can be bound; `layout.themeTile` (158 px) and `layout.themeSwatch` (56 px); SegmentedControl wraps under its label where it doesn't fit. (8) **Settings → Appearance** (D-103) is the summary with *Open theme manager*, *Interface size* 90 to 150%, and *Sync appearance* where there's a private repository; *Transparency* isn't built. | Proposed | [Theme manager](../design/views/theme-manager.html#as-built), [Theming](../experience/theming.md), [Secondary views](../design/views/secondary-views.md), [Components](../design/system/components.md), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#synced-settings), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory) |
| D-109 | **Spotlight as built (WP-4.2, proposed).** (1) **Where:** `packages/core/src/spotlight.ts` (modes and prefixes, filters, matching short lists, merging the index's answers into one list, quick actions, frecency, and which commands it lists) and `spotlight-app.ts` (the `ui.spotlight` state, its commands, and `vm.spotlight`); the shells give it the search service with `app.useSearch(startSearch(…))`. It isn't a CommandPalette compound yet: it's composed in the canvas view, like Move to… (D-94), from a frosted `DialogPanel` (new: size `m`, 640 px; `placement` top, about 20% down; `material` frosted; a `footer` slot), a `SearchField` (new: a `chip` for the mode, and the `spotlightField` key scope), and a `ResultList` (labeled groups, matches marked in bold, and a `state` at the end of the row; one API with Find in all notes' since the two merged: header items head the groups, `highlights` and `descriptionHighlights` mark matches, D-110 (6)). The panel is built when it opens rather than kept hidden; it still opens in under 10 ms on the 5,000-note fixture. (2) **Opening:** `spotlight.open` (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd>, All), `spotlight.quickOpen` (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd>, Notes), `spotlight.commands` (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd>, until now an alternate key of `spotlight.open`), and `spotlight.headings` (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>O</kbd>), the same in the browser; a mode's key switches mode and keeps the query, and the same mode's key again closes it. Quick open replaces the browser's old list (D-94 (2)): `picker.open` keeps only <em>Open…</em> in a shell with no workspace. Its empty list in the browser comes from the shell's own list of notes (the instance's private ones while unlocked), as the old list did, and importing notes in the browser now refreshes the workspace tree so Files and the index have them. React Aria's modals don't nest across view documents, so opening Spotlight first closes Settings, the cheat sheet, the old list, About, Unlock (when idle), Move to…, and Files' confirmation, as <kbd>Esc</kbd> would; the conflict resolver isn't dismissed. Closing gives focus back where it was (the Files panel keeps its own), or to the note when that's gone. (3) **KEY-01:** Spotlight lists every command but setters (`*.setX`), events from components, steps inside a dialog, card, or sheet (first run, Unlock, Move to…, the link editor, the old list, and, since they merged, Find in all notes' `findAll.*` and the theme manager's previews, choices, colors, close, and revert), and keys that belong to a field or panel (a `handledBy` scope other than `editor` and `table`); `isInternalCommand` names them, and a test checks that every other registered command is listed with its shortcut. The index keys commands and settings as `command:<id>` and `setting:<id>`. Theme, font, and appearance choices say what they choose ("Dark theme: Dusk"). (4) **Settings** are Spotlight's own list over existing commands, each with its value: Appearance, Light theme, Dark theme, Note font, Text size, Interface size, Focus mode, and Sync appearance where it's offered. <kbd>Enter</kbd> steps one and *Reset to default* goes back; toggles and settings leave Spotlight open. *Open in Settings* isn't offered yet (Settings → Appearance arrived with the theme manager, D-108). (5) **Quick actions** that change files reveal the result in Files (`files.reveal` now takes a path) and run Files' own commands, so its confirmations, batches, links, and commits apply unchanged (D-94, D-99). *Copy Markdown link* is relative to the open note and refused from a public note to a private one; *Copy public link* waits for publishing (M5) and *Open in new window* for WP-8.5, each saying so. An instance's read-only note in a browser has Rename, Move to…, Make private, and Delete unavailable ("Read-only here") and offers *Copy to my notes*. Copy actions close Spotlight. Not built: Copy as Markdown, Duplicate, Share with people…, Show in Finder or Explorer, New folder here, the note's outline, Change shortcut…, and each action's own shortcut. (6) **Find in all notes** is WP-4.3's `find.allNotes` command (D-110): with no match, *Find "…" in all notes* closes Spotlight and runs it with `{ query }`, so its side sheet opens searching for the query. It's unavailable only while that command is, with the command's own reason. The sheet's own steps (`findAll.*`) aren't listed. (7) **Keys:** registry shortcuts gain `whenEnabled` (the key reaches the field while its command is unavailable: <kbd>Backspace</kbd> with text, <kbd>←</kbd> and <kbd>Esc</kbd> with no action list) and `atFieldEnd` (<kbd>→</kbd> shows the actions only with the caret at the end of the text; the key handler reports `caretAtEnd`). <kbd>⌃P</kbd> / <kbd>⌃N</kbd> on macOS aren't bound, because the keymap keeps Control with a letter for text there; <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>1</kbd> to <kbd>9</kbd>, <kbd>⇧⌘C</kbd>, and <kbd>⌥↓</kbd> / <kbd>⌥↑</kbd> wait for the Pro layer (WP-4.4). (8) **Frecency** (a week's half-life) is kept in memory for the session, never written or synced, so the data inventory is unchanged; the empty field's recent notes are the ones opened since the app started. Storing it per device, and context boosts for commands that apply to the selection, are later. (9) **Measures:** `SPT-07.panelOpenMs` in `pnpm bench`: the key's own time to the first frame that shows the panel with its results (the app's `fn:spotlight.open` measure), in the app on the 5,000-note fixture, budget 50 ms; the M4 journey checks the same off CI. **After the merge with main (2026-10-14):** the empty field in All lists, after recent notes and suggestions, every command and setting it can run, as `>` does (the action center, D-111); on the macOS menu bar (D-106) Search, Search commands and settings, and Go to heading are in Go, Quick open is File's in place of the old note list (`picker.open`), Find in all notes and its next and previous result are in Edit → Find, and Theme… is in View. An open Spotlight asks again (at most every 250 ms) as the index reads more, so a note listed after the query was answered appears, and a row the person moved to stays active. Focus is never lost to the merge's timing: the note takes the caret back after the toolbar or a panel closes only if the toolbar hasn't opened again meanwhile (by its key, which the page handles before that timer), and a tab panel that had nothing to focus hands focus to the first control that appears in it (Settings' Account panel while it checks the account). The desktop journeys start once the search worker is attached (at most 5 s): while Chromium attaches it, the main process's inspector can drop a call's answer ("Resulting promise was garbage collected"), and `quit()` checks the app closes if it does. | Proposed | [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html#whats-built), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#spotlight), [Components](../design/system/components.md#compound-components), [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#whats-built), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#performance-budgets) |
| D-110 | **Find in all notes as built (WP-4.3, proposed).** (1) **A side sheet of its own, not a Spotlight mode.** <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F</kbd> (`find.allNotes`, Power), ⋯ → Edit → *Find in all notes* (the menu item D-100 left for later), and Spotlight's *Find "…" in all notes*, which passes its `query`, open a frosted `Sheet` on the right (`side` end, a new `find` width, `layout.sheetFind`, 380 px), composed in the canvas view document from catalog parts like the find bar (D-76) and Files (D-94): a heading, a close button, a SearchField driving a ResultList (the combobox pattern), a live summary, and the three toggles. Grouped lines need more height than Spotlight's panel gives, and Spotlight (WP-4.2) was built alongside. (2) **One matcher, one path.** The find bar's matching moves to `@focus-notes/markdown/match` (`foldedPattern`, `matcherOf`, `matchesIn`, `lineKey`), shared by the editor and the search worker, so both find the same matches in the same order: case and accents ignored by default, whole word as prosemirror-search defines it (no letter on both sides), a regular expression one block at a time, empty matches never counted. The worker scans the visible text it already holds for every note (one line per block, D-107's extraction), on the desktop and the web alike: no ripgrep on the desktop and no separate scan of cached notes on the web, as the spec planned. Notes not read yet can't be searched; the summary says how many, and an open search is asked again as the index changes. The extraction now drops a backslash that ends a line (a line break), as the editor does. (3) **Results** (`SearchService.find`, a `find` message to the worker): notes with a match, private first, then public, then the browser's own, each by path; every match counted, the first 1,000 listed (at most 5,000); each with its place in the note's count, its line, the line's key, how many earlier lines read the same, its place on the line, and the line around it (32 characters before it, from a word's start, about 120 long). One row per match, so a line with two matches shows twice, each marked once. (4) **Opening a match:** the sheet closes, the note opens, and once its editor is set up (`app.ready`, after paint) the find bar opens with the query and toggles and its field focused, and a new `find.select` editor action selects the match: on the line with that key (the nth that reads so, a code block's and a line break's lines each counting), its occurrence there; or, where no line reads so, by its place in the count. So a match opens right even after a block the editor shows as raw Markdown (counted in the sheet, not by the find bar); a table's row is one line in the sheet and a cell per block in the editor, so there it goes by the count, which can be off after raw blocks. An image's alt text is found in the sheet but isn't text in the editor. (5) **Keys:** in the field (`findAllField`) <kbd>↓</kbd> <kbd>↑</kbd> move through the lines, never onto a note's heading, and <kbd>Enter</kbd> opens one; <kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>C</kbd> / <kbd>W</kbd> / <kbd>R</kbd> (<kbd>⌥⌘</kbd> on a Mac) toggle anywhere in the sheet (`findAll`); <kbd>F4</kbd> / <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F4</kbd> (`find.nextResult`, `find.prevResult`, Pro) open the next or previous result across notes, wrapping, the first one opening the highlighted result; <kbd>Esc</kbd> clears the field, then closes the sheet, and the caret goes back to the note. (6) **Components:** ResultList, unified with Spotlight's additions (D-109) when the two merged into one API: an item with `header` heads a group of the items after it (a `role="group"` named by its label and description; here a note, with its path, visibility, and count; in Spotlight Top hit, Notes, Commands…), `highlights` and `descriptionHighlights` mark where the query matched, in bold on the find highlight (`cmp.resultList.match`, `matchText`, `matchWeight`, `matchRadius`), a row's `state` sits at its end, and `height` is content or fill; group tokens `groupGap`, `header`, `headerWeight`, `headerPaddingBlock`; Sheet gains the `find` width and the `findAll` key scope; SearchField the `findAllField` scope. (7) **Privacy:** results live in memory only; locking the vault in the browser drops every result at once, before the index is asked again, so nothing private stays on screen or within F4's reach; no new stored data and no network. (8) **Measure:** `FND-04.findAllP95Ms`, budget 300 ms, on the 5,000-note fixture through the worker's messages (about 50 ms on the development machine). (9) **Not built:** collapsing a note's group, filling the field from the selection, `type:` filters, showing more than 1,000 lines (VirtualList), and replacing across notes (FND-05, WP-8.4). | Proposed | [Find and replace](../experience/find-and-replace.md#find-in-all-notes), [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#find-and-replace), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html), [Components](../design/system/components.md), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#performance-budgets) |
| D-111 | **Visual quality and the action center (owner, 2026-10-14).** After using the built app, the owner found UI and visual bugs and asked for deliberate, critical visual care: tooltips anchored to their control (they appeared in the top right corner); the Files sheet vertically centered with equal inset at the top, side, and bottom; uniform padding all around in Files and the shortcuts cheat sheet (the top was too big); content fading at the top and bottom of scrolling areas over 50 px; checkboxes aligned with their label's text; the find and replace bar's alignment, grouping, and spacing improved; scrims and other overlays kept inside the app window; segmented controls and tabs kept inside their dialog; dialogs never edge to edge as they grow; and gaps between Files items. These become the [visual quality checklist](../design/foundations.md#visual-quality), a new row in the [work package definition of done](definition-of-done.md#work-package-definition-of-done), and part of the release bar. The owner also asked for an **action center on <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd>** where every action can be searched, chosen with the arrow keys, and run: that's Spotlight (D-16), whose empty field now lists every action after recent notes (SPT-04). **What was wrong, and the fixes (builder, 2026-10-14):** (1) *Tooltips in the top-left corner:* React Aria's tooltip measured its position against the page, but on Windows and Linux the drawn window sits inside a 16 px shadow margin and is the containing block for fixed content, and a tooltip whose control had gone (the toolbar closing) stayed open with nothing to anchor to; tooltips now place themselves next to their control in the window's box, follow it every frame while open, and close when it's hidden. (2) *Files not vertically centered, padding top too big:* the sheet sat at the 110 px top band with a 12 px inset elsewhere, and titles added their line height's leading to the padding; sheets now take one inset for top, side, and bottom (16 px, or more to clear the window's controls; narrower to clear the trigger in a small window), and interface headings trim their box to their letters. (3) *No fade at scroll edges:* added `useFadingEdges` and `fadingEdges` (50 px, only where there's more, none in forced colors) to the note, dialog and sheet bodies, result lists, and menus; the Files tree now scrolls with the sheet's body. (4) *Checkboxes off their text:* a task item's text took the list's block gap above it, 11 px below the checkbox; it no longer does. (5) *Find bar:* regrouped (search field, count, previous and next; the three options; close), 8 px inside a group and 16 px between them, every control 32 px tall, one × instead of three, fields that shrink first so it fits at 480 px. (6) *Scrims past the window:* Settings and the theme manager are views hosted beside the canvas, rendered outside the drawn window; they now render inside it (`InWindow`). Menus reached past the window's bottom because React Aria measured the page; their edge padding now includes the margin, and they scroll. (7) *Tabs and segmented controls past the dialog:* vertical tabs lay out across when there's no room for the list and the panel side by side, tab lists wrap, and segmented controls and buttons shrink with ellipses rather than overflow. (8) *Dialogs edge to edge:* the dialog's maximum height was 85% of the viewport rather than of the room the scrim leaves; it now never exceeds that room, and Spotlight's 20% top is measured in the window. (9) *No gaps between Files items:* `space.xxs` (2 px) between rows in the tree, menus, and result lists. Found on the way: the slash menu and other companions ran off the window's bottom in a short window (they now take the side with more room and scroll); menus' shortcuts were in the monospace font; a submenu's items had an empty check column; the link editor's field was 40 px beside 32 px buttons; the formatting toolbar's tooltip covered the selected text; Recently deleted's group names were larger than its titles. Every rule is checked by journeys on every surface in light and dark, at the default size and 480 × 360 (`vq-surfaces`, `vq-flows`, and the web's `visual-quality`). **Second pass (owner, 2026-10-14; as built):** fade and blur only on the note, never on interface surfaces, as a layered progressive blur beside its scroller (8 backdrop blurs, 40 px at the window's edge and halving inward, over a 50 px band, with a fade in the canvas's color); selected and current items shown by background, never a bar or border; focus rings drawn inside list items so no container cuts them, checked at both ends of every list; and **dialog height**: a modal dialog is at least 400 px tall (less only where the window with its 24 px inset has no room), at most that room (the earlier 85% cap went, as it stopped dialogs at 306 px where a 480 × 360 window allows 312), and while open it can grow but never shrinks. **Exception:** confirmations (Delete, Sign out, Remove workspace, Import) are short alerts and fit their content (`height="content"`), still inside the inset. | Decided (owner); Spotlight staying open after a toggle amended by D-283 | [Foundations → Visual quality](../design/foundations.md#visual-quality), [Definition of done](definition-of-done.md), [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html) |
| D-113 | **Throughput: validated progress per hour (owner, 2026-10-14).** The owner stopped all work after builders spent hours on long batches, big merges, full-suite reruns and other platforms before they could see anything. The loop now optimizes validated progress toward the definition of done per hour: serve the current constraint (now, the owner's feedback on the Windows app); run each check at the moment that matches its cost and risk (seconds to minutes per change, the full Windows suite in the background on every merge, everything at the stage gate and release); 45-minute time-boxed builder tasks that merge what works; branches no older than about an hour; at most two builders, one while the owner tests; owner feedback queued as the next small task, never widening a running one; a live progress log; and an independent throughput reviewer every 30 minutes that challenges the approach, with each finding acted on or answered. **Two working modes** (owner, 2026-10-14): *attended*, while the owner is monitoring, testing, and giving input, for rapid iteration on visual and functional feedback with fixes they can try within minutes; and *unattended*, while they're away for hours or the night, for deeper, systematic work toward the definition of done, ending in a handoff summary. | Decided (owner) | [The agentic loop → Throughput](agentic-loop.html#throughput), [Definition of done → When checks run](definition-of-done.md#when-checks-run) |
| D-114 | **Public pages as built (WP-5.1, proposed).** (1) **URLs:** a public note `notes/<path>.md` is published at `/n/<path>/` (each segment URL-encoded, the extension dropped, a trailing slash, served from `n/<path>/index.html`), so the URL is the note's path and stays stable while the note isn't moved; `publicNoteUrl` in the app core makes it for *Copy public link*. The redirect page for moved notes (D-08) isn't built yet. (2) **Rendering:** the publisher renders each page once in Node from the Markdown through the bridge, the reader view document (`packages/views/views/reader.view.json`, IA-VIEW-PUBLIC) and the UI runtime's new static renderer (`renderStaticView`), with only the page's components (`ReaderPage`, `NoteContent`, `Stack`, `Text`).
