The renderer is built by Vite (`apps/web/vite.reader.config.ts`) into one self-contained Node module, `apps/web/dist-reader/render.mjs`, with the pages' stylesheet and font files; the publisher takes it with `--reader` (and the site's address with `--site-url`, from `configure-pages`, now run before publishing, for canonical links) and copies its assets into the site's `assets/`. The release bundle carries the same folder for template instances (WP-5.5). (3) **Block components:** `NoteContent` is the first block component (`packages/ui/src/blocks/`): paragraphs, headings, lists and task lists, quotes, code, tables, rules, inline marks, and unknown syntax shown verbatim as text, on `cmp.noteContent` tokens that alias the same semantic tokens as the editor surface. The editor still draws its own node views with the same rules; moving its node views onto the block components is later work (DD-CMP-04 holds by shared tokens until then). (4) **Light and dark without a script:** a page's root says `data-theme="auto"`; the token build adds Ink's colors under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` for it. Pages have no script at all (CSP `default-src 'none'; style-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:`), so the small light/dark toggle the design shows isn't built yet. (5) **Links:** relative links to other public notes go to their pages; links to private or missing notes, outside the notes folder, or with unsafe schemes are shown as plain text, so a page never points at or reveals a private note. (6) **Title:** the note's first H1 when it's the first block, else its file name; "Updated ⟨date⟩" is the last commit's date. (7) **`noindex`:** front matter `noindex: true` adds `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` (PRV-04). (8) **Fonts (PRF-12):** a page preloads the content font's latin `woff2` faces its title and first ~1,500 characters use: regular and bold always, italic only when that text has italic. (9) **Lab metrics:** the journey (`apps/web/e2e/public-pages.spec.ts`) measures LCP, CLS, and the longest event duration after a click (as INP) with the browser's performance entries, on a 4× CPU slowdown and a slow 4G profile, in Chromium only. Images still render as verbatim Markdown (EXT-03 is later), so "images request the width they're shown at" has nothing to apply to yet. | Proposed (builder) | [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md), [Components](../design/system/components.md), [Secondary views](../design/views/secondary-views.md#public-note-page) |
| D-115 | **The Power layer as built (WP-4.4, proposed).** (1) **Built:** whole-block keys in `packages/editor/src/blocks.ts` (move up and down with <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>↑</kbd>/<kbd>↓</kbd>, and <kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>↑</kbd>/<kbd>↓</kbd> on Windows and Linux; duplicate with <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>D</kbd>, the caret going into the copy; delete with <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>K</kbd>, the last item of a list taking its list). A block is the list item with the caret, else the note's top-level block; in a table the table's row keys run first. Back and forward between notes (`note.back`, `note.forward`: <kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>←</kbd>/<kbd>→</kbd>, <kbd>⌘[</kbd>/<kbd>⌘]</kbd>) keep a browser-like trail of the last 50 notes opened; a note that's gone leaves the trail. <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>U</kbd> says once that Markdown has no underline (D-12). Each is a registered command, so Spotlight, the cheat sheet, and the macOS menu bar list it. (2) **Planned, not bound** (the spec's planned pattern): *New window* and *Reopen closed note* wait for multiple windows (WP-8.5), and so does *Close note / window* (<kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>F4</kbd> and the macOS menu bar's Close close the window until then); the Files panel's cut and paste waits for its own package (Move to… covers it). *Minimize*, *Hide app*, *Cycle windows*, and *Quit* are the OS's; the window menu is the desktop shell's <kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>Space</kbd> (D-104). The Spotlight Pro keys WP-4.2 left (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>1</kbd> to <kbd>9</kbd>, <kbd>⇧⌘C</kbd>, <kbd>⌥↑</kbd>/<kbd>⌥↓</kbd>) are Pro, so they move to the Pro layer (WP-8.1, KEY-04). (3) **Checked:** `packages/core/test/keymap-layers.test.ts` reads every Basic, Writer, and Power row of [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md) and fails on a row that is neither bound in its layer nor listed as planned or the system's. |
| D-116 | **Hardening and schema versions as built (SEC-01, DSY-10, proposed).** (1) **Fuses:** electron-builder's `electronFuses` turns `RunAsNode`, `EnableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable` and `EnableNodeCliInspectArguments` off and `EnableCookieEncryption`, `EnableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation` and `OnlyLoadAppFromAsar` on, for every platform's package. Playwright drives Electron through the Node inspector, so a journey against the packaged app (`FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_EXE`) runs a copy beside it, `focus-notes-e2e.exe`, with only that one fuse turned back on; the shipped executable is never changed, and `packaged.spec.ts` reads its fuses. (2) **Checklist in one place:** the renderer's web preferences, the request allow-list and the web-link check live in `apps/desktop/src/main/hardening.ts` so `hardening.test.ts` checks them. (3) **UI schema version:** the version stays in each view document's `$schema` (`view.v<major>.json`), as Views as data says, rather than in a second field; `packages/ui-runtime/src/migrate.ts` holds the current major and the migrations keyed by the major they start from, and `ui:check` migrates before validating, refuses a newer major with a clear error, and fails when a major has no migration. |
| D-117 | **Share sealing as built (WP-5.2, proposed).** (1) **Where:** `packages/seal/src/grants.ts` (which grants are active, scopes, scoped paths, the name lookup, and link keys, on Web Crypto so the browser uses the same code), the publisher in `publish.ts`, and `SiteReader.openLink` and `openPasscode` in `reader.ts`. (2) **Scopes:** `scope.notes` entries match a note's front-matter `id` or its path; `scope.folders` are path prefixes (a trailing `/` is added), so notes added later are included. `scope.all` counts only on the owner's grant: a share grant with it opens just what its `notes` and `folders` name, so no audience can be given every private note by mistake. Two grants with the same `id` fail the deploy. (3) **Scoped paths:** a shared folder keeps its own name and what's inside it, a shared note is just its file name, the folders above stay unseen, and two that would read the same get " (2)" before the extension; the vault keeps whole paths. (4) **Sealing once:** each note any active grant opens is sealed once per build with its own key and listed by every manifest that opens it; notes no grant opens aren't sealed, so the site doesn't even show how many there are. (5) **Link keyrings** (`_access/<build>/l/<id>.json`): `{ v, kdf: "hkdf-sha256", salt, iv, data }`, no `iterations`, with additional data `focus-notes/keyring/v1|<build>|hkdf-sha256|<salt>`; `parseKeyring` takes the derivation the caller expects, so a link keyring is never accepted where a passcode or vault keyring is, and the other way round. (6) **Passcode keyrings** (`p/<lookup>.json`): the vault keyring's format, derived each build from the stored passcode; the lookup is 32 hex characters, the name normalized as NFKC, trimmed, then upper case and lower case (ECMAScript's definitions, the same in Node and every browser). (7) **nameSalt:** copied into `focus-notes.json` whenever there's an `access.json`, passcode keyrings or not (since (11)); if `access.json` has none, the build uses a fresh one, which still works because a reader computes the lookup from the current site manifest each time. Two active audiences whose names normalize the same fail the deploy without quoting either. (8) **Generic errors:** every failure in `openLink` and `openPasscode` is `SealError("key")` with the message "key" (SHR-06); link IDs must match `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,64}$`. (9) **Leak check:** every grant's link secret, passcode, and `passcodeKey`, in force or not, of any length, fails the deploy as kind "secret". (10) **Workflows:** the project's Deploy app gets the nightly schedule, the 50-day rule, and a deploy only from the default branch (since (11), `github.event_name == 'schedule' || github.ref == format('refs/heads/{0}', github.event.repository.default_branch)`, not `refs/heads/main`); `configure-pages` moves before the private checkout in both workflows, so the only action after private plaintext reaches the runner is the artifact upload, once `private/` is deleted; `tools/test/deploy-workflows.test.mjs` pins both. **Not in this package:** the share reader page at `/s/` and its session (WP-5.4), creating grants and `nameSalt` in the desktop app (WP-5.3), and the app's 50-day reminder. **(11) After the security review (2026-10-15, no critical or high findings):** the name lookup is PBKDF2-SHA256 over the normalized name with `nameSalt`'s bytes at 50,000 iterations (`NAME_ITERATIONS`), not a single SHA-256, so each guessed name costs real work; the share reader's error is still generic, but the site's files show whether a guessed name has a passcode keyring, which the spec now says plainly. Each run's `github-pages` artifact (the build's keyrings and sealed files) is deleted by a final `after-deploy` job (`needs: deploy`, `if: always()`, `actions: write`), and the upload keeps it a day at most. The 50-day check moved into that job, after the deploy. Deploying and checking out `private/` need the default branch (`github.event.repository.default_branch`, or a scheduled run). `validateAccess` refuses a passcode that fails `passphraseStrength` (unless the grant is withdrawn; since (14), `generated` no longer exempts one; since D-134 (6), the app's rule `passcodeProblem`: 16 or more characters, or 3 or more different words, then `passphraseStrength`), grant IDs outside `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,64}$`, names empty once normalized, and a `nameSalt` outside 16 to 64 bytes. Keyrings bind their role and grant into the additional data (`|vault`, `|link|<id>`, `|passcode|<lookup>`), the link's HKDF info is `focus-notes/grant/v1|<id>`, and `openLink` checks the keyring names the grant it asked for. Each share keyring's salt is HKDF-SHA256 of the grant's salt with the info `focus-notes/salt/v1|link` or `|passcode`. `nameSalt` is published whenever there's an `access.json`. The leak check also matches each secret in Unicode NFC and NFD and as standard base64, and share audience names of 6 characters or more (kind `name`), but only in what the publisher generates for sealing (`_access/`, `_sealed/`, and `focus-notes.json`): public notes, their index (`notes/index.json`, which holds public titles), and their pages may mention the person ("Lunch with Alexander") without failing the deploy. **(12) Proposed for the owner:** put a random keyring ID in the invite (beside the passcode) instead of finding passcode keyrings by name, so the site's files can't confirm a guessed name at all; it costs an invite that must carry the ID, and readers who type only a name and passcode would need it too. **(13) Open:** publishing the public pages before `private/` is checked out, and a seal-only entry point for the publisher, so less third-party-built code runs while private plaintext is on the runner (review item L5). **(14) After the security re-review (2026-10-15):** `generated: true` no longer skips the strength check, since it's only a field in `access.json` (generated four-word passcodes score about 135 bits); keyring payloads are padded with spaces to a multiple of 256 bytes, with `expiresAt` as a full ISO string, so a keyring's size doesn't show its grant ID's length or whether it expires; the keyring additional data is labelled `focus-notes/keyring/v2` since the role joined it, and the reader accepts only `v2` (publisher and reader deploy together); the name check reads `_access/`, `_sealed/`, and `focus-notes.json` as JSON and skips `data`, `iv`, `salt`, and `nameSalt`, so a name turning up by chance in ciphertext doesn't fail the deploy; the leak check also matches each secret URI-encoded and JSON-escaped, and each name as `normalizeName` writes it. The run's Pages artifact lists every `_access` keyring file for the minutes before `after-deploy` deletes it, which allows offline name guessing until (12) is decided, and a failed deploy needs *Re-run all jobs*; both are documented. **(15) Still open for the owner:** (12); an environment-scoped `PRIVATE_CONTENT_KEY`, so only jobs in a protected environment can read it (review item L6); and a better passcode strength estimator than `passphraseStrength` (re-review #3, accepted for now as for SEC-08). | Proposed | [Repository and publishing → Sealing and share links](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#sealing-and-share-links) |
| D-118 | **Visual baselines: candidates and approval (DSY-09, proposed).** (1) **Capture:** `apps/desktop/e2e/baselines.spec.ts`, opt-in with `FOCUS_NOTES_CAPTURE_BASELINES=1`, visits every surface in `surfaces.ts` and `flows.ts` (the ones `vq-surfaces.spec.ts` and `vq-flows.spec.ts` check) in light and dark at the default window size, and writes `<id>.<theme>.png` with a `manifest.json` to `test-results/baselines/candidates/` (or `FOCUS_NOTES_BASELINE_DIR`); `e2e/baselines.ts` gives each surface a stable ID and its work packages. Candidates are never written into the repository, so nothing treats them as approved. (2) **Approved:** only the owner approves, by committing a candidate into `apps/desktop/e2e/baselines/win32/` in a reviewed commit (`snapshotPathTemplate` `{testDir}/baselines/{platform}/{arg}{ext}`). A surface with an approved baseline is compared with `toHaveScreenshot` (`maxDiffPixelRatio` 0.002, animations off, caret hidden). (3) **Platform:** Windows first (D-112). Approved baselines should come from the CI Windows runner, whose fonts and scaling the comparison runs on; a local capture is for review. Not yet covered: high contrast, compact, and reduced transparency (DSY-09), the minimum window size, and the web reader (WP-6.3, WP-6.4). |
| D-119 | **Gallery review fixes (D-111, proposed).** A screenshot review of the 44 baseline surfaces found five visual bugs, fixed in `packages/ui` and tokens: (1) **The trigger over scrolled text.** The note's top scroll edge first hides the trigger's place entirely, `layout.triggerClear` (80 px: the trigger's 40 px from the top, its 30 px circle, and room to spare) in the canvas's color, then blurs and fades over `layout.scrollFade` (50 px) below it, so scrolled text passes under the trigger without ever meeting the dot. Chosen over starting the scroller below the trigger band, which would cut the text with a hard edge and lose the progressive blur; the bottom edge stays 50 px. (2) **The scrollbar in the window's corners.** The note's scrollbar track stays `layout.scrollbarEnds` (the 20 px window radius) from the top and the bottom, so the corner's curve never cuts the thumb. (3) **The link card's insets.** Its address is inset like a small button's label (`Text` `inset="control"`: `cmp.text.controlInset` plus `cmp.text.controlBorder`), so its text and the last button's label sit 17 px from either edge. (4) **Sheet groups.** A side sheet's header and its body are separate groups, `cmp.sheet.groupGap` (`space.l`, 16 px) apart (Recently deleted's title sat 8 px under "Files"). (5) **The thumb behind a scrim.** Under a modal (the rest of the app inert or hidden), a scroller's thumb never shows. Checked in the visual quality journeys: text never meets the trigger, the thumb stays clear of the corners (by its pixels), a one-row bar's ink is inset equally at both ends, and a sheet's header and body are at least 16 px apart. **Amended (2026-10-15, owner asked for it):** “The fade blur effect on text content should extend all the way to the top edge of the window.” The solid 80 px band made scrolled text vanish abruptly 80 px down, and the blur never reached the edge. (1)’s top edge is now one continuous progressive effect from the window's edge down `triggerClear + scrollFade` (130 px), with no solid band: the eight blur layers span all of it, 40 px at the edge and halving inward, their slices eased out (layer k's ends at 1 − (1 − k/8)² of the edge) so the strong blurs hold over the trigger's place, and each layer's box trimmed to its slice (a backdrop filter costs its whole box; the eight boxes total about 380 px of height, as the old 8 × 50 px). The canvas color's opacity eases as `top · (1 − x^p)²` over 17 stops: `layout.scrollVeil` (92%) at the edge, `layout.triggerVeil` (85%) at 80 px, none at 130 px, level at both ends so no seam shows. D-119's intent holds: under the dot (40 to 70 px down) the canvas color is 88% or more and the text blurred about 6 to 15 px, so the dot sits on calm canvas. The visual quality check now asserts that, under the trigger, the top edge's canvas color is at least 85% opaque and a blur layer covers it (where the edge doesn't show, still that no text meets the trigger), and “D-111: the note's edges blur progressively…” that the outermost 40 px layer starts at the window's edge. | Proposed, amended (owner) | [Foundations → Visual quality](../design/foundations.md#visual-quality), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html) |
| D-120 | **When PRF-04's memory is read (D-105 (7), proposed).** (1) **Why the bench had two modes:** on Windows CI `PRF-04.memoryMB` read 275 or 278 MB on some runs and 531 MB on others for the same build. It was one sample, 3 s after the bench's typing. For 15-20 s after use the renderer is still collecting garbage and handing memory back (its JavaScript heap goes from 22 of 83 MB to 15 of 16 MB, its private working set from about 350 to about 120 MB), so the sample landed before or after that at random. The processes were always this instance's own four (browser, GPU, network utility, one renderer; the search worker runs inside the renderer), and none was left over from an earlier step. Locally, three runs read 525, 537, and 547 MB just after use and 279, 282, and 283 MB settled. (2) **The reading now:** the same metric as D-105 (7), every process's private working set plus the largest shared working set, for the processes in Electron's `getAppMetrics()` and from one reading of Windows's process counters. It's taken after `fn:search.indexed`, the scripted use, and 20 s of rest; then a sample every 2 s until eight in a row (14 s) lie within 2% of their median, which is the reading (`tools/bench/settle.mjs`, at most 120 s, then the median of the last eight with a warning). This reads the spec's "resident with a 10,000-note workspace open" as the app at rest after use, not the transient just after a keystroke; a shorter window could take the 10-s plateau on the way down (about 296 MB) for the settled value. (3) **Explaining a reading:** in CI, or with `FOCUS_NOTES_BENCH_DETAIL=1`, the bench prints each sample with every process's private and shared working set and its private bytes (committed memory, about 70 MB more than resident in all), and the sample just after use. The 350 MB budget and the CI margin are unchanged. **For the owner:** whether PRF-04 means memory at rest (this reading) or the peak during use (about 540 MB with 10,000 notes, over budget); and whether resident working set or private bytes (about 355 MB settled) is the measure. | Proposed | [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#performance-budgets) |
| D-121 | **The note's edges without a GPU (D-111, proposed).** On Windows CI runners, which have no GPU, Chromium composites in software, and the note's eight stacked backdrop blurs dropped the window to a few frames a second: focus came back late, and the timing and A11Y-01 journeys failed at random. People without GPU compositing (remote desktops, virtual machines, blocklisted drivers) would get the same stutter. (1) **Detection:** the desktop's main process reads `app.getGPUFeatureStatus().gpu_compositing` (`apps/desktop/src/main/window/compositing.ts`): a status starting `enabled` is the GPU; `disabled_…` or `unavailable_…` is software. Chromium's status is only right after its first `gpu-info-update` (before it, even a GPU reads `disabled_software`), so the window starts on the GPU path and switches if the update says otherwise; the status is read again after the GPU process goes away (`child-process-gone`, type `GPU`), and after 3 s if no update ever came. (2) **Flow:** the window's query (`compositing=`), the preload's `focusNotes.compositing` (`initial`, `onChange`; IPC `compositing:changed`, sent again once the page has loaded), the app's state `compositing`, `vm.app.compositing`, and `AppRoot`'s `compositing` prop, which sets `data-compositing="software"` on the root. (3) **Fallback:** in `EditorSurface.css.ts`, `:root[data-compositing="software"]` hides the blur layers, as reduced transparency already did: the canvas-colored gradient fade alone, over the same band (since D-119's amendment, the top's eased curve starting fully opaque at the window's edge instead of 92%, so unblurred text under the dot is at most about 8% visible; no solid band). With the GPU the progressive blur is unchanged. Only the edge layers: the frosted surfaces are one blur each and stay. (4) **The browser** can't tell how it composites, so the web app keeps the blur; reduced transparency (`prefers-reduced-transparency`) still turns it off everywhere. (5) **Overrides:** `FOCUS_NOTES_GPU_COMPOSITING=gpu` or `software` (read in a packaged app too, to diagnose) forces the path; `FOCUS_NOTES_DISABLE_GPU=1` (a test seam) turns hardware acceleration off, to reproduce a CI runner. With `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E=1` the app prints `[focus-notes] compositing: <path> (gpu_compositing=<status>)` once, and the journeys' runner prints the first such line, so CI's log shows which path ran. (6) **Tests:** `apps/desktop/test/compositing.test.ts` and `packages/core/test/compositing-app.test.ts`; the journey "D-111: the note's edges blur progressively…" forces the GPU path and checks every blur layer is drawn, and "D-121: in software compositing…" checks the fade alone. The visual quality check expects no blur in software, as with reduced transparency. (7) **Measured** (2026-10-15, a 170 Hz laptop, scrolling a line a frame with both edges showing, frames in 500 ms): GPU 85 (vsync); software with the blur 23 at 100% zoom and 6 at 400% (12 frames a second, CI's symptom); software with the fallback 121 at either zoom. | Proposed | [Foundations → Visual quality](../design/foundations.md#visual-quality), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html) |
| D-122 | **The owner's Preview (D-113, accepted).** The owner can try the latest working app at any moment, without waiting for running work: a desktop shortcut, Focus Notes Preview, opens the newest commit on `main` whose Windows CI passed. An updater outside the repository (`focus-notes-preview\update.ps1`, a per-user scheduled task every 10 minutes) builds each new green commit, unpacked, into a folder of its own and writes what changed. A running Preview is never replaced, and journeys and builders never touch it. Getting a change to the owner means merging it green; the measure is the time from a green `main` to a Preview the owner can open (target 15 minutes). Data is sample content during development, so Preview shares the app's normal profile. Later the rolling preview becomes a GitHub release with in-app updates (WP-5.5). | Accepted | plan/agentic-loop.html#preview, AGENTS.md |
| D-123 | **Owner feedback on build 04705ae (D-111, proposed).** (1) **Files header:** the *New ▾* menu is replaced by two `IconButton`s up front, *New note* (`newNote` icon, its tooltip showing `note.new`'s shortcut, as `files.newNote` has none of its own) and *New folder* (a new `newFolder` icon, Fluent's FolderAdd, `Mod+Shift+N`), running the same commands; disabled with "This workspace is read-only here." where nothing can be written. `vm.files` exposes `newNote` and `newFolder` items in place of `newText` and `newMenu`. (2) **Side navigation fills the dialog:** a dialog's body is a flex column and vertical `Tabs` grow and stretch in it, so the list (and its divider) and the panel run from the top of the body to the bottom (Settings, the only dialog with side navigation). The journeys check every vertical tab list in a dialog body is as tall as the body's content. (3) **The first run's card is a dialog:** `Layer` `position="center"` (used only by the first run) keeps the 24 px dialog inset (`layout.gutterCompact`) and holds its content to the room inside it; a shaped `Surface` directly on the dialog layer marks itself `data-surface="dialog"`, so the inset rule checks it; and `Surface scroll` puts its padding inside a scrolling body with the minimal scrollbar and a stable gutter, so the card scrolls inside rather than running edge to edge. The whole card scrolls (its step heading with it); a fixed header and footer would need the card split into regions, not done. (4) **Vertical segmented control:** `SegmentedControl orientation="vertical"` lists choices one full-width row each (at least `size.controlM`, labels wrapping, never cut), still a radio group with arrow keys; the first run's *Workspace* choice uses it. (5) **Subtle buttons at the content's start bleed:** `Button bleed` pulls a subtle button's box into the padding by its inline padding, so its label lines up with the content's edge (a first-run step's *Back*); *Choose folder…* sits under the folder's path; and the end group of a step's buttons grows, so when the row wraps it stays at the end. | Proposed | [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Secondary views](../design/views/secondary-views.md), [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Components](../design/system/components.md), [Foundations → Visual quality](../design/foundations.md#visual-quality) |
| D-124 | **A first run anyone understands (the owner asked for it, proposed).** The owner asked to make setup super simple, in language anyone understands, even people who've never heard of GitHub, and to be able to pick an existing workspace once signed in. (1) **Four steps instead of seven:** Welcome, Connect, Your notes, Ready. Git is checked silently on Connect and shows only when it's missing or too old ("Focus Notes needs one free helper app to save your notes", one install button, *Check again*). Sign-in stays Git's own credential tooling (D-21, FRE-03): only the presentation changed. Connect's one button opens the browser sign-in; a device code, a token, the GitHub CLI, single sign-on, and *Use a different account* sit under *Having trouble?*. (2) **No identity step:** continuing sets the notes' name and email from the account (its name and private noreply address) for the notes workspace only; Git's global settings are never changed. Editing them later in Settings is queued. (3) **Your notes:** the account's Focus Notes workspaces (the existing detection, with each one's last push) are offered first as *We found your notes*, each with *Open*, which answers the owner's question; otherwise *Create my notes* with the existing defaults (`notes`, `notes-private`, `Documents/Focus Notes`, publishing on, as the spec has it, since the browser app reads it). Names, the folder, publishing, the browser passphrase, *Open another repository from GitHub…*, and *Open a folder on this computer…* sit under *More options*. The setup checklist is behind *Show details*, which opens by itself when a part fails. (4) **The vault passphrase leaves the main path:** nothing requires it at setup (D-98 already skips it with *Skip for now*), so it moved under *More options* as optional, and SEC-08's strength rule applies whenever it's set. Asking for it the first time the person turns on reading private notes in a browser, in Settings, is queued. (5) **Make it yours folds into Ready:** System, Light, or Dark on Ready; fonts, size, and focus mode stay in the ⋯ menu. (6) **Just use this computer for now** on Welcome: notes in `Documents/Focus Notes` with no account; Settings → Workspace → *Connect to GitHub* runs setup later, making the workspace in a free folder beside it. Moving the notes written meanwhile into it is queued. (7) **Settings → Workspace → Open another workspace from GitHub…** shows *Your notes* alone for a signed-in person; a clone goes in `Documents/Focus Notes`, or a free folder beside it when that's taken. (8) **Old progress resumes:** a first run saved at Git, Sign in, or Identity resumes at Connect; Workspace at Your notes; Make it yours at Ready. (9) **Words:** the setup word rules are in Foundations → Words. | Proposed (owner asked for it) | experience/first-run.md, product/requirements.md (FRE-01, FRE-05, FRE-06, FRE-08, SEC-08), product/spec/scenarios.md, product/spec/user-can.md, design/foundations.md#words, design/information-architecture.html, design/views/secondary-views.md, architecture/desktop-and-git.md |
| D-125 | **The share reader as built (WP-5.4, proposed).** (1) **One page, links in the fragment:** the share reader is the web app's second page, `s/index.html` (`apps/web/src/share`, built beside the app by `vite.config.ts`), not a page per grant at `/s/<token>/`, which would list grant IDs in the site's files and send them to the server. A magic link is `<site>/s/#<grant id>.<secret>` (`shareLink` and `parseShareFragment` in `packages/seal/src/grants.ts`); browsers never send the fragment, and the page removes any fragment that isn't a shared note's own (`#note=<path>`) with `history.replaceState` before it does anything else, so the secret leaves the address bar, this page's session-history entry, and referrers at once. The browser's global history (and its sync) still records the address the link was opened at, so on a shared computer the passcode is the better way in; the invite says so (WP-5.3). (2) **Rendering:** the view document `share.view.json` (IA-VIEW-PUBLIC, root `ReaderPage`) on a small registry (`packages/ui/src/share.ts`: the reader's components plus `Heading`, `TextField`, `Button`, `Spinner`, `Inline`), mounted in the browser by `mountPage` (`packages/ui-runtime/src/page.ts`); notes go through the public pages' model (`sharedPage` in `packages/core/src/public-page.ts`), so they read exactly like public pages, light or dark by the reader's preference. Links go only to notes the same share opens (`#note=<path>`); anything else, including any other fragment link (`#…`), becomes plain text, and a fragment naming a note outside the share shows the list (or the share's one note), so nothing outside a grant's scope is reachable by address. A share of several notes opens on "Shared with you", a list by title; a share of one note opens straight to it. The document's title is always "Private note", so no note title reaches the browser's history. (3) **State and commands** live in core (`createShareReader` in `packages/core/src/share-reader.ts`, `vm.share`, `share.submit`, `share.setRemember`, `share.signOut`, `share.showList`), with the sealed site and the session's storage supplied by the shell. (4) **One generic error:** every failure, a wrong name or passcode, an unknown, withdrawn, or expired grant, a wrong or damaged link, a stored session that no longer opens, or a file that fails to decrypt, shows the same words: "That didn't open anything. Check your name and passcode, or the link, and try again. If it still doesn't work, ask the person who shared it with you." This replaces the separate "expired or withdrawn" and "link not valid" messages the design pages had: the reader can't tell them apart (an ended grant's keyring is simply gone), and telling them apart would say a grant had existed (SHR-06, D-117 (8)). An empty field asks for it instead. (5) **Names:** `normalizeName` now also turns every run of white space into one space, so "SAM   lee " finds Sam Lee; the publisher and the reader use the same function and deploy together. (6) **Sessions (as revised after the security review):** `SiteReader.openLinkSession`, `openPasscodeSession`, and `resume` derive straight to a **non-extractable** AES-GCM `CryptoKey` (decrypt only) and return a session `{ v: 1, via, ref, key, expiresAt }`: the key, the grant's ID for a link or the name's lookup for a passcode, and the grant's expiry from its keyring; never the link secret, the name, the passcode, or the key's bytes. `parseShareSession` accepts only such a key, and an expired or malformed session fails before any request. The page keeps it in IndexedDB (`focus-notes-share`, store `sessions`), by structured clone, which keeps the key non-extractable, with `savedAt`, `usedAt`, and whether it's remembered (`apps/web/src/share/session.ts`); text storage holds nothing secret: `sessionStorage` holds only this tab's random record ID (`focus-notes.share`), and `localStorage` only `focus-notes.share.signedOutAt`. `pickSession` (core) uses this tab's record, else the device's one remembered record, and deletes records saved before the last sign-out, past their grant's expiry, tab records unused 12 hours, and remembered records of never-expiring grants unused 30 days (each use renews it). A link always opens for its tab only; *Open and remember* or *Remember on this device* marks the record remembered. *Sign out on this device* records the time, deletes every record, and tells every tab through a `BroadcastChannel`; a session that stops opening (withdrawn, expired, a changed passcode) forgets only its own record. Remembered keys survive new builds (keyring salts derive from the grant's) and stop at the publish that withdraws or expires the grant; access ends at the first publish after the expiry, and the page itself hides the share at the expiry. What remains: any script on the same `<user>.github.io` origin can *use* a stored key while it's there (open the share), but can't copy it out; and `ref`, a lookup of the name, isn't the name but can be recovered by guessing names. Closing a tab ends its session in that tab (its record is deleted after 12 hours unused, or at sign-out). An unknown name costs the same 600,000-iteration derivation as a wrong passcode (against a fixed dummy salt), so the time taken doesn't show whether a name has a share. (7) **"Remember on this device" as buttons, not a checkbox:** the form has [Open] and [Open and remember], like the vault's *Unlock* and *Unlock and remember*, and a signed-in page's footer has a *Remember on this device* toggle button and *Sign out on this device*, so link readers can choose too; no new component. (8) **Site just updated:** a note that fails to read is read once more from a fresh copy of the site (a deploy may have replaced the build), then the generic error; no reload and no separate message. (9) **Page rules:** the page empties itself and stops if it's inside a frame (a meta CSP can't set `frame-ancestors`); its CSP is the app's with `trusted-types 'none'` (it renders no HTML from text) and `img-src 'self'` (no `data:`), plus `noindex, nofollow` and `no-referrer`; it fetches only the site's own files (`credentials: "omit"`), sets no cookies, and loads about 191 KB of compressed JavaScript (PRF-05's 300 KB, measured in the journey). react-aria's injected `<style>` for touch-action on pressable controls is blocked by the CSP, as in the app (harmless). **Security review (2026-10-15, no critical or high findings):** the medium finding (the key stored as text) and the low and informational ones (history wording, sign-out and tab-close overclaims, a new link inheriting *remember*, name timing, `ref` wording, fragment links, framing, the expiry race, `data:` images) are fixed as described above. **Open:** the shared-origin warning isn't shown beside *remember* (readers may not know what an origin is), so the owner's other Pages sites on the same origin could use a remembered share key, though not copy it; WP-5.3's invite builds links with `shareLink`; the light/dark toggle (D-114 (4)) isn't built here either. | Proposed (builder) | [Sharing and visibility → The share reader](../design/views/sharing.md#the-share-reader), [Secondary views](../design/views/secondary-views.md#public-note-page), [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#sealing-and-share-links), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md) |
| D-126 | **Setup is a full window, shows rather than describes, and nothing else works until it's done (the owner asked for it, accepted).** On build ab5c30b the owner asked for a bold Welcome, not a dialog. (1) **Full window:** every setup step is one onboarding screen: the whole window below the title bar band (`Layer` position `window`; the band stays the window's, so it drags and shows its controls), on the canvas color with no card, border, radius or shadow (a `canvas` `Surface` that scrolls inside, D-111), its content a 560 px column (`layout.onboarding`) centred in the width and, while it fits, the height (`Center` `block: middle`). The progress ("Step 2 of 4") stays. **Type ramp:** the interface ramp had no display size; it gains **Display** 40 px (`font.uiDisplay`, line height 1.25, `Heading` level `display`, an h1) for every step's title and **Lead** 18 px (`font.uiLead`, `Text` role `lead`) for its one sentence; main actions are large (`Button` size `l`: 44 px, 16 px text). (2) **Welcome is the hero:** the app's icon at 112 px, a new `AppMark` primitive drawing the desktop icon's geometry (`scripts/icon.mjs`) with new `brand.*` tokens (Paper's tile, ring and dot, the same in every theme), centred with the title, "Your notes are saved online for free using GitHub." beside GitHub's mark, [Get started] large and *Just use this computer for now* quiet below it. Ready shows the icon at 48 px. (3) **GitHub's mark:** `mark-github` from GitHub's Octicons (@primer/octicons 19.40.0, MIT), only its path, copied into `packages/ui/src/github-mark.tsx` and credited in the third-party notices' *Copied into the app*; the one icon outside Fluent, used where the interface names GitHub, unmodified, one color (the text's), never larger than the app's icon (GitHub's logo guidelines). (4) **Show, don't describe (D-127's visual score):** a new `ItemCard` primitive: Connect's account is a profile card (the picture, the name large, @login with GitHub's mark; [Continue as *first name*]), or, with no account yet, a *GitHub* card with the mark; the notes found are rows (an icon, the name, "Updated 3 days ago", a *Public*/*Private* badge, [Open]); *Create my notes* has a *New notes* option card; Ready's hints have the red dot and the search icon. Copy is shorter to fit the 40-word budget. **The picture** comes from the account API's `avatar_url`, fetched by the main process from `avatars.githubusercontent.com` only (no credentials, no redirects, 512 KB at most, 5 s), cached in `avatar.json` in the data folder (Security and privacy → Data inventory) and handed to the window as a `data:` URL, so the renderer makes no third-party request and its CSP is unchanged; offline or missing, the card shows the initials. (5) **Nothing else until setup is done:** while it's open the trigger, toolbar, account pill and note aren't rendered, and every app command is held at the command layer (`CommandRegistry.setHold`): disabled with "Finish setting up first." wherever it's listed (the macOS menu bar, Spotlight), its keys match nothing, and running it does nothing. Setup's own commands, the window's, zoom, toasts, and what its steps run (Ready's look, *Bring notes from a browser…*) still work. (6) **Checks:** `vq-flows` measures every primary-path step's visual anchor and words (`e2e/visual-score.ts`) at 480 × 360, the default size and 1600 × 1000, light and dark, and that Welcome isn't drawn as a dialog (no border, shadow or radius; it fills the window) with its icon and title in view and centred; `m3-first-run` checks that during setup the trigger, toolbar, pill and note are absent, <kbd>Ctrl+K</kbd>, <kbd>Ctrl+O</kbd>, <kbd>Ctrl+/</kbd>, <kbd>Ctrl+N</kbd> open nothing and focus stays in setup, and that everything works after. | [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Foundations](../design/foundations.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md) |
| D-127 | **Visual over verbose (the owner asked for it).** The owner asked to use the right UI elements and be more visual than verbose, with a framework for a visual vs verbose score and a minimum visual score for a UI to be acceptable. (1) **The score:** every surface that presents a task or a state gets a **VVS** of 0–100 from five criteria worth 0–20 each: visual anchor, glanceable, right element for the information, text economy, and hierarchy, each with anchored rubric examples for 0, 10, and 20. (2) **Thresholds:** VVS ≥ 70 with no criterion below 10; first-run (onboarding) steps and empty states need ≥ 80. (3) **Budgets** of interface words on the primary path: first-run step 40, dialog 60, panel or side sheet 80, Settings section 80. (4) **Measurement:** the visual-quality journeys measure the anchor and the words; the design-system-reviewer scores the other three from light and dark screenshots; the five scores per surface go into the evidence record and the gate evidence. (5) **Where:** every new or changed surface at its work package's definition of done; existing surfaces at the next stage gate, with anything below threshold going into the feedback queue. **Refinements of the orchestrator's draft:** (a) Hierarchy names the interface type ramp's real steps (title, emphasis, body, secondary or caption): there's no *display* token, and adding one goes through the component process. (b) An **empty state budget of 30** words (a title of 6, one sentence of 20, and an action), since empty states carry the 80 threshold but the draft had no budget for them; popovers share the dialog budget of 60. (c) Deterministic scoring for the automated criteria: the anchor is the largest non-text element fully in view, 20 at 48 px or more, 10 at 24–47 px, 0 otherwise, which the reviewer may lower with a reason but never raise; text economy is 20 × (2B − W) / B clamped to 0–20, so full marks within the budget and 0 at twice it, capped at 10 if any paragraph wraps past 2 lines. (d) Words counted are the interface's own: the person's content and data (note text, file names, results, a name and @login) don't count, or every list would fail. (e) **Scope:** menus, tooltips, toasts, toolbars, and the trigger aren't scored, since their job is a command or a word; confirmations are exempt from the anchor, because their question is the anchor; text-purpose surfaces (the note canvas, help pages, legal notices) are exempt from text economy, and their anchor may be the content's display heading, since a calm canvas has no 48 px graphic by design (CP-CALM-01). With a criterion exempt, the other four are scaled ×1.25. (f) When light and dark screenshots score differently, the lower counts. (g) The worked example scores the old Connect step at 40 and the profile-card target at 86; the old step already met the word budget, which is why a word limit alone isn't enough. | Accepted (owner asked for it) | [Visual vs verbose score](../design/visual-score.html), [Foundations → Visual vs verbose score](../design/foundations.md#visual-score), [Foundations → Words](../design/foundations.md#words), [Definition of done](definition-of-done.md#work-package-definition-of-done), [Definition of done → When checks run](definition-of-done.md#when-checks-run), `AGENTS.md`, `.github/agents/design-system-reviewer.agent.md` |
| D-128 | **The release pipeline as built (WP-5.5, DST-03, proposed).** (1) **One pipeline:** `release.yml` runs on a pushed `v<x.y.z>` tag and drafts the release; a person checks the draft and publishes it. It supersedes `preview-release.yml`: stage exits (`v0.<stage>.<n>`) and suffixed versions are drafted as pre-releases by the same pipeline instead of being published straight away. (2) **Version:** the tag is the version, written into the packaged app with electron-builder's `extraMetadata.version`, so a release never needs a commit that bumps `package.json`; the bundle, the template and the SBOM carry the same version. (3) **Dry run:** a manual run (dry run ticked by default, read case-insensitively; on a branch it's always a dry run, versioned `<desktop version>-dryrun.<run number>`), or a tag spelled exactly `v<x.y.z>-dryrun[.n]`, builds, smoke-tests and checksums everything and attests and drafts nothing, so no dry run's files can pass an attestation check (review M2). A tag that reads like a dry run in any other case or spelling (`-DryRun`, `-dry-run`, `-beta.dryrun`) is refused rather than released (L7). Dry-run tags never count as the "last release" for notes. (4) **Jobs and permissions:** `version` (notes from the commits since the previous release tag, `tools/release/notes.mjs`), `desktop` (Windows, macOS, Linux; only Windows blocks, D-112; the Windows build's fuses and packaged smoke test as in nightly), `web` (the web app, the page renderer and the publisher, bundled by `tools/release/web-bundle.mjs` into `focus-notes-web-<version>.tgz` and smoke-tested by publishing a sample note with the bundle's own publisher; the template pinned to it; the SBOM) all read-only; `collect` (read-only) downloads each `asset-*` artifact into its own folder, fails if two hold a file with the same name, then flattens them and writes `SHA256SUMS` (L8); `attest` runs only for a real release, builds nothing, and alone holds `id-token: write` and `attestations: write`; `release` alone holds `contents: write`, in the `release` environment, only when it isn't a dry run, and first checks that the tag, peeled if annotated, still names `GITHUB_SHA` (L6). No job restores a cache (setup-node's `package-manager-cache: false`, since it turns its cache on by itself when `package.json` names a `packageManager`, and pnpm/action-setup's cache stays off, L9), every checkout drops its credentials, every action is pinned by SHA, and scripts get values only through `env`. (5) **SBOM:** CycloneDX 1.6 from `pnpm licenses list --prod` (the list `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES` comes from), made in the repository rather than by a downloaded scanner. (6) **Template releases:** the release attaches `focus-notes-workspace-<version>.tgz`, `templates/workspace/` with `FOCUS_NOTES_VERSION`, `FOCUS_NOTES_SHA256` and `templateVersion` pinned to that release's bundle (`tools/release/template.mjs`), so a copy finds its next pin on the latest release and checks it with `SHA256SUMS` and the attestation, as its workflow checks the bundle. **Verification** names the signer and the ref, not just the repository: `gh attestation verify <file> --repo prabinpebam/focus-notes --signer-workflow prabinpebam/focus-notes/.github/workflows/release.yml --source-ref refs/tags/v<x.y.z>`, in the release notes, in [Distribution](distribution.md), and in the template's *Deploy app* workflow (with `v$FOCUS_NOTES_VERSION`), so only this workflow's attestation for that exact tag passes (M2). The template's *Deploy app* workflow now passes the bundle's `publish/` as `--reader` and the site's address as `--site-url`, so template instances get public pages too (WP-5.1). Pushing the pinned template to `focus-notes-workspace` and opening update pull requests in instances stay WP-5.6. (7) **Known gaps:** unsigned and not notarized until DSK-07 (no secrets in this repository, PKG-04), so no `latest*.yml` update metadata yet; x64 only; installs on clean machines and the test-account smoke runs are manual release-checklist steps. (8) **Set by hand (repository settings, not files; M1, L6):** the `release` environment has no protection until the owner adds themselves as its required reviewer and gives it a deployment policy that admits only `v*` tags; and a tag ruleset on `v*` limits creating, moving and deleting release tags to the owner. Until then, anyone with write access could push a tag and get a draft. (9) **Package registry (L10):** CI installs from the registry the repository's `.npmrc` names, a package feed proxy rather than the npm registry itself; every package is still checked against the integrity hashes in `pnpm-lock.yaml` (`--frozen-lockfile`), so the proxy can withhold a package but not change one. `.npmrc` is unchanged. | Proposed | [Distribution](distribution.md), [Gate evidence](gate-evidence.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) |
| D-129 | **The rolling preview release (D-122 phase 3, proposed).** `preview-channel.yml` runs on `workflow_run` when `ci` completes, and acts only for a successful `push` run on `main` from this repository itself, and only while the commit `ci` tested (`head_sha`) is still `main`'s tip: under `workflow_run`, `GITHUB_SHA` is the tip, and it's the commit an attestation names, so building anything else would attest the wrong commit. When `main` has moved on, the gate stops quietly (a notice, not a failure) and the newer commit's own `ci` run publishes; the build also refuses outright if the two differ (review M3). It also reads the run's jobs and needs every job with "windows" in its name (the Windows unit job and the four journey shards) to have succeeded, so a later change to `ci.yml` can't let a red Windows build through. It builds exactly `ci`'s `head_sha` on Windows with read-only permissions, as `<desktop version>-preview.<run number>`, zips the unpacked app before the packaged smoke test (which leaves a test copy of the executable beside it, D-116), runs the fuse check and the smoke test, then a separate job writes `SHA256SUMS` and attests, and the only writing job deletes the `preview` pre-release and its tag and creates it again at that commit with the installer, the zip, `commit.txt` and `SHA256SUMS`. A run for a commit that the published preview is already ahead of does nothing, and the build and publish jobs each queue rather than cancel one another (job-level concurrency, so a run that stops at the gate never displaces a green one waiting, L4), so the preview never goes back. Only HTTP 404 reading the `preview` tag means there's no preview yet; any other error fails the job (L5). The commit subject in its notes is escaped like `notes.mjs` escapes subjects (L11). Check a download with `gh attestation verify <file> --repo prabinpebam/focus-notes --signer-workflow prabinpebam/focus-notes/.github/workflows/preview-channel.yml --source-ref refs/heads/main`. For DST-07: Beta update channels must match only `^v\d+\.\d+\.\d+` tags and exclude `preview`, which is a pre-release too. Between the delete and the create, about a minute, there's no `preview` release; a reader retries on its next check. The owner's updater (`focus-notes-preview\update.ps1`, outside the repository) can switch from building to downloading this release; it stays as it is until the owner chooses to switch. | Proposed | [Distribution → The rolling preview](distribution.md#the-rolling-preview), [The agentic loop → Preview](agentic-loop.html#preview) |
| D-130 | **Ctrl+wheel sets the text size, and the focus band follows line metrics (owner, 2026-10-15, proposed).** (1) **Ctrl+wheel and pinch.** <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> with the wheel runs `font.larger` (up) and `font.smaller` (down), the same commands, steps, limits and persistence as <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>=</kbd> / <kbd>-</kbd>, and <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>0</kbd> resets. A trackpad pinch arrives as a wheel with Ctrl (Windows precision touchpads and macOS), so it does the same. Deltas add up to 50 px a step (a mouse notch is about 100 px), and one event is at most one step, so a pinch or a high-resolution wheel never jumps several sizes. The handler cancels the event, so the page never zooms: the text size is the only zoom. On macOS <kbd>⌘</kbd>+wheel isn't a zoom gesture anywhere, so it keeps scrolling; pinch is the Mac's gesture. It lives in the runtime's global input handler (`ui-runtime/src/mount.ts`), beside the keys. (2) **The focus band follows line metrics.** Besides the canvas, the band watches the caret's block (both ends of a selection) with a `ResizeObserver`, re-aimed as the caret moves, and the window's `resize`. Before, it watched only the canvas, which in a short note keeps its minimum height when the text grows, so after <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>=</kbd> the band kept the old line's top and height and the caret's row was half dim. Any change of line metrics (text size, content font, zoom, a web font loading) changes the caret's block size, so one observer covers them all. | Proposed | [Focus mode → When it recomputes](../experience/focus-mode.md#when-it-recomputes), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md) |
| D-131 | **Setup polish: accurate not-found errors, compact short windows, leading actions, and a pressable option card (D-124, D-126, D-127).** A 404 from GitHub is never "your sign-in doesn't allow this": the engine maps only a real 403 to `permission`, a 404 to `notFound` ("GitHub couldn't find something this step needs…"), and a 404 on *Copy the template*'s generate to `templateMissing` ("Focus Notes couldn't find its starter notes on GitHub. Try again in a minute, or tell us if it keeps happening."). Below `breakpoint.short` (560 px tall, a height) the setup is compact: `AppMark` size l shows at `size.appMarkM`, a display `Heading` at `font.uiTitle`, and the setup's `Surface` and `Stack`s use their new `shortPadding` and `shortGap`. After Welcome (the centred hero), every step's action row starts at the content column's edge: the primary button first, then other actions, then *Back*; nothing is right-aligned, so the primary is the focal point under left-aligned content. An `ItemCard` with a `press` action is one button (one tab stop, a focus ring, an accent border and a chevron), named by what it shows; *Create my notes* is that card, with no separate button. A view `switch` can say `"remount": true`: each case then mounts afresh, so a step's primary takes focus even where the last step had a button. Only the setup's step switches (`step`, `connect`, `panel`) remount; disclosures (*Having trouble?*, *More options*, *Show details*) don't, so their button keeps focus as they open and close. | Accepted | [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Visual vs verbose score](../design/visual-score.html) |
| D-132 | **Saved here or backed up to the cloud, shown calmly, and Save to cloud (the owner asked for it, proposed).** The owner had no visual feedback telling *saved on this computer*, which is instant, from *committed and pushed*, which people know as *saved to the cloud*, and asked what <kbd>Ctrl+S</kbd> means when everything is saved as it's typed. (1) **Words:** the interface says *saved on this computer* for the file on disk and *backed up* or *Save to cloud* for a commit and push; *commit* and *push* stay out of it. (2) **The save state** shows as one calm, muted icon at the leading edge of the bottom-left pill (UI-12), whether or not anyone is signed in, wherever a note is open on the desktop: *Saved on this computer* (a check; while a change is being written, and always where there's no Git), *Saved here · not backed up yet* (a cloud with a dot), *Backing up…* (a cloud with an arrow that rises gently, still with reduced motion), *Backed up at ⟨time of day⟩* (a cloud with a check; the time of day, since keeping "2 min ago" true would need a timer at rest, which PRF-04 rules out), *Offline · will back up when you're back* (a cloud with a slash), and *Couldn't back up · Try again* (a warning, in the danger color). It never pops up or takes focus: pointing at it or focusing it shows the words as its tooltip, pressing it backs up now (on a problem, that's the retry), and the pill's menu shows the state with *Save to cloud*. The shell reports waiting changes as soon as a write lands, so the icon follows typing within a second without asking Git. Icons are Fluent's (D-32), with four cloud glyphs added. (3) **Shortcuts:** <kbd>Mod+Shift+S</kbd> is **Save to cloud** (`sync.now`; it was free, as there's no Save As), and <kbd>Mod+S</kbd> runs the same, for muscle memory. When the person asks, the label opens beside the icon, *Backing up…* and then *Backed up*, for about 2 s, over the corner, so neither focus nor the note moves; screen readers hear only that result (a polite announcement) and problems (their toast), never every keystroke. (4) **When it backs up on its own** stays D-23's cadence for now; backing up by how much changed is the next step. | Proposed | [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html), [Feedback → Save and sync status](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#save-and-sync-status), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md), [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (EDT-11, UI-12) |
| D-133 | **Backing up by how much changed (the owner asked for it, proposed).** Supersedes D-23's cadence (60 s with no typing, and every note switch, made a commit for every pause). The shell counts the characters changed since the last backup (inserted plus deleted, between the common start and end of each write, across notes) and when the first change not yet backed up was made. (1) After a **60 s** pause in typing it backs up if at least **200** characters changed or the first change is over **10 minutes** old. (2) At least **2,000** characters back up after a **10 s** pause. (3) **Ceiling:** any change waiting **30 minutes** backs up at the next 10 s pause. (4) Switching or closing a note backs up only when one of those thresholds is met (whatever the pause); quitting always backs up (within the ≤ 8 s quit limit), and so does *Save to cloud* (D-132). (5) Each backup is one commit (*Update ⟨note⟩* or *Update ⟨n⟩ notes*). (6) The thresholds are one object (`BACKUP_POLICY` in `git/cadence.ts`), with `FOCUS_NOTES_IDLE_COMMIT_MS` (the 60 s pause) and `FOCUS_NOTES_BACKUP_SHORT_MS`, `_MIN_CHARS`, `_BIG_CHARS`, `_STALE_MS` and `_CEILING_MS` overrides for tests; no Settings yet. One pure function gives the earliest moment a backup is due, and one timer waits for it. (7) Offline and failed pushes keep their commits and retry with backoff, as before; the save state shows offline or the problem (D-132). | Proposed | [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync), [Editing](../experience/editing.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (EDT-11) |
| D-134 | **Sharing as built in the desktop app (WP-5.3, proposed).** (1) **Where:** Settings → **Sharing** (the category appears only where the shell keeps `access.json`, the desktop) lists *You* (the vault, when there is one) and every audience as a card with its initials, what it opens and how ("trips/ and 1 more · Link"), and a badge (Expires in 12 days, Never expires, Expired, Withdrawn), with [Invite] and a ⋯ menu (Change…, Extend 30 days, Withdraw…, Delete…). [Share with someone] opens the editor: the name, the ways in as two toggle chips (Link, Passcode; both on to start), the passcode as *Four random words* (shown large, with [New words]) or *My own* (the strength rule under the field), what they can read as toggle chips for every private folder and note with a filter (the note that's open is picked to start with), and *Works for* (1, 7, 30, 90 days, Always; 30 by default; a date picker isn't built). Saving shows the invite: the audience's card, the message as a preview card, [Copy invite], [Copy link], [Copy passcode] (each turns into "Copied" and says so in a live region), the passcode in large type, and the line *Changes are live after your next backup and publish.* Withdraw and Delete ask first, with focus on Cancel. The Share popover on a note (IA-VIEW-SHARE) isn't built here. (2) **Who writes `access.json`:** only the main process (`apps/desktop/src/main/share/access.ts`). The window sends a change as data (`access:apply`: create, update, withdraw, delete), checked field by field by `parseAccessChange`; the main process applies it with `applyAccessChange` (`packages/seal/src/access.ts`), which makes every secret with Web Crypto's random generator (the grant ID `s` + 16 base32 characters, a 16-byte salt, a 32-byte link secret, and the workspace's `nameSalt` on the first audience), checks it again with the publisher's own `validateAccess`, writes the file atomically, and leaves the commit to the save cadence like a note, so it reaches GitHub with the next backup and the site with the next publish. The vault's grant can't be changed, withdrawn, or deleted this way, and its derived key never leaves the main process: the window gets only the share audiences. (3) **Where secrets live:** a link's secret and a passcode stay in `private/access.json` in the private repository, as the spec says, so the invite can be shown and copied again; they are never written anywhere else, and the journey checks the public repository's history holds none of them. Generated passcodes are made in the main process (`access:suggest`, `packages/seal/src/words.ts`, the EFF large wordlist under D-52, now in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`), so the list never reaches a web page's bundle. (4) **Rules:** names are stored NFKC with white space collapsed and their case kept, at most 80 characters, unique among audiences that still work by `normalizeName` (case and spacing don't matter; a withdrawn or expired name can be used again; an expired audience extended back is checked again), and not "You". Every passcode, generated or not, must pass `passcodeProblem` (at least 12 characters and `passphraseStrength`), stored trimmed. An audience needs a way in and something to open; an expiry can't be in the past. Withdrawing keeps the record and its secrets (so the history stays complete) and can't be undone by an edit; Delete removes the record. (5) **The invite:** the template's lines from the catalog (`share.manage.invite.*`), with a line left out when it uses a placeholder the audience has no value for (no link, no passcode, never expires). The shared-computer line (D-125 (1)) is included only when the audience has both a link and a passcode, since it advises choosing the passcode. `{site}` is the share reader's page, `<site>/s/`. The site comes from setup's report, or from the instance's `origin` on github.com; without one, links can't be made and the page says so. (6) **After the security review (2026-10-15, no critical or high findings):** custom passcodes need 16 or more characters or 3 or more different words of 3 letters or more, and then `passphraseStrength` (M1: "Password1234", "Summer2026!!" and similar passed the bits estimate alone; a bundled 10k-password list was the alternative, rejected for size and licensing); the window's listing (`audienceOf`) holds no link secret, passcode, or salt, one audience's link and passcode reach the window only while its invite is open (`access:reveal`) and are dropped when it closes, and the editor's state is reset when it closes (L2); copying goes through `access:copy`, where the main process fills the invite's link, passcode, and site into the window's secret-free template and clears the clipboard after 90 s if it still holds that text (L1; Electron can't add Windows' clipboard-history exclusion formats beside text, so that's documented instead; journeys copy into memory, `FOCUS_NOTES_MEMORY_CLIPBOARD`, a test seam that's off in a packaged app, so they never touch the owner's clipboard); generated passcodes are made only in the main process (`{ generate: true }`), and only those are stored as `generated: true` (L5); an invite names a folder by its last segment (L3); names with control or format characters are refused as `name.invalid` (L4); a damaged access.json's error never quotes it (L6); the word list is pinned by a uniqueness test and the SHA-256 of its words (L7). The publisher's `validateAccess` uses the same function (`passcodeProblem`) for every passcode in force, so a hand-edited access.json can't get past the rule, and its error never quotes the value. **Open:** editing the template and storing it as `access-message.json` (SHR-09's editable template), the Share popover, and a date for the expiry. | Proposed | [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md#settings-sharing), [Repository and publishing → Sealing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#sealing-and-share-links) |
| D-135 | **Setup states that show rather than tell, and finish (the owner's review of every setup state, proposed).** The catalogue of every setup state (`fre-catalog.spec.ts`) found two bugs and states below the visual score (D-127). (1) **Try again finishes setup.** The app's `setup.retry` runs the failed step again and, when it passes and steps remain, resumes the whole sequence with the engine's `start` (its steps are idempotent, D-98, D-101); the engine's per-step `retry` is unchanged. (2) **A rejected token is said on the token form.** The form stays; the field is marked invalid with the reason as its description (`TextField` gains `error`, a `FieldError`), it takes focus, and the words say the token was cleared (the app never keeps a token, SEC-03); typing again clears the reason. (3) **A hero mark where a step had no visual anchor:** a new `StepMark` primitive (one icon in a 64 px round tile, 48 px in a short window; toned neutral, accent, danger or success; a turning ring while it waits, still with reduced motion; tokens `cmp.stepMark.*`, `size.stepMark`, `size.stepMarkIcon`) shows on checking, waiting for the browser or its window, the token form, Looking for your notes, Open from GitHub, Open a folder, Your notes offline, and Creating your notes (a rising cloud while it runs, a warning on failure, a cloud with a check when done). The one-time code is an item card with GitHub's mark, the code large, and *Copy*; Git missing or too old is the helper app's card with one install button. (4) **Fewer words:** a failure is one sentence (the failed step's problem, in the danger color) with *Try again* as the primary action and no disabled *Continue*; the details stay closed. *Having trouble?* and *More options* lose their explanatory paragraphs for compact rows with icons; the folder is one button with the path shortened in the middle and the whole path as its tooltip. (5) **Polish:** an invalid field is red whether focused or not, focus thickening the red edge; *Look again* shows only when looking for the notes couldn't reach GitHub, and then *Create my notes* is disabled with "Needs a connection to GitHub.". (6) **A gate:** the catalogue's journeys always run and assert (completion after Try again, errors shown where they happen, and on every setup state a full visual anchor and text economy of 10 or more); its screenshots stay opt-in (`FOCUS_NOTES_CAPTURE_FRE=1`). | Proposed | [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Foundations](../design/foundations.md), [Visual vs verbose score](../design/visual-score.html) |
| D-137 | **Template updates and publishing the template repository (WP-5.6, DST-06, proposed).** (1) **The pin is a file:** *Deploy app* reads the release to deploy from `.focus-notes/app.json` (`version` and `sha256`), not from its own `env:`, because a workflow's `GITHUB_TOKEN` can't get the `workflows` permission, so no workflow could otherwise propose a new pin. The first step after the checkout reads both values with `jq`, matches each whole (`[[ =~ ]]`, so a value with a newline can't add lines to `GITHUB_ENV`), refuses the placeholder `0.0.0`, and only then exports `FOCUS_NOTES_VERSION` and `FOCUS_NOTES_SHA256`; every later step is unchanged. `FOCUS_NOTES_SOURCE` stays in the workflow, so the pin can only name another genuine release of `prabinpebam/focus-notes` (the attestation check still binds the tag): the worst a changed pin can do is downgrade, and only through a commit to `main`. `tools/release/template.mjs` writes the pin (`pinApp`, replacing `pinWorkflow`). (2) **Update app** (`templates/workspace/.github/workflows/update.yml`, with `.github/focus-notes/update.mjs`, Node, no dependencies): weekly and by hand. A read-only `check` job (`contents: read`, `attestations: read`) picks the newest non-draft release tagged exactly `v<x.y.z>` that has the bundle, the template and `SHA256SUMS` (never `preview`, a dry run or a suffixed tag; stage exits below 1.0.0 count although GitHub marks them pre-releases), stops if it isn't newer than the pin, downloads the three files, checks both archives against `SHA256SUMS` and with `gh attestation verify --signer-workflow prabinpebam/focus-notes/.github/workflows/release.yml --source-ref refs/tags/v<x.y.z>`, and plans: the release's template must pin that version and exactly the checked bundle, and only paths in the release's `.focus-notes/managed.json` that differ change; a manifest path under `notes/` or `private/`, `.gitmodules`, `.focus-notes/workspace.json`, `.git`, or one that isn't plain and relative fails the run, and links in the archive are never followed. The `pull-request` job alone has `contents: write` and `pull-requests: write`, runs only from the default branch, and through the API makes one commit on the default branch's tip, force-moves the bot branch `focus-notes/update` to it, and creates the pull request, or retitles the open one for a newer release, or leaves it if it already names this version; so there's only ever one. It never merges; the owner reviews (DST-06). Workflow files (`.github/workflows/`) are never in the commit: the pull request lists them for a manual copy. Actions are pinned by SHA, checkouts drop credentials, no `${{ }}` in scripts, no `pull_request_target`, no secrets. The template repository itself doesn't run it. (3) **Set up by hand in each instance:** *Settings → Actions → General → Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests* (off by default; without it the pull request fails with HTTP 403 and says so). The desktop setup could turn it on (`PUT /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/permissions/workflow`); not yet. (4) **Publishing the template repository** is a manual release step, not a workflow: `node tools/release/publish-template.mjs --version <x.y.z>` uses the owner's `gh` login (which has the `workflow` scope), refuses a draft or suffixed version, downloads the release's template and `SHA256SUMS`, checks both checks, and makes `focus-notes-workspace`'s default branch hold exactly the template's files in one commit. A workflow would need a token for another repository; PKG-04 and `release-workflows.test.mjs` keep secrets out of this repository's workflows, so no `TEMPLATE_PUSH_TOKEN`. (5) **Open:** end-to-end evidence needs the first real release (publish the template, then an instance's update pull request from it); copies made from the template before this change (published 2026-10-15 12:25) have neither `update.yml` nor `app.json` and need one manual copy of both plus `pages.yml`. **(6) After the security review (2026-10-15, no critical or high findings):** archives are never unpacked by the update or the publisher: `readTgz` (in `update.mjs`) reads the `.tgz` in memory and refuses any member that isn't a plain file or folder (links, hard links, devices), a `.git` segment, a duplicate, a bad header checksum, or a path outside `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+)*$` (and never `.` or `..`); reading this repository's own files checks the real path stays inside it. The installed `update.mjs` changes only an allow-list (`.focus-notes/app.json`, `.focus-notes/managed.json`, `.github/focus-notes/*.mjs`, `.github/workflows/pages.yml` and `update.yml`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`), whatever a release's manifest says, and the pull request says *Review closely* when the update script itself changes. A release is taken only 3 days after `published_at`, unless the run's typed `skip-wait` input is ticked; **kill switch:** deleting a release (or its files) withdraws it, and a *Deploy app* pinned to it fails safe at the download, leaving the site as it was. `SHA256SUMS` must name each expected file exactly once. Every attestation check (*Deploy app*, *Update app*, `publish-template.mjs`) uses `--cert-identity https://github.com/prabinpebam/focus-notes/.github/workflows/release.yml@refs/tags/v<x.y.z>` with `--source-ref` and `--deny-self-hosted-runners`. Before moving `focus-notes/update`, the job checks the tip was made by `github-actions[bot]` on one parent that's on the default branch, and otherwise warns and stops; a version whose pull request was closed is skipped; creating the branch is the fallback only on HTTP 404 or 422. When workflow files differ, the title says *(action needed)* and the body gives each file's expected SHA-256; `app.json` records the release's workflow hashes (`workflows`, written by `pinApp`), and *Deploy app* warns while its own hash differs. `pages.yml` has `FOCUS_NOTES_MIN_VERSION` (`0.0.1`) and refuses any pin below it. Without *Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests* (a trade-off: any workflow with `pull-requests: write` could then open and approve pull requests), the branch is still pushed and the run's summary links the comparison to open the pull request by hand. `publish-template.mjs` writes the files into a fresh staging folder and commits through `git -c core.hooksPath=<empty folder> -c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.autocrlf=false --git-dir=<clone>/.git --work-tree=<staging>` on a `--no-checkout` clone, pushes only to `focus-notes-workspace` (or `focus-notes-workspace-test`), and refuses a version older than the newest published plain release unless `--allow-older`. `release.yml`'s `attest` job now runs in the `release` environment too, so nothing is attested until the owner approves the release (two approvals per release while both jobs are gated); the environment's required reviewer, its `v*` deployment policy and the `v*` tag ruleset stay settings the owner makes by hand (D-128 (8)). | Proposed | [Distribution](distribution.md), [Gate evidence](gate-evidence.md) |
| D-138 | **Committed generated files are verified, and decision numbers come from the orchestrator (proposed).** The UI schemas in `schemas/ui/` fell out of date on main twice on 15 October because nothing compared them with their source. (1) **UI schemas:** `ui-check --verify` (what `pnpm ui:check`, and so `pnpm check`, runs locally and in CI on every platform) generates `view.v1.json` and `contracts.v1.json` in memory and fails, without writing, when the committed files differ, ignoring CRLF versus LF; the message names the stale files and the command to regenerate them: `pnpm --filter @focus-notes/ui-runtime exec node src/cli/ui-check.ts --write`. A changed contract, icon set or token group means regenerating them in the same commit. (2) **Other generated files:** `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` was already verified (`notices.mjs --check` in `check:licenses`); the desktop icon (`apps/desktop/build/icon.*`, `icon.mjs --check`, which existed but ran nowhere) and the web entry documents (`apps/web/src/index.html`, `s/index.html`, `entry.mjs` gains `--check`) are verified by a new `check:generated` in `pnpm check`. Token output (`packages/tokens/dist/`) and the desktop entry documents are ignored by Git and rebuilt before use, so they can't go stale. (3) **Decision numbers:** the orchestrator assigns them; each builder brief names the D- numbers its builder may use, and builders never take the next free number themselves, because parallel branches collided four times on 15 October (D-113). | Proposed | [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md), [Agentic loop](agentic-loop.html) |
| D-139 | **Public pages, second part: Copy public link, the index, and PRF-06 (WP-5.1, proposed).** (1) **Copy public link** is a command, `note.copyPublicLink`, in the ⋯ menu's Note group after *Share and visibility…*, in the macOS File menu, in Spotlight's command list, and as the quick action on a public note's result. It copies the note's page address (`publicNoteUrl`, D-114) and says "Public link to “⟨note⟩” copied". It's available only for a note in the public root (the desktop's `notes/`), and only once the site's address is known; otherwise it's disabled, saying "Only public notes have a public link" or "Public links work once your site is set up". A command's unavailable reason can now depend on the state (`disabledReason` takes a function). (2) **The site's address** comes to the window with the workspace's `info()` (`site`), from the same place Share uses: setup's report, else the notes repository's `origin` on github.com (`siteFromGitConfig`). It's read when the format is checked, at start and when the format file changes, so a site that setup finds later shows after the next launch. The browser's instance doesn't report one yet, so there the action stays unavailable with the second reason. (3) **The index** of public notes is a page at `/n/` (`n/index.html`), rendered by the same renderer, stylesheet, and document as the notes' pages (the `index` view document, `vm.publicIndex`, `renderIndexPage`): a heading, the count, and a row per note, newest first: its title (a level-2 heading linking to its page), "Updated ⟨date⟩", and its excerpt only when front matter gives one (`excerpt:`, one line, up to 300 characters). The publisher writes it only when there's at least one public note, so its empty case ("No public notes yet") is there for `ui:check` but not published. The site's root stays the web app. (4) **The release bundle** already carries the renderer: WP-5.5's `web-bundle.mjs` packs `apps/web/dist-reader` as `publish/render.mjs` and `publish/assets/`, its smoke test publishes a page with it, and the template's *Deploy app* passes `--reader $RUNNER_TEMP/bundle/publish`; nothing was missing. (5) **PRF-06** is measured by `pnpm bench` (`tools/bench/publish.ts`, `PRF-06.publishMs`, budget 300,000 ms): the real publisher on a generated instance of 1,000 public notes (rendered, indexed) and 200 private notes in 20 folders sealed for 10 audiences (5 by link, 5 by passcode) and the owner's vault, leak check included. (6) **Share and visibility…** in the ⋯ menu and the macOS File menu now opens Settings → Sharing (WP-5.3) until its popover is built, instead of saying sharing arrives later; where there's no Settings (the browser) it's unavailable, saying "Sharing is in the desktop app's Settings". | Proposed (builder) | [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md), [Secondary views](../design/views/secondary-views.md#public-note-page), [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html), [Gate evidence](gate-evidence.md) |
| D-140 | **Local diagnostics as built (WP-5.7, QLT-04, proposed).** (1) **Where:** Settings → **About** (desktop only, where the shell builds diagnostics) previews the report as six cards, one per part (the app and OS, Git and its credential helper, the workspace's repositories, the recovery journal, compositing, recent problems), says what's never included, and has *Copy diagnostic report*, *Save diagnostic report…*, and *Report a problem…*; all three are in Spotlight and in the macOS Help menu. (2) **Who builds it:** the main process gathers facts (versions; `git version` and whether Git Credential Manager is the helper and the GitHub CLI is installed, no network call; for each repository only its role, whether `origin` is on GitHub, elsewhere or missing, branch and upstream, ahead, behind, changed and unmerged counts, and conflicts held; the journal's note count and bytes; compositing; device settings by value, a custom theme as "custom"; the window's timings, each name's count, p50 and p95, checked again in the main process; and its own log) and builds the Markdown in `@focus-notes/core/diagnostics`.
