The window gets only the preview's summary and never the report. (3) **Redaction:** the report is made from facts, never from note text, file names or command output. Every value from the facts goes through `redact` with the **known strings** (the GitHub login and name, Git's user.name and user.email, setup's repository names, and each remote's owner and repository name, read locally before each report), whole words in any case; then every line again without them, so the report's own words stay readable. `redact` reads at most 4 KB and every pattern is bounded (20,000 adversarial characters take under 50 ms), and it takes out: the workspace's and the home folder's paths (either slash, any case, whole folders only) **with everything after them** to a quote, `)` or the end of the line; credentials in addresses, an address's path (the host stays), Git's SSH form; GitHub and bearer tokens; secret-style values (`password`, `passcode`, `passphrase`, `secret`, `token`, `key`, `authorization`), quoted or not, after `=`, `:` or a space; private keys; email addresses; device sign-in codes (`ABCD-1234`); runs of 32 or more base64 or hex characters; any other absolute path (a drive letter, UNC in either slash, `/a/b` and MSYS's `/c/…`) **to a quote or the end of the line, spaces included**; and runs of words ending in a Markdown file name. Timings keep only an allow-list of the app's own measure names (the task tracker's targets are fixed command IDs, never a note's name). Tests feed it tokens, note text, paths with spaces and non-Latin names, emails, and repository names from every field and check none comes out (the security review's findings, 2026-10-15). (4) **The log stays in memory:** the spec's redacted, rotated 7-day log file is replaced by the last 200 lines the main process logs (counts, states and problem codes), redacted as they arrive and gone on quit, so no new file is kept; a crash's lines are therefore lost, and a file log is open for the owner to decide. (5) **In English:** the report is written for maintainers reading a GitHub issue, so its words are fixed English, not interface messages. (6) **Saved where the person chooses**, named `focus-notes-diagnostics-<date>.md`, with the system's overwrite confirmation; a place inside the workspace (it would be committed, maybe published) or the app's data folder is refused and the dialog asks again, through links and in any case. Nothing is sent (D-55). (7) **Report a problem** copies the report and opens the project's new-issue form at a fixed address with a short template; the person pastes the report there (D-203 replaces this with the *Send feedback* dialog, which posts the issue when signed in and continues here otherwise). It isn't pre-filled in the address, as QLT-04's "pre-filled" might suggest, because opening that address would send the report to GitHub before the person had read it. | Proposed | [Performance and quality → Diagnostics](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#diagnostics), [Security and privacy → Data inventory](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Secondary views](../design/views/secondary-views.md) |
| D-141 | **The window's shadow stays inside its margin, vertical maximize as on Windows, and journeys never take over the owner's screen (the owner asked for it, accepted).** (1) **Shadow:** the owner asked that the drawn shadow never be cut by the native window's edge. The native route was tried on paper and rejected: Windows 11's DWM rounding is 8 px, not D-14's 20 px, and DWM's shadow follows the native (rectangular, margin-including) bounds of a transparent window, not the drawn corners; a non-transparent window would also lose D-111's overlays-inside-the-window and the click-through margin. So the CSS shadow stays (`cmp.appRoot.windowShadow` = `shadow.1`, 16 px `window.margin`, unchanged), with a rule: each layer's offset + spread + 1.5 × blur (where Chromium's Gaussian ends, 3σ) is at most the margin on every side, in every theme and mode (`shadowExtent` in `window-geometry.ts`, a unit test over the built `tokens.css`). It reaches 14 px below the window and 10 px above. A journey captures the app's own window (`webContents.capturePage`, light and dark) and checks its outermost pixel rows and columns are fully transparent while the shadow below the window isn't. Measured on the owner's machine at 100 %, GPU and software compositing, light and Ink: no pixel of the native edge differs from the backdrop. A maximized, snapped, vertically maximized, or full-screen window has no margin or shadow. (2) **Vertical maximize:** a transparent window has no native resize border (`WS_THICKFRAME`), so Windows can't do it; the app does (`verticalSnapAt`, `verticalRect`; `CustomWindow.maximizeVertically`, `toggleVertical`, `snapKey`): resizing by the top or bottom edge until the pointer is within 8 px of the work area's top or bottom (the taskbar counts), still on the window's display, previews and then fills the work area's height at the same x and width; double-clicking the top or bottom resize border toggles it (`WindowFrame`'s `toggleVertical` event, the `window.toggleVertical` command, "Maximize or restore window height"); <kbd>Win</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>↑</kbd>/<kbd>↓</kbd> do it if the shell passes them to the window. It's a snapped state (square, no margin); dragging it away or Restore puts back the height before; it refits when the work area changes and is remembered (`window.json` `vertical: true`). The real gestures are checked by hand. (3) **Screen safety:** a local journey run maximized and snapped windows over the owner's apps. Journeys that maximize, snap, go full screen, or resize the real window skip locally unless `CI` or `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_ALLOW_SCREEN=1` is set (`screenAllowed`, `SCREEN_SKIP` in `e2e/fixtures.ts`: all of `m2-window`'s app-built window journeys, `m2-titlebar`'s full screen, `m2-demo`'s full-screen step, and `fre-catalog`'s small and large captures); they still run in full on CI. AGENTS.md: never take over the owner's screen. | Accepted (owner) | [Desktop app → Window management](../experience/desktop-app.html#window-management-on-windows-and-linux), [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md) |
| D-142 | **Device code by default, and setup states that look like their state (the owner's items after the v2 review, proposed).** (1) **Connect to GitHub is a device code** through Git's own helper (`git credential-manager github login --device`, or `gh auth login --web`): the code shows in the app large and monospaced (`font.uiCode`) with its own *Copy* and [Copy code and open GitHub], which opens `github.com/login/device`, then "Waiting for you to approve on GitHub…". Where Git Credential Manager shows the code in its own window instead of printing it, the step says to finish there. GCM's browser sign-in (*Sign in in the browser instead*) and a token (*Use a token instead*) move under *Having trouble?*; declined, expired and offline recover as before (FRE-02). (2) **One anchor per step:** Connect and Your notes always show the `StepMark` hero in the same place, its icon and tone saying the state (a download for an app to install, GitHub's mark ringed while waiting, toned success when connected, danger on a problem). (3) **The title names the state:** *Checking this computer*, *Install the helper app*, *Update the helper app*, *Install the sign-in helper*, *You're connected*, *Use this GitHub account* (the GitHub CLI's session), *Enter the code on GitHub*, *Approve on GitHub*, *Use a token*, *Looking for your notes*, *Create your notes*, *Your notes are ready*. (4) **New catalogue components:** `StepDots` (the stepper: four dots, the current one a longer segment, named "Step N of 4"), `StatusRow` (an icon and one sentence on a tinted row, near the top: every setup problem), `CodeBlock` (a command or a code, monospaced, with Copy), `VersionCompare` (found → needed pills), `StrengthMeter` (four segments under the passphrase, a meter to assistive technology), and the `Columns` layout (two short fields on a row, stacked when narrow). (5) **Errors:** the status row near the top plus the field at fault marked invalid with a short error, and focus moved to it; a fresh token form never shows the last sign-in's problem; with no helper to keep a sign-in, the primary is *Install the sign-in helper*. (6) **Actions:** every step's primary is `size.controlL`; found notes have one primary *Open* (the first row's), the rest secondary; the *Create my notes* card's tile is accent and its title larger than body text; disclosure content sits on the column's edge; a part setup didn't run shows a neutral dash ("Not run"), a part not started a hollow circle. | Proposed | [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md), [Visual vs verbose score](../design/visual-score.html) |
| D-143 | **Sharing, second part: the invite template, a date for the expiry, the note's Share popover, and an anchor on every sharing surface (WP-5.3, proposed).** (1) **The invite template (SHR-09):** Settings → Sharing has an *Invite message* card (Default or Edited) whose [Edit] opens a dialog: the message in a multi-line field, the six placeholders as buttons that add one at the end (`{name}`, `{title}`, `{link}`, `{passcode}`, `{expires}`, `{site}`), a live preview with made-up values (Asha, "Trip plans", `harbor-maple-river-cloud`, the site's own `s/` page and `#example`; never a real audience's secrets), [Reset to default], [Cancel] and [Save]. `templateProblem` (`packages/seal/src/access.ts`) refuses an empty message, more than 2,000 characters or 20 lines, hidden characters (format and control characters other than line breaks and tabs), any `{word}` that isn't one of the six (named, so `{pascode}` says "“{pascode}” isn't a placeholder"), and a message with neither `{link}` nor `{passcode}`. Saving goes to the main process (`access:template`), which checks it again and writes `private/access-message.json` (`{ "template": … }`, `schemas/access-message.schema.json`) atomically, committed on the cadence like `access.json`; saving the default text, or Reset then Save, removes the file, so the app's own wording (and its shared-computer line, D-134 (5)) applies again. A damaged file is ignored, never quoted. The template holds no secrets: the window sends it with Copy invite as before, and the main process now also refuses a template that fails `templateProblem`, then fills in the link, passcode and site itself (D-134 (6), L1). Lines whose placeholder has no value are still left out. (2) **A date for the expiry:** under *Works for* (1, 7, 30, 90 days, Always), *Or until* is a date picker (`DatePicker`, a new primitive on react-aria-components' `DatePicker`, with `@internationalized/date`, Apache-2.0, already installed beside it): typed segment by segment or picked on a calendar, from today on. A day picked lets go of the presets, and the audience works until the end of that day in the owner's time zone (`expiryUntil`); a day in the past is refused before the shell is asked. *Expires in N days* now counts calendar days, so a picked day reads as that day. The five presets fit side by side in a 480 × 360 window (measured in the journey), so they stay a segmented control. (3) **The Share popover (IA-VIEW-SHARE):** ⋯ → *Share and visibility…* (and the macOS File menu) opens *Share “⟨note⟩”* for the note that's open, instead of Settings → Sharing (D-139 (6)): who can read it (every audience in force whose scope names the note or a folder above it, "via trips/"), each as a card with [Copy invite] (copied by the main process, "Copied" and a live-region line), *Only you can read it* when nobody else can, *Anyone can read it* with [Copy public link] for a public note, [Share this note with…] (the editor with this note picked; its invite and its Cancel come back to the popover) and [All people with access] (Settings → Sharing). As built it's a compact modal dialog (size s, as tall as its content), not a frosted popover anchored under the trigger: the ⋯ menu closes as it opens, and no layout anchors to the trigger yet. The three-way *Who can read* switch (moving a note between Private and Public) and the per-row ⋯ menu stay in Settings and the Files actions. (4) **Anchors (D-127):** the editor opens with a card for who it's for (their initials once a name is typed, a share icon before), what it opens, how, and for how long, updated as the fields change; the confirmation shows the audience's card; the template dialog's preview has a mail card; the popover's rows are audience cards. Text has a `personal` flag: an invite message (its preview, the invite's message) is the person's own words, so the visual score counts neither its words nor its line length. Measured in `m5-sharing.spec.ts`, light and dark, all with the anchor at 20: Settings → Sharing 20 economy (39 words), the editor 15 (74 words), the invite 20, the template 20, the popover 20, the confirmation 20. (5) **TextField** gains `multiline` (about six lines, `size.textArea`, growing with its content). (6) **After the security review of 8a6627c (2026-10-15, no critical or high findings):** the Share dialog decides who can read the note with the publisher's own `inScope` (`@focus-notes/seal/grants`), so a grant naming the note by its front-matter `id` (read with `noteId`, as the publisher does) or a folder written `/trips` is listed, and `all` counts only on the owner's grant (medium: it could say *Only you can read it* when someone could); for Copy invite the main process takes the name from the grant itself and refuses a title or date from the window with a line break or a hidden character; hidden characters now include a lone carriage return and U+2028 and U+2029, and a template's CRLF counts as one line break before the checks; the expiry is worked out once, after the checks, and a picked day that has passed stops with *Choose how long it works* instead of falling back to 30 days; a day can be at most a year away (the date picker's last day, and `expiry.far` in the main process's `applyAccessChange`; the publisher doesn't check expiries, so a hand-edited `access.json` can still go further). A note title with a hidden character (a zero-width joiner in an emoji) now makes Copy invite fail rather than send it. **Open:** the anchored frosted popover and its *Who can read* switch, Spotlight's `> Share` entry, and a design-system review of the scores above. | Proposed (builder); the note's Share dialog, audience cards and Add people superseded by D-284 and D-285 | [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md#the-share-popover), [Repository and publishing → Sealing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#sealing-and-share-links) |

(2) **The app's name:** Welcome shows "Focus Notes" under the hero icon as a wordmark (`role: title`, `tone: muted`, `space.s` from the icon), clear but quieter than the display title. (3) **Ready features focus mode,** the app's signature: a hint row with the focus icon, "Focus mode keeps only your current line bright.", and the toggle's keys as key caps (`Shortcut`), read from the keymap (`focus.toggle`'s registered shortcut, so macOS shows ⇧⌘D and Windows and Linux Ctrl Shift D) and read aloud in words. The owner's wording ("the line you're writing") was shortened so the keys stay on the hint's line at the default window. | D-145 | **Agent permissions and attribution as built (WP-7.1, AGT-03, AGT-04, AGT-09, proposed).** (1) **One check:** `checkAgent(permissions, operation)` in `@focus-notes/core/agents`, pure, called by every MCP tool (WP-7.2) and command (WP-7.3) on every call with the permissions read again, so a switch turned off applies to the next call. It refuses anything it can't prove is allowed, and deleting, visibility, sharing, and unknown operations always. Allowed is never final: it returns an *unresolved* grant that only `resolveAgentPath` (`@focus-notes/core/agents-fs`) turns into files to read or write. (2) **One set of permissions for every agent,** not one per agent: the spec has one Settings → Agents page, and client names are self-reported (Agents → Risks), so a grant per agent would protect nothing. (3) **Stored per computer** in `agents.json` in the app's data folder, with `schemas/agents.schema.json`; never synced, so a grant on one computer never applies on another; removed on signing out and removing the workspace. Missing or damaged means the defaults (nothing until *Agents can connect*); only `true` turns a switch on; a damaged folder list allows no folder, never all. (4) **Meaning, where the spec was open:** editing, moving, and renaming a note need *Create and edit* **and** reading its root (no blind writes to private notes); creating a note or folder needs *Create and edit* and reading its root (so private creates need *Read private*), and in `notes/` also *Create public notes*, without which a public folder is refused rather than redirected; *Limit to folders* applies to every path an operation names, by whole folder names; a move between Public and Private is a visibility change and never allowed; roots match exactly as written, and paths that could name another file (`..`, drives, `\`, `:`, trailing dots or spaces, device names, `.git`) are refused. The person at the terminal (the command-line tool) can read and edit both roots whatever the switches say, never more. (5) **Safety:** 60 reads and 20 writes in any 60 seconds, refused calls not counted; 1 MB of UTF-8 per body; note text returned as structured untrusted data and between markers with a random name per response, said before them; titles, file names, and snippets are wrapped the same way (WP-7.2). (6) **Attribution:** each agent write is its own commit after the writer's own pending changes to that note are committed; `Agent edit: <note path>` (repository-relative, as in D-97) with the `Focus-Notes-Agent: <client>` trailer; the author stays the writer's Git identity (hooks and signing as usual), and the client name is one plain line of at most 64 characters; a write that fails partway is still committed as the agent's. (7) **Undo** puts the commit's notes back to its parent's version as a new commit, `Undo agent edit: <note path>`, only for an agent commit in the current branch's history, named by its full ID, and refuses (*changed*) when any of its notes changed since, rather than merging. (8) **Where the rest lands:** the toast with [Review] and [Undo] and the in-place reload come with the agent write path (WP-7.2); the switches and Recent agent changes with Settings → Agents (WP-7.4); the *Agent* badge with Note history (WP-8.4). (9) **After the security review of `e2be409`:** (a) **sharing:** in `private/notes/`, a move or rename must keep exactly the audiences that open the note (from `access.json`, every audience not withdrawn, expired or not) and neither end may be named by a share; nothing is created inside a shared folder or at a named path; without readable shares nothing private is moved or created; (b) **on disk:** `resolveAgentPath` requires each root to be its own real folder, refuses any link, junction, or short name on the path (its real path must be itself), checks the root and folder limits again on the real path, and refuses files with a second hard link, for reading too; `readAgentFile` and `writeAgentFile` compare the opened file with what was resolved; (c) **terminal:** only the command-line tool's entry point claims it, once per process, as an object nothing else can make, and the MCP server seals it at start; the tool's writes are attributed commits named `command line`, and Settings says the tool acts as you; (d) **rate limit:** per computer, a token bucket per kind in `agent-limits.json` under `agent-limits.lock`, on the shared monotonic clock, refusing when the lock can't be had; (e) **writes** compare the version the agent read, so an editor save in between is never overwritten; (f) **Undo** also requires this computer's Git identity as the author and exactly the note plus the moves the message describes, under the notes folder; (g) paths and change lines in messages are one line; client names lose `\p{Cf}` and show as "name (self-reported)"; (h) `agents.json` is written atomically, user-only where there are modes, with a check: changed outside the app means the defaults and a notice in Settings; it isn't a boundary against programs running as the same user; (i) the schema's folder pattern accepts exactly what `agentPath` does. (10) **After the re-review of `90114a7`:** (a) **ids:** a share can name a note by its front-matter `id`, so the sharing check counts ids, and an agent may never add, change, or remove an `id` line (edits keep them exactly; new notes bring none; the app gives ids); (b) **case:** share paths compare without regard to case (and in NFC) on every system, refusing more on case-sensitive ones, and `resolveAgentPath` runs the share check again on the real on-disk spelling; (c) **binding:** a grant records its operation and the shares it was checked against: `writeAgentFile` creates only under a `create` grant and overwrites only under an `update` grant, and `moveAgentFile` and `createAgentFolder` take `move` and `createFolder` grants, so WP-7.2 never writes or renames outside a checked path; (d) **on disk:** missing folders are made one level at a time, each checked as it's made; writes go to a temporary file and take the note's place in one rename after a last version check; creates and moves use a hard link, which fails rather than replace anything; (e) **accepted residual risk:** an agent that can run programs (a shell tool) can run the command-line tool, which acts as the person and ignores the switches; that's the same reach it already has over the files, so the switches protect only agents limited to the MCP server; Settings → Agents says the tool acts as you. (11) **After the re-review of `a237ee4`:** (a) every part of a path that exists must be spelled exactly as on disk, so on NTFS and APFS an agent can't open `Plan.md` as `plan.md` and change its case, or which path-named shares open it; (b) a move whose old name can't be removed (held open, no permission) removes the new one again, so a note is never in two places; (c) `cleanAgentTemporaries` removes only this module's own temporary files, older than 5 minutes, inside the two note roots, never through a link or junction or in `.git`, when the MCP server and the command-line tool start; (d) creating and moving use a hard link so they never replace anything, which means they fail, safely and with nothing changed, on file systems without hard links: FAT32, exFAT, and some network and synced folders. Editing works there. | Proposed | [Agents and automation](../architecture/agents.md#permissions), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Work graph](work-graph.json) |
| D-146 | **Review captures at every window size come from CI, and a menu owns its Home and End (DEF-9, proposed).** (1) **Review captures:** the **Review captures** workflow (`.github/workflows/review-captures.yml`, Windows) runs a catalogue journey (`tools/review/catalogs.mjs`: `fre-catalog`, `vq-surfaces`) with `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_ALLOW_SCREEN=1` at the default, smallest (480 × 360) and large (1600 × 1000) windows, light and dark, and uploads its images, `index.html` and manifest as the artifact `review-<catalogue>`. It runs by hand (a catalogue, a repeat count and a filter to choose) and on every push to a `vq-*` branch; `node tools/review/fetch-captures.mjs --branch <branch>` downloads the latest into `fre-review\<label>\` beside the repository. `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_ALLOW_SCREEN` is CI-only: `tools/review/capture.mjs` drops it off CI, so a local capture takes the default window only (D-141). A new catalogue is an entry in `catalogs.mjs` and a choice in the workflow. (2) **DEF-9's root cause:** React Aria (3.52) moves focus on Home and End in a menu but leaves the keys' default action on (its handler returns false once it has moved focus), so the browser also ran its own smooth Home or End scroll of the menu. Pressed in quick succession (as the visual quality check does), the End animation was retargeted to the top with its speed kept: the menu swung 18 to 32 px past its top and back over about 150 ms, and a check that sampled it there saw the first item, Restore, cut. Reproduced at the default window by capping the menu's height, at zooms 1 to 1.75 and fractional offsets: 17 of 40 runs swung before, none after. The clip was real while it lasted; the measurement (border box plus `clientTop`) was not at fault, and the menu's border and padding play no part. (3) **The fix is the menu primitive's:** every menu (`CommandMenu`, its submenus) prevents the default action of Home and End on its own element (`behaviors/menu-keys.ts`), so only React Aria's instant scroll moves it; the visual quality check now also fails any list whose Home or End leaves the browser's default on. (4) The small-window run of `vq-surfaces` opens a real 480 × 360 window, so it runs on CI or with `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_ALLOW_SCREEN=1` only, as `m2-window` does. | `AGENTS.md`, `.github/workflows/review-captures.yml`, `tools/review/`, `packages/ui/src/behaviors/menu-keys.ts`, `apps/desktop/e2e/visual-quality.ts`, `apps/desktop/e2e/vq-surfaces.spec.ts` |
| D-147 | **The window always drags by its title bar band, and a template is never your notes (the owner's live feedback, proposed).** (1) **Dragging:** the owner couldn't move the window on *We found your notes*: the band was part of the trigger's layer, which setup hides. The band is now the window's own, in `AppRoot` (the top `layout.titleBar`, under the layers that come after it, so the trigger and the window controls stay above it), there in every state: every setup step, its problems, the app locked while setup runs. A dialog (`DialogPanel`: Settings, Spotlight, sharing) carries the band over its scrim inside its modal, so the rest of the app going inert doesn't take it, and a press there moves the window and leaves the dialog open. While a menu is open the rest of the app is inert too, so `WindowFrame`'s hit test also takes a press at the band's place when no control is there (`[data-no-drag]`, buttons, fields, menus, the window controls). Controls in the band are never dragged. The first-run catalogue journey checks the band in every state it reaches, and starts a move (start and end at one point, so the window never moves) on *We found your notes* and in Settings. (2) **Templates:** *We found your notes* listed `prabinpebam/focus-notes-workspace`, the template, which has a workspace file. The account's repositories now drop any with GitHub's `is_template`, and the source template by name (any letter case), before the workspace check, for *We found your notes* and *Open from GitHub* alike (`notesRepos`). | Proposed | [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html) |
| D-148 | **No blank, white or unstyled frame, ever: windows and views change in place (the owner saw the whole window flash white, proposed).** (1) **Cause:** finishing setup, opening another workspace and removing the workspace call `webContents.reload()`, and a transparent window shows the unpainted page as white or see-through until it paints. (2) **The window shows its first finished frame:** created hidden, its page's first paint already the resolved theme's canvas (the theme passed with the page's address, the boot styles from `packages/ui`), shown when the renderer reports its first frame with the layout drawn (`fn:window.firstFrame`), not on DOM ready. (3) **No page reloads for state changes:** the main process sends the new workspace or state over the bridge; the renderer rebuilds its stores and views off screen and swaps them in one frame. A reload is allowed only after a renderer crash, behind a captured image of the last frame; a repository check fails on any other `reload(` in `apps/desktop/src/main`. (4) **Views swap complete** (double-buffering): nothing unmounts to a blank area while its replacement loads; setup steps don't remount the whole tree. (5) **Theme changes all at once** (a whole-window cross-fade over `motion.theme` between finished frames is allowed; no element transitions its own colour). (6) **Window operations never show unpainted area:** bounds change in one call; the canvas covers the window inside the margin; layout follows within a frame. (7) **Checked** by a frame-integrity journey through the DevTools screencast of an inactive window (safe on the owner's computer), failing on any frame with 2% or more of the window white, see-through or outside the theme's palette, and on layout shift after the first frame; the screen-level variants run on CI only (D-141). Built as WP-5.8 (DSK-09). (8) **Built, phase 1:** the `workspace:switched` message replaces both reloads (finishing setup and opening another workspace through `switchWorkspace`, and removing the workspace); the renderer gives each app instance its own view of the bridge, starts a new instance with the old view still on screen, then swaps them in one task (`swapApp` in `packages/ui-runtime`: the old root unmounted and the new one rendered with `flushSync`, so no frame is painted between them), and the old instance's listeners and calls end (its bridge is revoked, its search worker stopped). The main process passes `theme` and `scheme` (from state.json and the system's scheme) in the page's address; `bootTheme` in `packages/ui` applies them, with the drawn window's shape, before anything renders; the page sends `window:firstFrame` (mark `fn:window.firstFrame`) once the app's layout is drawn in that theme, and the window shows then, or after 4 s at the latest (`window-shown on=first-frame|timeout` in the log). The native background starts as Paper's or Ink's canvas and follows the theme's own after that. Setup's remounting step switches (D-131) showed no blank frame, so they're unchanged. Checked by `apps/desktop/test/no-reload.test.ts` (any `reload(` in `apps/desktop/src/main` fails unless marked `allow-reload: renderer crash`; there's none yet) and `e2e/dsk09-frames.spec.ts` (frames through the screencast, from the built tokens' Paper and Ink palettes). Before: three all-white frames (Open → Ready, opening another workspace, removing it) and three page reloads; after: none. Phase 1 left the crash-recovery reload, layout shift, reduced motion and the screen-level variants (CI), and named waits (PRF-13, phase 2). (9) **Built, phase 1 leftovers (`wp-5.8c`):** **renderer-crash recovery**: on `render-process-gone` (not a clean exit) a cover (a native `View` in the theme's canvas, the last the page reported, with the window's corner radius) goes over the drawn window inside the shadow margin, what the journal holds goes back into its notes (QLT-03), and the page loads again with the theme showing now in its address (not `reload()`, whose address would carry the launch's theme; the line is still marked `allow-reload: renderer crash`); the cover goes on the new page's `window:firstFrame`, or after 10 s; after 3 crashes in a minute the page isn't loaded again and the cover stays (`window/crash-cover.ts`; `renderer-gone … cover=#…` and `renderer-recovered on=first-frame|timeout` in the log). A captured image of the last frame was set aside: `capturePage` is impossible after the crash, and a running snapshot would cost idle CPU (PRF-04). **The native background** starts as the chosen built-in theme's own canvas (`themeCanvas` in `@focus-notes/core/window-geometry`, from the built tokens), not just Paper's or Ink's; a custom theme's colours are kept only while the app runs (D-108), so its canvas follows from the page as before. **Checked** by `e2e/dsk09-frames.spec.ts`: signing out (the light journey); a layout-shift check (PerformanceObserver `layout-shift` in the page, after `fn:window.firstFrame`, failing on any shift over 0.01 no input caused), which found real shifts, fixed rather than quarantined ([gate evidence](gate-evidence.md)): the setup column is now placed as a block of `layout.onboardingBlock` would be centred, its content at the top (`Center` `block: anchored`), so a step's content changing height never moves what's shown; a centred confirmation keeps its top where it opened (`DialogPanel`, `height: content`); and setup's wait line, percentage and parts sit below the step's content; the dark journey again with reduced motion (every animation or transition that runs for any time fails, and so does a frame whose main colour is none of a theme's large surfaces, alone or under the modal scrim once or twice (computed from `color.scrim`), a frame caught mid-transition (`apps/desktop/test/frames.test.ts`: the scrimmed dark canvas passes, a Paper-to-Ink cross-fade is caught at its midpoint and in at least 14 of its 17 steps from 10% to 90%): 18 frames for the 10 theme changes against 126 with motion); and a forced renderer crash (`forcefullyCrashRenderer`), whose new page's frames are taken in the main process (`beginFrameSubscription`, the crashed page's DevTools target being gone): those painted under the cover may be unfinished, every one after it must pass. Still to come: the screen-level variants (CI). | Proposed | [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md#glitch-free-windows-and-views), [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html), [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#the-workspace-folder), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (DSK-09) |
| D-149 | **Perceived performance: four classes of wait, a pattern for each, and an inventory of every wait (adapted from the owner's personal-site framework, proposed).** The owner found many steps that take time with the screen frozen and no feedback. (1) **Classes** after Nielsen's limits and the Doherty threshold: instant (under 100 ms, no indicator), responsive (100 ms to 1 s, an inline state past `wait.signDelay`), waiting (1 to 10 s, the work named at its place, the window usable), long (over 10 s or several steps: the current step in words, determinate progress where known, elapsed time, Cancel where the work can stop). (2) **Patterns:** direct echo, save state, optimistic change, stale while updating, double-buffered swap, skeleton, named wait, steps, background task, prefetch on intent, first-start notice; each with its measure. (3) **The wait inventory** lists every operation that can take longer than 100 ms with its class, pattern, place, words, measure and status; a new one is added in the pull request that creates it. (4) **Setup first:** one boolean `busy` per step is replaced by named waits and steps (getting the code, reading the account, finding your notes with skeleton rows and a count, prefetched at Connect; creating with its parts always shown, not under *Show details*; downloading with Git's percentage; opening a folder). (5) **Long bridge calls stream progress** (`onReport`, `onProgress`; Git with `--progress`). (6) **Checked** by a "no silent wait" journey that slows each inventory operation through the test bridge and checks its sign and words. Built as WP-5.8 (PRF-13). **Phase 2, setup (built):** the first run's waits are named and go through the tracker with fixed targets (`FIRST_RUN_WAITS`, allow-listed for the report): "Connecting to your account…" with the profile card's skeleton, "Getting a code from GitHub…" then the approval wait with its seconds and [Cancel], "Looking for your notes on GitHub… N of M" with three skeleton rows (asked as soon as Connect has the account; the 30 most recent repositories checked six at a time and counted), "Downloading your notes · 45%" from `git clone --progress` with its parts (download, private notes, linking: n of 3), "Opening your notes…", and creating's current part ("Making your notes on GitHub · 2 of 9 · 14 s") over its parts, always shown. The parts' names on the main path are plain words (D-124): *Make your notes on GitHub*, *Make a place for private notes*, *Let your website read them*, *Save the browser key*, *Turn on your website*. A journey-only slow-down (`globalThis.focusNotesSlow` in the main process, before any first-run call or setup part; inert unless FOCUS_NOTES_E2E and never packaged) drives the "no silent wait" journey. **Round 2 (built):** *Download to this computer* streams Git's percentage through `onReport` (a step's `percent`, not kept in the progress file) and shows a `ProgressBar` (a new primitive: the platform's progress element, styled with `cmp.progressBar.*`); Open shows its parts (Download to this computer · Get the private notes · Link them) ticking off, with the percentage on a bar; Settings → Workspace asks for Your notes on intent, used by Open another workspace from GitHub… within 30 s; Sign out and Remove say "Signing out…" and "Removing…" on their buttons with the seconds, through the tracker. Waits that only read (checking Git, the account, finding the notes) can be left with Back or Esc, and a call that ends after the person left, or after a newer wait began (a cancelled sign-in), never clears the newer one. | Proposed | [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md), [Feedback and dialogs → Waits](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#waits), [First run](../experience/first-run.md#your-notes), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (PRF-13) |
| D-150 | **A window that let clicks through its margin always takes the mouse back (the owner's report from the Preview build: clicking the window selected the app behind it).** (1) **Cause:** the margin passes clicks through with `setIgnoreMouseEvents(true, { forward: true })`, and the window took the mouse back only when the page reported the pointer, which it did only near the edges. A pointer that left through the margin and then reached the window's middle without crossing the edges (Alt+Tab, a window uncovering it, the window moving or appearing under a still pointer, a taskbar activation) was never reported, so the whole window stayed click-through. (2) **The fix:** the shell tells the page when the window lets clicks through (`window:passthrough`); meanwhile the page reports every move Windows forwards (at most one a frame), not only near the edges, and outside it only near the edges as before (the decision is `pointer-report.ts`, pure and unit-tested). (3) **Re-checks:** the window takes the mouse unless the pointer is in its margin right then (`CustomWindow.settle`) whenever the system shows, restores, activates, moves, or resizes it, after the app's own maximize, snap, vertical maximize, full screen, restore, drag, keyboard Move and Size, and when the displays change. Nothing polls (PRF-04); Linux keeps its 50 ms watch only while the pointer is in the margin (D-104). (4) **Every mode:** a unit test holds that no corner or middle of the visible window counts as margin, normal, maximized, snapped, vertically maximized (D-141), or full screen. | Proposed | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html), D-104, D-141 |
| D-151 | **Snappy: the window follows the hand, and every control answers in the next frame (the owner found fast drags trailing like a rubber band and the window floating into place, proposed).** (1) **Cause:** the app-built move relays each pointer move from the page (one per animation frame, over IPC) to the main process, so the window is at least two frames behind; when moving the transparent window takes longer than a frame, moves queue in the main process and keep playing after the release; the snap preview is shown again on every move. (2) **The main process owns the drag:** one message starts it, then the main process reads the cursor itself once per display frame (only while dragging; PRF-04 allows no timers at rest) and applies only the latest position. The alternative, the system's own move loop on Windows (a native move from the band, with snapping from `will-move` and `moved`), is measured against it, and the faster one is kept and recorded here. (3) **Moving never repaints the page;** the preview is shown only when its zone changes. (4) **Resizing** uses the same loop, with nothing but layout on its path. (5) **The main process never stalls** (`fn:main.loopLag` p99 ≤ 16 ms): no synchronous file, Git or crypto work. (6) **Controls answer on pointer-down,** within a frame (INP p95 ≤ 50 ms), the result in place before any motion, and first uses warmed during start-up. (7) **Checked** with a real pointer on CI (screen level, D-141: drag lag, settle, resize frames) and in the inactive-window journeys (loop lag, paint counts, INP); every measure in `pnpm bench` with budgets. Built as WP-5.9 (PRF-14), first, after D-147 and D-150 merge (the same window code). (8) **As built (WP-5.9, first part).** *Measured before:* in the journey that makes the window's move take 25 ms (longer than a frame) and drags at 3,000 px/s, all 60 page moves were applied, 51 of them after the release (about 1.3 s of the window floating into place); the page recalculated style 18 to 35 times and laid out once during a move; the snap preview was shown again on every move (11 shows for 11 moves in one zone). *Measured after:* 11 positions applied for the same 60 moves, 1 after the release (the release's own) and 0 after the end; during a move 0 layouts, 0 style recalculations and 0 DOM changes; one preview show and one raise per zone. The main process's own work per frame stays about 0.004 ms at p99 (it was never the cost: moving the transparent window and the relay were). *Chosen:* **the main process owns the drag, paced by both clocks it has.** Its loop (`drag-loop.ts`, at the display's `displayFrequency`, only while a drag lasts, stopped by the end, a lost pointer capture, hiding, minimizing, going inactive or closing; PRF-04's test checks it) reads `screen.getCursorScreenPoint()` and applies only a new position. On Windows the main process's timers fire only every 15.6 ms (measured: 15 ms apart on a 240 Hz display, where a frame is 4.2 ms), so each move the page sees, at most one per page frame (measured 4 ms apart at 240 Hz), is also a signal to read the cursor *now*; it carries no position the main process uses, so a backlog of them never replays a stale one. Snapping, restoring under the pointer, vertical maximize, keyboard Move and Size and D-150's settle are unchanged. Resizing uses the same loop. Journeys (`FOCUS_NOTES_E2E=1`) follow the page's pointer, applied only on the loop; the screen-level journey switches to the real cursor (`fn:window.dragLag`, `fn:window.settle`, CI only, Windows). *The system's move loop (`-webkit-app-region: drag`), evaluated and not chosen:* it would follow the cursor natively, but the page gets no pointer events on a drag region (the band's hover reveal, DD-DSK-04, and double-click and right-click would go to the system, whose maximize and menu are wrong for a transparent window, D-14, DSK-08), restoring a maximized window under the pointer would need its bounds changed inside the system's modal loop (a jump), Windows' own snapping would compete with the app's, and journeys couldn't drive it. Revisit only if CI's `fn:window.dragLag` misses its budget with this loop. *`fn:main.loopLag`:* in `pnpm bench` as `PRF-14.mainLoopLagP99Ms` and `PRF-14.mainLoopLagMaxMs`, while notes open, are typed in and saved. It measured 21.5 ms at p99 and 76 ms at most on the owner's computer, over (5)'s 16 ms and 50 ms (part of the p99 is Windows' 15.6 ms timer, which the histogram's own timer also waits for; the maximum is a real stall). So it reports only for now: its budget joins `budgets.json`, where Windows CI enforces it, once the stalls are found and removed, which is the next part of WP-5.9, with the measure during setup, backups, the 10,000-note scan and a drag. (9) **Measured on CI with a real cursor (run 38795218104, Windows runner: software compositing, 1024 × 768 at 64 Hz, the window layered per D-153).** The 556 px that pulled the first candidate was the journey, not the loop: it took the press's offset from where it meant to park the cursor, but the cursor driver (PowerShell compiling its `SetCursorPos` call) parked it only later, so every sample carried a constant offset and the release never matched. The journey now parks the cursor, reads the press where the cursor really is, and only then starts the sweep, and it runs the same drag on three paths, chosen by a journey-only switch (`focusNotesDragPointer`, never read outside `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E` nor in a packaged app). *Before D-151* (each page move applied as it arrives): p95 409 px, max 494 px; 45 positions in 1.27 s, the window standing still for up to 200 ms at a time while the relay fell behind. *D-151* (the cursor read at each page frame and each tick): p95 **38 px** (budget 46.9 px at 64 Hz), p50 12 px, max 155 px, 132 positions, settled 6 ms after the release. *Timer only* (the page sees no moves): p95 48 px, 71 positions, paced by Windows' 15.6 ms timer, which is why the page's frames pace the app's drag. The cursor driver ran at 2,830 to 2,910 px/s, and `setPosition` on the layered transparent window took 0.3 to 0.5 ms (p95 under 0.7 ms): neither the runner's compositing nor moving the window is the cost, so PRF-14's budget holds on software compositing as written and isn't split. Only the D-151 path is gated; the other two report, to compare. `fn:main.loopLag` on the same run's Windows runner: p99 13.1 ms, max 44 ms, within (5)'s budget there, against 21.5 ms and 76 ms on the owner's computer while in use; it stays reported only until the reference computer meets it too. (10) **Where the main process stalled (WP-5.9, third part), measured with `tools/bench/main-profile.mjs`** (a CPU profile of the main process and its loop delay, phase by phase, on the 10,000-note workspace: the scan after start, opening notes, typing and saving, a backup; on the owner's computer). *Before:* 8 stretches of JavaScript over 10 ms after the window appeared, all in the first 1.3 s while the start-up Git work and the account check ran: starting each Git process (`spawn`, 9 to 27 ms each on Windows, where creating a process blocks the thread that creates it) and looking for `gh` and Git's credential manager on PATH (`statSync` on every folder and extension, 40 ms at once); the scan's loop delay reached 61 ms. *Fixed:* Git starts on a worker thread of its own (`git/run.ts`: the main process posts the call and gets the result; where a worker can't start, Git starts on the main thread as before), and programs on PATH are looked for with every place checked at once, off the main thread. *After:* 1 stretch over 10 ms (12 ms, loading the HTTP client for the first request, once). Left on the main thread: creating the window itself (Chromium's view, 160 ms, before it shows; Electron gives no other thread for it). Opening notes, typing, saving and the backup: loop delay p99 15.9 ms and max 16.3 to 17.1 ms both before and after; on Windows a p99 of about 16 ms is the timer's own 15.6 ms step, not a stall, so the histogram can't show a p99 under it. *Proposed gate for `fn:main.loopLag`:* measured from the first frame on, max ≤ 50 ms (a real stall), and p99 ≤ 16 ms where the platform's timer allows it, reported but not gated on Windows until the measure is taken from the profile's stretches instead of the timer. The drag journey now reports the main process's loop delay during the drag, to tell a stall from the loop. *On CI with Git off the main thread (run 38809514812):* the D-151 path p95 39 px, max 180 px, settled in 4 ms, with the main process's loop delay during the drag p99 4.0 ms and max 7.1 ms; before D-151 p95 103 px, max 157 px; timer only p95 49 px, max 176 px. The 415 px of the second run came with Git starting on the main thread while the app finished starting; now the largest gap is about the same on all three paths, whatever paces the window, and the main process never stalls more than 7 ms meanwhile, so it's most likely the cursor driver itself pausing (a 60 ms pause at 3,000 px/s moves the cursor 180 px in one step), not the app. (11) **`fn:window.resizeFrame`** (journey, inactive window, the edge handle driven by the journey's pointer; from the main process's bounds call to the page's first frame at that width, with Windows' occlusion tracking off so a covered journey window still draws): run alone, p95 8 to 10 ms and max 10 ms over 38 sizes; after other journeys, the first one or two sizes take 26 to 138 ms and p95 is 29 to 33 ms, in long animation frames with no script in them (so not the page's layout or our code; most likely the layered window's surface being made again for a new size, D-153), and every later size stays under 16 ms. Reported, not gated, until that first resize is explained; it's the next item. (12) **Input to next paint** (journey, the Event Timing API; the worst input of each use of the toolbar, Files, Spotlight and Settings, five rounds, with the warm-up after start on as people have it): p95 40 ms, max 40 ms, no long task, on the owner's computer; one run among other journeys, with the computer in use, read 200 ms. On CI (run 38811175153, software compositing): toolbar 16 to 32 ms, Files 24 to 40, Settings 24 to 32, but **Spotlight 56 to 88 ms**, over the 50 ms budget, with no long task (so the time is in rendering its panel, not in script); `fn:window.resizeFrame` there p95 30 ms, max 46 ms. Both report, not gate, until Spotlight's opening frame and the resize frame on software compositing are brought under budget, the next items of WP-5.9. With the warm-up off (as other journeys run), the toolbar's first use took 184 ms, so the warm-up is what keeps a first use fast. First use against fifth: toolbar 40 against 16 ms, Spotlight 40 against 24, Files and Settings 24 against 16; Event Timing reports in 8 ms steps, so the 20% rule can't be read at these sizes and isn't gated yet. (13) **Spotlight in the next frame, and the resize budget without a GPU (WP-5.9, PRF-14, D-121).** *Where Spotlight's time went* (journey traces, software compositing, `FOCUS_NOTES_DISABLE_GPU=1` reproduces it on any computer): its panel opened on every action the app has, 183 rows and 1,253 elements made, styled and laid out before the first frame (a forced layout of 1,575 objects in its opening, 11 ms on the owner's computer); its frosted panel drew a backdrop blur, a surface of its own every frame (a frame took 15 ms to draw instead of 7); and its fade and scale in redrew the whole window on the processor for about 27 frames, just as the first keys were typed, so typing waited behind them. *Fixed:* a long result list (`ResultList`) draws its first 24 rows in the first frame and the rest once that frame is drawn, never short of the active row; and on software compositing a token mode (`[data-compositing="software"]`, `modes/software-compositing.json`) makes the frosted surfaces opaque and unblurred and dialogs and menus appear and leave at once (`motion.enter`, `motion.exit` 0), and no element draws a backdrop filter (a blur of nothing still cost a surface). The token mode is a design choice for computers without a GPU, under D-121: approved in principle by the orchestrator on 16 October, **for the owner to confirm** (what changes there: frosted surfaces are opaque, and dialogs and menus have no open or close animation). *CI (software compositing, input to next paint, the worst input of each use, five rounds):* before (run 38813688003) Spotlight 56 to 80 ms (88 in run 38811175153), p95 80 ms; after (run 38823306148) Spotlight 32 to 40, toolbar 16 to 32, Files 16 to 32, Settings 24 to 32, p95 40 ms, no long task; now **gated at p95 ≤ 50 ms**, and the journey names each use's worst input with its wait, handlers and time to paint, and traces one opening. *`fn:window.resizeFrame` on software compositing:* traced on CI, every size is drawn (about 5 ms a frame, 8 at most) and rastered (about 6 ms a size, the whole window, as the window's width moves its content) by the processor, with the page's own work under 3 ms a size and no script; so a size takes one to three frames at CI's 64 Hz: p95 28 to 45 ms and max 46 ms over four runs (38811175153, 38813688003, 38821567259, 38823306148), p50 21 to 27 ms over the last three. Nothing of ours is left on that path but the window's area, so on software compositing the journey **gates a measured budget, p50 ≤ 35 ms and p95 ≤ 60 ms**, as a regression check; the 16 ms budget stands where the GPU composites, reported there until the first resizes after other journeys ((11)) are explained. (14) **The bench never ended on CI after (5) (QLT-05).** `pnpm bench --check` printed its whole report in about 5 minutes and then never exited (run 38813688003: report at 22:07, cancelled at 23:18; the same on candidate O, run 38823580855, and on wp-5.9d). *Cause:* the Git worker of (5) was unref'd before its `message` listener was added, and adding the listener refs the worker's port again, so any Node process that ran Git through it (the bench's PRF-07 step) was held open for good; the app itself quits explicitly, so it never showed there. Unref'd for good instead, a Node process would end with a command still running. *Fixed:* the worker is ref'd from a post until its last answer and unref'd when idle, as a Git process started on the thread would be (`git/run.ts`; `git-run-lifetime.test.ts` runs Git in a Node process that must end by itself, and fails on the old code after 20 s). *And a hang now fails fast:* every bench step has a hard limit (10 minutes, `FOCUS_NOTES_BENCH_STEP_TIMEOUT_MS`), logs its name and time, and on the limit fails naming the step, the handles still open and its child processes, which it ends; once the report is out the bench must end within 15 s or fail the same way. Locally the bench now ends by itself in about 4 minutes. *Still to build:* (6) and the rest of (7) (INP for every control, first use against fifth), `fn:window.resizeFrame`, and the focus band following a resize by CSS rather than script. **(14) DEF-23, how `fn:window.settle` is timed:** candidate run 38861478942 measured 22 ms against a 16 ms frame on a 64 Hz runner (earlier runs 4 to 8 ms). The product's path was already the one D-151 asks for: `endDrag` stops the loop, reads the cursor, and applies its position in the same call as the page's release arrives (`custom-window.test.ts` now checks that no tick runs in between). The 22 ms was the journey's own timing: it started in Playwright's process before dispatching the release, so it included a round trip to the page and another to the main process, and it then polled the window with `setTimeout(1)`, which waits for Windows' 15.6 ms timer. The journey now times the settle in the app's timeline: from the release dispatched in the page (`performance.timeOrigin + performance.now()`) to the main process having handled the drag's end with the window's point under the cursor (a listener added after the app's own), and falls back to watching the window only if the end didn't put it there, which is then the regression it reports. The budget stays one frame (`Math.ceil(frame)`), unchanged. (15) The control-response journey measures a warm runner: a throwaway launch and round of first uses come first, logged as cold and held to a loose 300 ms ceiling (D-178). | Proposed | [Perceived performance → Snappy](../architecture/perceived-performance.md#snappy-the-window-and-every-control-follow-the-hand), [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html#window-management-on-windows-and-linux), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (PRF-14) |
| D-152 | **Intermittent Windows CI failures are fixed at their cause, not waived (DEF-13 to DEF-27, proposed).** (1) **DEF-14, the recovery journal had no headroom:** on `windows-latest` a crash lost 1.20 s (fault injection, run 38771660611) and 1.50 s (`m2-recovery.spec.ts`, run 38773187568) against QLT-03's 1 s. The design allowed 0.75 s before anything reached the disk (the editor's handover up to 0.5 s after a change, then up to 0.25 s in the app), and the main process appended every text it was sent one after another, so a disk slowed by a busy runner (each append is opened, flushed, and closed) built a queue the journal lagged behind. Now the editor hands text over at most 0.25 s after it changed (`SERIALIZE_MAX_WAIT_MS`), the app keeps each handover within 0.15 s (`JOURNAL_INTERVAL_MS`), so a key is kept within 0.4 s; and a text that arrives while its note's last one still waits behind a write replaces it (`RecoveryJournal.text`), never across a save, a rename, or another note, so the journal is at most one write behind. Still off the typing path and no timer at rest (PRF-01, PRF-04): handovers and journal writes only follow typing. The browser keeps saving within 0.25 s of a handover (`UNJOURNALED_SAVE_MS`). With a journal, the note itself is written once typing pauses for 0.4 s, as before; the journal covers the time between. The 1 s budget and its journeys are unchanged. (2) **DEF-13, cold-runner setup counted as test time:** in run 38765485634 (attempt 1) the first Git test in `first-run.test.ts` took 63 s (30 s allowed) and in `git-sync.test.ts` 103 s (60 s), passing in about 1 s on the rerun. Each was the first test of its file to start Git: the fixtures' one-time setup (the fakes' `where git`, and some 20 Git commands building the repository template) ran synchronously inside it, with no timeout, so a slow first start on a cold runner blocked the worker past the test's own timeout. The setup now runs in `beforeAll` with its own 180 s (`prepareTemplate`, `prepareFakes`), every synchronous Git and `where` in the fixtures has a timeout so a hang names its command, and a setup slower than 10 s says so in the log. The tests' timeouts are unchanged. (3) **DEF-16, Backing up shown for a fetch:** `m3-git.spec.ts` "typing shows saved here … then not backed up yet" saw the icon go saved here → syncing → not backed up (4 of 6 runs locally). The window becoming active fetches; the fetch said it was syncing, and with saves waiting the app shows that as backing up, though a fetch commits none of them. A run now says it's syncing only when it sends something: saves it commits, commits not pushed yet, a Files batch, or the pointer. (4) **DEF-15, the toolbar not closing after Switch light and dark:** CI's page snapshot shows the toolbar open and the theme unchanged, so the press never ran its command. Not reproduced before the change in 70 local runs, 30 of them like CI's (software rendering, 1024 × 720, the renderer slowed fourfold); the cause is the likeliest one, not a proven one. The journey now clicks once the panel has finished opening (focus inside, every animation done), as every other toolbar journey does, and checks the switch before the close, so a recurrence says whether the press ran. (5) **DEF-17, a passcode of five words:** the EFF list's four hyphenated words (drop-down, felt-tip, t-shirt, yo-yo) are drawn again, so a passcode always reads as four words (`750e5c6`). (6) **DEF-18, signing out not done within 15 s on a runner:** the Sign out dialog was still busy; in the same run Switch account also took 20 s longer, and both run `git credential reject`, so the cause is most likely a Git or helper process stalling until the app's 30 s limit. Not seen locally. The app now logs any Git or helper call that takes 5 s or more, or is stopped by its limit (its first two arguments only, never a token or name), and how long signing out took. The journey waits for the dialog to close within the app's own limits (45 s), and a failure carries those log lines. (7) **DEF-19, nothing to restore:** with the faster journal (1), the save to disk can catch up between the journey's check and the crash. The journeys now try again from scratch when it did (`m2-recovery`) and expect the notice only when the journal was ahead (`m2-demo`), every assertion unchanged (`b62ff6d`). (8) **DEF-20, Recently deleted not shown in time (`m3-conflicts.spec.ts:54`):** on Enter's key-down the Files tree was re-created with the store's focus request unchanged, and on mounting it treated that request as new: focus jumped to the current note, the key's release landed there, and the panel closed. A tree now moves focus only for a request it hasn't already met (kept by its id while it's re-created, forgotten when the panel closes), `5d48ac6`; a smaller path, Files asking for focus again once the workspace was listed, was fixed first (`0968b65`). 50 of 50 CI-like runs after the fix, against about 1 failure in 20 before. The quarantine is lifted. (9) **DEF-26, a save over another program's change (`m3-conflicts.spec.ts:162`):** a save checked only whether the watcher had reported a change yet, so another program's write landing during the save's 400 ms delay was written over. Each save now carries the text the window knows is on disk, and the main process writes only if the file still holds it, checked when the write's turn comes (after the per-file queue and any Git pull, and again before the rename), else the edits stay and the conflict resolver gets the text found (D-92); Files' link rewrites carry the text they were made from and are refused the same way, and never rewrite the open note while it isn't saved. (10) **DEF-25, the slash menu a frame behind the note (`m1-slash-paste.spec.ts:83`):** the reading zone scrolls from a frame's callback, and a scroll written there reaches scroll listeners only in the next frame, so a menu placed earlier in that frame was painted once over the caret's row; the reading zone now says it scrolled (`anchors.moved`) and open surfaces are placed again in the same frame (D-156 round 6); and a surface opening runs a band check still waiting for the next frame at once, so the note is at rest before it's placed (a second path, run 38917526303). Details and evidence: [gate evidence](gate-evidence.md#ci-stability-def-13-to-def-27). | Proposed | [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md), D-105, D-132, [Gate evidence](gate-evidence.md#ci-stability-def-13-to-def-27) |
| D-153 | **The shadow margin is never white: measured at the screen level, and the window stays layered (the owner saw the 16 px margin around the window opaque white on Windows 11, and once the whole window white; open: unconfirmed on the owner's hardware).** (1) **How it's measured:** the page screencast (DSK-09) can't see the system's composition, so `e2e/white-margin.spec.ts` (screen-level, CI only, D-141; skipped elsewhere) puts a solid magenta window behind the app and captures the screen (`desktopCapturer`) again and again while the window lets clicks through and takes them back (`setIgnoreMouseEvents`, the margin's pass-through), focuses and blurs (`settle()`, D-150), hides and shows, minimizes and restores, resizes, maximizes, maximizes vertically, snaps (with its preview), after the GPU process crashes (`chrome://gpucrash`), and checks that typing still reaches the screen. It runs in software compositing (a runner, a remote desktop, after the GPU went away, D-121) and on the GPU path (DirectComposition), reached on a runner with no GPU through Direct3D 11 on WARP, each with the window kept layered and as before (dev-only seams `FOCUS_NOTES_CHROMIUM_SWITCHES` and `FOCUS_NOTES_WINDOW_LAYERED=0`, inert in a packaged app). A capture counts only when the backdrop around the window is in it; a margin pixel fails when it isn't magenta or magenta darkened by the shadow. (2) **Measured (CI runs 38778255653, 38780838951 and 38782014363):** 0% white or opaque margin in every scenario, on both paths, layered and as before; typing reaches the screen in every variant. The white margin did not reproduce on the runners. The only other findings were the window not drawn (the backdrop showing through, never white) for up to about a second while the GPU process restarts, and harness problems now handled: blank frames from the capturer, `moveTop` not raising a window on a runner, and `blur()` activating another window over the screen. (3) **Suspected cause, from Electron's source (v42, `native_window_views.cc`), not reproduced:** on Windows `SetIgnoreMouseEvents(false)` takes `WS_EX_LAYERED` off the window unless Electron made it layered itself (`layered_`, set only by `setOpacity`), so each time the margin's pass-through ended, the window's style changed under the compositor, and the system can show the window's plain redirection surface until the next composition. A real GPU and driver (overlay planes, several monitors, scaling) may be needed; the runners have none. (4) **Shipped as a mitigation:** the app-built window on Windows is made layered once at creation (`setOpacity(1)`), so the pass-through only turns `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT` on and off and the window's composition style never changes. Measured harmless on both paths (margin clear, content updating). The pass-through (D-104), taking the mouse back (D-150) and the band's drag (D-147) are unchanged. (5) **Open until the owner checks it on his computer.** If it happens again, the owner reports: what he did just before (moved the pointer across the window's edge, switched apps, restored or snapped the window, woke the computer, plugged in or changed a display), whether it stays or goes by itself, the GPU and driver (Settings → System → Display → Advanced display, or Task Manager → Performance → GPU), each display's scale and refresh rate, and whether several monitors are connected; *Report a problem* adds the compositing state (D-121, D-140). (6) **If it still happens:** drop the margin on Windows: no drawn shadow, a hairline border, resize handles just inside the edges as for a snapped window, and no pass-through at all (the owner accepted no shadow if it can't be done without glitches). (7) **The screen-level journey is a diagnostic, not a merge gate:** it never reproduced the white margin on CI and adds minutes and harness flakes to a shard, so `white-margin.spec.ts` runs only with `FOCUS_NOTES_DIAGNOSTIC=white-margin`. | Proposed (open: unconfirmed on owner hardware) | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html), [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md#glitch-free-windows-and-views), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (DSK-09) |
| D-154 | **No dead ends in setup (the owner's report: *Download to this computer* failed with "That folder already has other files", and [Try again] failed the same way, with nothing else to do while setup held the app).** (1) **Cause:** setup fixed its folder when it started (`Documents/Focus Notes`, or a free one beside it) and kept it in `setup.json`; by the time *Download* ran the folder held other files (notes written on this computer meanwhile, or an earlier run), and the only action repeated the same check. (2) **A folder the app chose never fails:** *Download* checks it again when it runs, and when it isn't empty and isn't already this clone (resumed), it uses the next free folder beside it then (`Focus Notes 2`, …) and records it in setup's progress. (3) **A folder the person chose** (or the open workspace's, on Run setup again) that's taken offers [Choose another folder…] (the system's dialog; the part then runs again there), [Use a new folder] (the next free folder beside the default) and *Back*. (4) **No dead ends:** every failed part offers something that changes what happens next, not only the same call again, and *Back* last (D-131's action row): [Try again] and [Start over] for most problems (transient ones, offline, sign-in and rate limits, keep [Try again] first), [Start over] alone for names GitHub can't use. *Start over* forgets setup's progress, with nothing removed on GitHub or on disk, and brings back *Create my notes*. A unit test walks every part × every problem; the catalogue's failure states check *Start over* and *Back*. (5) **Run setup again from a folder used on this computer only** (the owner's case: the open workspace was `Focus Notes 2`, local notes): that folder is never the download's target; setup is app-chosen there and goes in a free folder beside it (D-124); a setup an earlier version started there moves off it. Only an open workspace that's a clone with a remote counts as the person's folder. (6) **Open from GitHub into a taken folder** offers the same [Choose another folder…], [Use a new folder] and *Back*. (7) **An error belongs to what produced it:** a lookup's error ("Can't reach GitHub to look for your notes.") shows only on the lookup panels, never while setup runs or on *Your notes are ready*, and every error clears when the step changes or the action is retried; the catalogue checks that no state that went well shows an error. | Proposed | [First run → Principles](../experience/first-run.md#principles-of-the-desktop-first-run), D-98, D-124, D-131, D-135 |
| D-155 | **The notes folder is named after the notes' repository (the owner: "Folder name should be the same as the repository name, else it's confusing"; their `prabins-notes` went in `Documents\Focus Notes`).** (1) **Creating:** the folder is `Documents/<instance repository name>` as entered in setup (default `notes`), for example `Documents/prabins-notes`; when it has other files and isn't already this clone, the first free `<name> 2`, `<name> 3` beside it (D-154's free-folder rule). A folder the app chose is decided when *Download to this computer* runs, so changing the name in the form changes the folder. (2) **Opening existing notes** (*We found your notes* → Open, *Open from GitHub*): `Documents/<repository name>`, the same rule. (3) **Just use this computer for now** has no repository and keeps `Documents/Focus Notes`, the app's own neutral name. (4) **Names Windows can't use:** GitHub already limits names to letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`; trailing dots are dropped (`notes.` → `notes`, only dots → `notes`) and device names get `-notes` (`con` → `con-notes`, `con.x` → `con-notes.x`). (5) *More options* shows the real folder for the name typed ("Saved on this computer in …"), and Settings → Workspace shows the folder in use. (6) No existing workspace folder is renamed or moved. | Proposed | [First run → Your notes](../experience/first-run.md), [Desktop and Git → The workspace folder](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#the-workspace-folder), D-98, D-124, D-154 |
| D-156 | **The note scrolls smoothly, and the focus band follows a resize live (the owner asked for both, proposed).** (1) **Auto-scroll** that keeps the line being edited inside the reading band animates over 200 ms (`motion.autoScroll`) with a symmetric ease-in-out (`easing.inOut`, `cubic-bezier(0.42, 0, 0.58, 1)`), every time, never a jump. A new target while one runs continues from the note's current position and speed toward the new target (a cubic Hermite curve, at rest there) and finishes no later than one frame after the running scroll would have, so scrolls never queue or stack and never push their end out by a fresh 200 ms; held keys follow the caret continuously (while a key repeats, each step finishes within three frames, so the caret stays on screen even in a 360 px window); reduced motion makes it instant; a drag-selection still waits for the release. Owner-approved refinements: (a) only a scroll the person's own key, input, or click caused animates; opening a note and other moves the person didn't make place the note at once; (b) a move of under 4 px (or a target past the end the browser clamps away) is written at once, so typing at the end of a long note starts no animation; (c) held keys follow as above. Round 4 (CI): only a caret move from typing, a key, or a click in the note animates, with the note focused, find closed, and a caret rather than a range; every other scroll (a programmatic selection, focus returning from the link editor or Spotlight, find, a range the contextual toolbar opens on) is ProseMirror's own instant scroll, in the same task, as before D-156, so no overlay tied to the selection ever opens on a moving note. The animator only writes scrollTop. Also: a press in the note stops a running scroll where it is (the text stays under the pointer), and focus leaving the note ends it at once on its target (an anchored surface such as the link editor is hidden while its anchor is under the top band, which dropped the focus it was given). Round 5 (DEF-21, CI): a surface anchored to the note (the slash menu, a link card, the contextual toolbar, the link editor) opening ends a running scroll at once on its target, and while one is open every scroll is instant, so none opens or sits on a moving note; and anchored surfaces follow their anchor the way floating-ui's `autoUpdate` does: placed again on any scroll or layout change, once per frame, against the anchor read live in that frame (the slash menu had been placed against a rectangle published a frame earlier, so for a frame the scrolling caret slid under it). Round 6 (DEF-25): a scroll the reading zone writes from a frame's callback reaches scroll listeners only in the next frame, so the reading zone says it scrolled the note (`anchors.moved`) and every open anchored surface is placed again in that same frame. Round 7 (the owner, 21 October: selection auto-scroll, and deselecting, were jumpy): a selected range whose moving end leaves the band (Shift with an arrow, or a drag released there) uses the same 200 ms animation as a caret, and so does a range collapsing (deselecting) while its toolbar is still open. With a range selected, the formatting toolbar may stay open while the note glides, because it follows its live anchor in each written frame; a toolbar about to open waits until the scroll ends, so opening never cuts the animation short. Shift with the note's start or end key (Windows and Linux `Mod+Shift+Home/End`) selects to there through the editor rather than the browser, whose own reveal is an instant jump; where that shifted chord is already another command's key (macOS `Mod+Shift+Down` moves a block) the command keeps it. A fresh caret (the slash menu, a link) still scrolls at once while an anchored surface is open. Round 8 (the owner, 21 October: deselecting with a click past the band still jumped): a quick click's selection reaches the editor only after the release (the browser's `selectionchange` follows `mouseup`), when the release's own check had already run against the old range; a selection change within 150 ms of a release in the note now counts as that click's move, so it glides. (2) **The focus band follows a window resize in real time:** while the window is being resized it's re-measured every frame (one layout read per frame, FOC-05), so it stays on the caret's row as lines rewrap, never waiting for the resize to end or the next caret move (FOC-02). Cause of the owner's height-resize report: the band's transition list named its properties as `var()` values, so it fell back to `all`, and the note's 25vh/35vh padding glided after every height change while the band, measured once, stayed behind. The transition now names the band's properties; any resize (width, height, both, or the top edge) marks the band stale, and the band is then written without a glide (`cmp.editorSurface.focusLiveDuration`, `motion.none`). | Proposed | [Foundations → Motion](../design/foundations.md), [Focus mode](../experience/focus-mode.md) (FOC-02) |
| D-157 | **A template repository is never your notes, on any route (security P0, proposed).** Before D-147, *We found your notes* listed the public template; the owner opened it, and the app committed and pushed his notes to it (the template has since been cleaned). D-147 only filtered the list. Now one guard, `isTemplateRepo(owner, name)` (`TemplateGuard`: the Focus Notes template by name in any letter case, without the network, or GitHub's `is_template`, kept for the session), is asked at every way in: downloading or opening from GitHub, a folder on this computer (its `origin`), switching workspace, and setup's parts (copy, private repository, download, save to GitHub). **Before every push**, saving and setup read the remote the branch pushes to and refuse a template: the commits stay here, and the save state, the trigger, and a toast say "This is a template repository. Your notes weren't saved to it." with *Run setup again*. When GitHub can't answer, the last good answer stands, and a repository never known as a template saves as normal; the known template is always refused. After security review: every push URL is checked, before the push and before its retry; the known template's name is matched anywhere in a URL; and a remote that can't be read as owner/name (GitHub, `www.`, `ssh.` on 443, or an SSH alias) is refused (sync `unreadableRemote`). The fail-open cases are listed in [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md). Problem codes: sync `template`, setup `template` (with *Start over*), and opening `template`. Open: a workspace without a private repository puts new notes in the public `notes/` folder (FRE-06); queued, not changed here. | Proposed | [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync), [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md), D-88, D-147 |
| D-158 | **A new note in a workspace without a private repository is marked Public and isn't backed up until the writer chooses (privacy, proposed: the orchestrator's default from the handoff, for the owner to confirm).** Found by D-157: in a clone without access to the private repository (FRE-06), *New note* fell back to the public `notes/` folder and the next backup published it, against private by default (D-05, SHR-01). (1) **Where it goes:** still `notes/` (there's no private folder to put it in), and the empty note says so: its placeholder starts *Public note* (`canvas.placeholderPublic`). (2) **Marked from creation:** the bottom-left corner shows a *Public · not backed up* pill beside the save state (the read-only pill's place, UI-12) while the note waits, and the Files panel describes it the same way. (3) **Held until answered:** before the note's file is first written, the main process adds its path to a block the app manages in the notes repository's `info/exclude` (in Git's common folder: local to this clone, never committed, never pushed). Git then never lists or stages it, so neither the app's backups nor a terminal's `git add -A` can publish it, and the hold survives quitting and crashes. (4) **Asked once:** the first time a backup would include it with the writer's words in it (*Save to cloud*, or switching to another note; an empty note left behind isn't asked about) the app asks once per note and session, *Where should "⟨title⟩" live?*, with two option cards: **Make it public** (anyone can read it once it's published) and **Keep it on this computer only** (never backed up or published). Dismissing leaves it waiting; the pill asks again. *Make it public* removes the line and the note backs up with the next save; *Keep it on this computer only* moves the line to the block's *this computer* list, and the pill then says *On this computer only* (pressing it offers *Make it public* again). (5) **Following the note:** a rename that follows the title, a move in the Files panel, and a delete update the line before the file changes, so a held note's new path is never committed.
