| D-255 | **Judgement over procedure; risk-sized gates; the full suite is rare (the owner, 31 October 2026, 13:31 to 13:35).** 13:31: in *show me the UX sooner*, a change goes to the owner's review build once the fast checks for what changed pass; CI and the full suite run only after the owner accepts it. 13:33: judgement over convention; rules, gates and kit procedures are the owner's preferences, applied only when they serve what the owner wants now. 13:34: size checks to the risk of the change. 13:35: "Update the orchestrator agent to be not dumb and do smart automation and not dumb automations. Full test suite should be run sparingly and there should be checklist that should all check before you run the full suite, ever. There's already a throughput per unit time rule, why are you not following it." (1) Every agent asks before each step whether it moves the owner's current goal forward, and skips it with one line if not. (2) The full suite (`pnpm test:e2e`, all the journeys, a full CI wait) runs only when every item of the *Before the full suite* checklist is true: the owner accepted the change; it touches runtime behaviour the fast checks can't cover; the targeted checks pass; it's going to `main` or a release now; nothing about to land would force a rerun; and the agent can name the risk only the full suite covers. (3) The merge gate is sized to the risk: low-risk UI-only changes (tokens, primitive styling, view documents, copy) land on `main` after their fast checks; desktop runtime changes (main, preload or IPC, file I/O and saving, sync or Git, security or privacy, build or packaging) keep the Windows CI gate. Amends D-113's "no exceptions" merge gate and extends D-206. | Accepted (owner) | [AGENTS.md → Before the full suite](../../AGENTS.md#before-the-full-suite-d-255), [AGENTS.md → Working modes](../../AGENTS.md#working-modes-and-throughput-d-113), [The agentic loop → Throughput](agentic-loop.html#throughput), [Definition of done → When checks run](definition-of-done.md#when-checks-run), `.github/agents/orchestrator.agent.md`, `builder.agent.md`, `verifier.agent.md`, `code-reviewer.agent.md` |

## Projects, sharing and publishing (3 November 2026)

**Authoritative-source correction, 3 November 12:22:** the nested source replaces the flattened brief; see the exact [capture](next-direction.md#the-owners-notes-3-november-2026). D-293 corrects the missing folder pins in D-277. D-292 supersedes every earlier browser-writing, browser-local-notes, owner-vault and ease-in proposal, including D-90; their old rows are historical, not parallel plans. D-291's site theme now applies to a reading website only. The site/share reader remains, not a hosted writing app.

| ID | Decision and reason | Status | Source and affected pages |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-292 | **Pages is a clean website; retirement is approved to plan.** Retire browser writing, IndexedDB notes, owner vault, demo and ease-in funnel; retain public pages and sealed reading. The owner confirmed there are no users and rejected heavy inventory, byte-equivalent export and retention gates. Before removal, offer the owner's own browser drafts, if any, a one-click local export; failed/cancelled export leaves those drafts untouched. No upload or continuing browser editor. Rejected: a speculative multi-user migration programme or silent draft deletion. This approves the retirement plan, not implementation during this spec-only task. | **ACCEPTED by the owner on 3 November 2026** (13:35) | Owner: “We don't have any users at the moment.” [Browser retirement](../architecture/web-app.md), WEB-01 / WP-P.13 / MP.9 |
| D-293 | **Pin folders as well as projects and notes.** The source asks for pinned folders and projects; D-277's notes-only Files interpretation was incomplete. A pinned folder is a shortcut to its existing subtree, with its path and Pin/Unpin, not a copy or a second scope. Folder and note pins share `.focus-notes/pins.json`; project pins remain device-local. Moves retain stable pins, deletes remove the shortcut without deleting content, and unpin never changes a file. | Decided (owner: folders and projects); persistence and note pins Proposed | Nested “pinned folders and projects → Pinned vs Favorites”, [Projects](../experience/projects.md#recent-and-pinned), [Files](../design/views/files.html), PRJ-07. |
| D-294 | **Custom SVG project logos.** Add Upload SVG… beside presets; validate and sanitize into a passive local SVG before saving. Reject scripts, event handlers, foreignObject, embedded HTML, external references, CSS imports, fonts, animation and unsupported elements; no network during parsing/preview. Never render raw upload markup. Keep the old logo on refusal and offer a preset. The sanitized asset can be included in the public site only after preview makes that exposure clear. Proposed bounds: 256 KiB source, finite positive viewBox, 1,000 graphic nodes; no external tool/service. | Decided (owner: upload SVG); safety/bounds Proposed | Final source reread: nested under project logo presets. [Projects](../experience/projects.md#telling-projects-apart), PRJ-13. |
| D-295 | **Public website home and sections.** List all published/public notes and folders, never shared-only/private titles, excerpts, counts or paths. Folders are navigable sections; notes are articles with title and first readable lines. Folder rows show a descendant public-file count and expandable navigation; every note has its own article page. Preserve stable public URLs and Markdown links. Large/deep sections use pagination/progressive disclosure and keyboard navigation, never one unbounded menu. | Decided (owner); scale pattern Proposed | Final source reread, “What will we need for the GitHub pages website”. [Published website](../experience/published-site.md), SHR-21. |
| D-296 | **Minimal website navigation with reading controls.** Optional project logo follows its site-visible setting, top-level public sections, Light/Dark/System and the same paragraph-focus reading mode as the desktop app. Site reading controls are not an editor; no caret, writing toolbar or browser notes. Reader preference is local to the site, independent of app appearance; focus defaults off, reduced motion and keyboard/screen-reader reading remain intact. | Decided (owner); preference/default mechanics Proposed | Final source reread, top navigation nesting. [Published website](../experience/published-site.md#reading-controls), SHR-21. |
| D-297 | **Preview site inside the app.** Preview the public home/sections/article with the exact website renderer; choose site theme, approved fonts and default font size. Read-only and clearly Unpublished preview, no deploy or grant creation; Apply saves site settings, Publish is separate. Use bundled licensed fonts and design tokens, no remote font URL. Cancel preserves saved settings; failed saves keep the draft. | Decided (owner); safety/settings mechanics Proposed | Final source reread, “Preview site” sibling of web direction. [Published website](../experience/published-site.md#preview-site), SHR-22. |
| D-298 | **Proportionate recovery, without a 30-day gate.** The owner accepted simplified browser retirement: offer one-click local export of their drafts, if any, before removal (D-292). Drop the heavy inventory/byte-equivalence/30-day recovery prerequisites. Local notes still retains a local recoverable copy before an explicit delete, supports Undo/Recently deleted across restarts and collision-safe restore; failed retention prevents deletion. No timed automatic purge or standalone-window Delete file is introduced. Rejected: treating recovery as permission for unsolicited deletion, or requiring a retention programme before a hobby project's browser retirement. | **ACCEPTED by the owner on 3 November 2026** (13:35) | [Projects](../experience/projects.md#local-notes), PRJ-08 / MP.3, [Browser retirement](../architecture/web-app.md) / MP.9 |
| D-299 | **Encrypted shares may remain in public Git history indefinitely.** The owner accepts public ciphertext storage and its irreversible history: “Stuff can't be recalled from git history, that's fine” and “History retaining indefinitely is fine.” Seal before public commit; only opaque authenticated ciphertext and deliberately published plaintext belong there, never private source, metadata, keys or passcodes. Withdrawal/expiry removes current output, not previous commits or readers' copies. Keep one honest history sentence in first-share confirmation, not a repeated approval blocker. No all-private owner vault or history rewrite. | **ACCEPTED by the owner on 3 November 2026** (13:35) | [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#project-model), SHR-20 / SHR-21, D-274 |

## Inline images (3 November 2026)

The [second authoritative source](../experience/images.md#the-owners-image-notes-3-november-2026), relayed at 13:36, requests this scope. The following defaults resolve reversible detail, not a claim of approved UI or implementation. No critical product question remains open; actual data/network operations are outside this spec-only task.

| ID | Decision and reason | Status | Source and affected pages |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-300 | **Images stay in the document flow, with three proportional modes.** Column is default; Wide extends by up to 8 rem inside canvas/wide-content bounds; Actual size never upscales. Fit height caps at min(36 rem, 70% usable viewport). Resolve the source's contradictory “Remove max height … on by default” positively: Fit height on by default, uncheck for tall images. Rounded corners initially off; successful last-used Rounded/Fit height options seed new insertions, per-image values stay stored. Bulk mixed selection changes images only, one Undo. Rejected: floating layout, cropping as display fit, an unchecked label whose double negative hides the default. | Owner-requested modes/toggles; reversible wording/bounds decided 3 November | [Images](../experience/images.md#three-display-modes), IMG-01 to IMG-03, DD-IMG-01 |
| D-301 | **Keep assets in their existing ownership roots; share the encrypted reference closure.** Managed assets/images uses random filenames and relative references, private/site/local roots separate; standalone Markdown has an explicit local sidecar. Copy/stage asset before note reference, no external source overwrite, bulk relocation or orphan purge. Encrypt authorized private media and metadata before public checkout; no plaintext thumbnails/EXIF, name-derived URLs or unrelated assets. Public exports strip ancillary source/location metadata. Rejected: a media server, absolute paths, copying all assets, public intermediates or an architecture migration. | Owner-requested storage/encryption; bounded safety/detail decided 3 November | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#planned-image-attributes-and-asset-transactions), IMG-05, IMG-06, IMG-10 / SHR-23 |
| D-302 | **Image actions live where the image is.** Context menu and keyboard provide Cut/Copy/Replace/Save, display choices, Edit image and Auto caption; mixed-selection controls act on fully selected images, never text. Clipboard failure prevents Cut; replacement is a new managed file and ordinary Undo. Drop/picker supports bounded PNG/JPEG/WebP batches; unsupported media is refused, not fetched/converted silently. Rejected: a persistent image toolbar, arbitrary first image for multi-selection, deleting source files on Cut. | Owner-requested menu; reversible formats/limits decided 3 November | [Image menu](../experience/images.md#image-menu-and-selection), IMG-01 / IMG-03 / IMG-04 |
| D-303 | **AI captions are explicit selected-image requests to an existing capable integration.** Show chosen integration/model/destination, send only a metadata-stripped bounded image and instruction; review editable plain text below image before Use caption. Stop/error preserves previous caption. Do not equate caption with alt text, silently switch model/provider or host a model. Reuse existing disclosure/no-extra-first-send-consent policy. Rejected: surrounding-note upload, automatic public image URLs, automatic overwrite or executing model output. | Owner-requested caption; reversible review/default mechanics decided 3 November | [Auto caption](../experience/images.md#auto-caption-using-ai), IMG-09 |
| D-304 | **Crop/rotate has an explicit destructive save, not pixel Undo.** Modal preview with keyboard crop fields, 90° rotations and Reset; Cancel writes nothing. Save changes has a truthful no-Undo warning, staged atomic commit and external-version guard; isolate references so other notes do not change. Existing committed Git versions may restore old pixels; non-Git/uncommitted images have no promised recovery. Rejected: silent overwrite, claiming ordinary Undo can restore bytes or building a non-destructive image-history product. | Owner-requested destructive boundary; safe commit/UX detail decided 3 November | [Crop/rotate](../experience/images.md#crop-and-rotate-dialog), IMG-07 / IMG-08 |

The owner's notes of 3 November ([Next direction](next-direction.md)) set the direction; these rows record it and the choices that close the gaps. **Decided (owner)** rows restate the owner's own words; **Proposed** rows are the worker's answers to the owner's open questions, treated as the plan until the owner changes them. The owner accepted the six follow-up choices on 3 November at 13:35 (D-272, D-274, D-285, D-290, D-292, D-299); none is open. The review of the same afternoon amends some of these rows; see D-305 to D-314 below. The detail is in [Projects](../experience/projects.md) and [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md).

| ID | Decision and reason | Status | Source and affected pages |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-272 | **Project is the product word for the existing repository scope.** One private GitHub repository owns notes/settings/history; its existing public instance repository becomes its site. New projects start private-only; sharing adds a site. The owner accepts a mostly naming/mental-model migration with minimal architecture change: explain Project at first run/upgrade, update Settings/Files/title bars and Help, preserve paths, repositories, IDs, links and history. Nothing moves on disk. Keep existing two-repository publishing and private-first commits. See the [UX-led migration plan](../experience/projects.md#meeting-project-for-the-first-time); one snapshot/rollback note is sufficient. Rejected: moving/recloning notes, redesigning storage, folder-per-project scopes or turning this terminology change into a data migration. | **ACCEPTED by the owner on 3 November 2026** (13:35) | Owner: “It's mostly a name change” and “more from a UX and UI point of view to communicate the mental model effectively.” [Projects](../experience/projects.md), [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html) |
| D-273 | **Open a project; never "switch".** Each project opens in **its own window**, one project per window, and several can be open at once (built on multiple windows, WP-8.5). Opening a project that's already open brings its window forward. The window is scoped to the project: Files, Spotlight, Find in all notes, links, Recent, Move to…, Import and agents see only that project, and there's no way to browse outside it. The words are *Open project…*, *New project…* and *Close project*; *Switch* is never used. At launch the app reopens the projects that were open when it quit. Why: the owner's words ("we should open a Project rather than think of it as Switching"); a window per project makes the scope visible and keeps unsaved work in one project untouched by another. Rejected: switching in place (one project hidden behind another, scope invisible); tabs for projects (several scopes in one window, which defeats focus). | Decided (owner); details Proposed | Owner: "Do we switch or do we open a project. I believe we should open a Project rather than think of it as Switching." [Projects](../experience/projects.md#the-open-view) |
| D-274 | **Private only until first share; turning off removes content, never the repository.** First-share confirmation names the public site repository/address, encrypted shares versus readable publications, and one honest Git-history limit. Use ⟨project⟩-site as the reversible suggested name, confirmed before creation. Turn off withdraws shares and removes all shared/published content from the site repository/current website at the next successful publish; repository, private notes and history remain. Never delete the site repository. Offline/failure stays pending, not “off”; retry the same removal. Rejected: deleting the repository, claiming Git-history erasure or removing private notes. | **ACCEPTED by the owner on 3 November 2026** (13:35) | Owner: “Turning off sharing remove content from the public repo, the repo can stay.” Git-history retention accepted with D-299. [Projects](../experience/projects.md#private-only-until-the-first-share), [Sharing](../design/views/sharing.md) |
| D-275 | **Each project has a colour and a logo.** A project has an **accent colour** (12 presets drawn from the theme's accent tokens, each checked by the contrast guard in light, dark and contrast) and a **logo** from a preset set of 24 generic SVG marks (shapes and simple objects, no letters, drawn on one 24 px grid with the Icon primitive's stroke). *Show the logo as* **Badge** (in the title band, Files' heading and the Projects list), **Watermark** (also faint, 4% opacity, bottom right of the canvas, never over text) or **Off**. The colour tints the window's accent, the logo badge and the taskbar or Dock icon's overlay. Both are stored in the project, `.focus-notes/project.json`, so every computer shows the same. Why: the owner wants to tell project windows apart at a glance; preset marks avoid uploads and licensing, and tokens keep every colour readable. Rejected: a full theme per project (themes are personal and per device, D-46); uploaded images (licensing, size, and nothing in the design system to check them); initials (the owner asked for logos). | Proposed | Owner: "separate color theming and logo each representing a different projects … Logos can be shown as a proper project logo or as watermark." [Projects](../experience/projects.md#telling-projects-apart) |
| D-276 | **Recent files, not recent folders, each with its place.** *Recent* lists notes only, newest first, and every row shows where the note lives as muted text after the title: *⟨project⟩ · ⟨folder path⟩* in the Projects list, and the folder path alone inside a project window (*Root* at the top level). Folders aren't listed: a note is what people come back to, and a folder is reached in one step from its note (*Show in Files*). Why: the owner's own answer ("Recent files, maybe not folders") and question ("Might need to show the location"): two notes called "Plan" are only told apart by their place. Rejected: recent folders (a second list for a rare need); full paths (long and noisy; the folder path is enough inside a project). | Proposed | Owner: "Recent files, maybe not folders. Might need to show the location of the folder." [Projects](../experience/projects.md#recent-and-pinned) |
| D-277 | **Pinned, not Favourites.** People **pin** projects (to the top of the Projects list) and notes (to a *Pinned* group at the top of Files). One word, one gesture (*Pin* / *Unpin* in the item's menu and Spotlight), no star. Pinned projects are kept on this computer; pinned notes are kept in the project (`.focus-notes/pins.json`), so every computer shows them. **Amended by D-293 and D-313:** folders can be pinned too, and folder/note pins are per account. Why: pinning says what it does, keeps an item *in place at the top*, and is how the platforms people know name it (Windows Start and taskbar, macOS Finder sidebar, VS Code). Favourites implies a separate collection to curate and a second list. Rejected: Favourites with a star (a second, competing list); both (two words for one need). | Proposed | Owner: "pinned folders and projects … Pinned vs Favorites". [Projects](../experience/projects.md#recent-and-pinned) |
| D-278 | **Local notes: write now, decide where later.** A built-in place outside every project, a plain local folder under the OS Documents location's `Focus Notes/Local notes`, with its own window, autosave and Recent. **Import into project…** copies first and keeps the original until explicitly deleted; no implicit upload. No GitHub/sharing/publishing in Local notes. Local Undo/Deletion recovery follows D-298 (durable local copy, no 30-day gate), not Git. Why: the owner's quick-capture use case. Rejected: hidden note storage or saving into the last project they did not choose. | Proposed; copy/recovery safety derived from CP-SAFE-01 | Owner's use cases for files outside a project. [Projects](../experience/projects.md#local-notes) |
| D-279 | **Open with Focus Notes: one file, on its own.** On the desktop, Focus Notes registers as an app that can open `.md`, `.markdown` and `.txt` files (Open with, double-click when chosen as default, drag onto the icon, `focus-notes <file>`). A file inside an open project's folder opens in that project's window. Any other file opens in a **file window**: the canvas with the file's name in the title band, no Files panel, no Sharing, Publish or History, saved in place with its encoding and line endings kept. A `.txt` file keeps its extension and is shown as Markdown text; nothing is converted unless the person edits it. *Move to project…* copies it into a project. Why: the owner's use case ("I've this txt file and I want to open it in Focus notes … opened as a standalone separate file"). Rejected: importing the file into a project first (changes where it lives); opening it in the last project's window (breaks the project's scope). | Proposed | Owner: "Open with Focus notes … the note will have to be opened as a standalone separate file outside the context of a repo/project." [Projects](../experience/projects.md#a-file-on-its-own) |
| D-280 | **Ctrl+Shift+N is New note in…** <kbd>Ctrl+Shift+N</kbd> (<kbd>⇧⌘N</kbd>) opens a small picker before the note is made: **this project's folders only** (the current or last-used folder chosen, then the tree with a filter, and *New folder…* inline). <kbd>Enter</kbd> makes the note there and opens it with the caret in the title. **Corrected by the owner on 3 November:** the picker never lists other projects, *Local notes*, *Another project…* or *New project…*, in its results or its empty state; making a project is the deliberate flow in D-315. In a Local notes window the key starts a new local note with no picker. <kbd>Ctrl+N</kbd> stays instant (no picker, the current folder, SC-FILE-01). Key moves: *New folder* in Files moves from <kbd>Ctrl+Shift+N</kbd> to <kbd>Ctrl+Alt+N</kbd>; *New window* (reserved, never bound) is retired in favour of *Open project…* on <kbd>Ctrl+Alt+O</kbd> (<kbd>⌥⌘O</kbd>). The web keeps its keys (it has no projects). Why: the owner's request; Ctrl+Shift+N was only reserved for New window, which projects replace. Rejected: making Ctrl+N ask (slows the instant note, D-05's calm start); a separate *New note in folder* command with no key. | Decided (owner); keys Proposed | Owner: "Ctrl + shift + n creates a new file but let user select a project or a folder first". [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md) |
| D-281 | **A note's title and its file name are separate.** The title is the note's first H1 and can change freely; the file name never follows it. A new note gets its file name once, from its title when it's first saved with one (`on-focus.md`), or *Untitled ⟨date⟩.md* if it's left without one; after that only *Rename file…* (Files, ⋯, Spotlight) changes it, and links are updated as today (FIL-09). Files, Recent, Spotlight and tabs show the **title**; a row shows the file name as muted text only when the two differ. Supersedes FIL-07 and the "follows the title" part of D-92 (the "fixed once published" rule becomes unnecessary). Why: the owner asked to break the forced relationship; a following name renames the file under Git and links while the person is still writing. Rejected: the name following until the note is closed (still surprising, still churns Git). | Decided (owner); details Proposed | Owner: "Will need to break the forced relationship between file name and title of the doc inside the file". [Files](../design/views/files.html#names-and-titles) |
| D-282 | **Rewrite: Ctrl+Enter generates, and it can write at the caret.** (1) In the Rewrite prompt, <kbd>Ctrl+Enter</kbd> (<kbd>⌘Enter</kbd>) is *Generate*; <kbd>Enter</kbd> still adds a line. (2) With **no selection**, Rewrite opens anchored at the caret as **Write** (*Write with AI*): the prompt alone is sent, and the answer streams in at the caret as new text, one undo step, with the same disclosure, Stop and errors. Presets that need text (*Shorter*, *Clearer*…) are hidden. Amends RWR-72 ("with nothing selected, it opens nothing"). Why: the owner's requests. Rejected: Enter to generate (the prompt is multi-line); a separate Write command (two commands for one prompt). | Decided (owner) | Owner: "Ctrl + enter should submit prompt rewrite … It should be possible to use AI rewrite to write without selection too." [Rewrite with AI](../experience/rewrite-with-ai.md) |
| D-283 | **Spotlight closes after Enter.** Pressing <kbd>Enter</kbd> (or clicking) on any result runs it and closes Spotlight, toggles and settings included; focus goes where the result sent it, or back to the note. <kbd>Shift+Enter</kbd> runs a toggle or setting and keeps Spotlight open, for stepping through themes. Amends SPT-04's "toggles and settings leave Spotlight open" (D-111). Why: the owner reports that it lingers. | Decided (owner); Shift+Enter Proposed | Owner: "after an item has been selected and enter has been pressed, the spotlight dialog should be automatically dismissed. Currently it lingers." [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html) |
| D-284 | **One share flow, from the item, straight to the share dialog (Flow 1).** *Share…* is in every note's and folder's Files row menu (and ⋯ → Note and Spotlight). It opens the **share dialog** at once, with no screen in between, for that item only; nothing else can be added. The dialog has three parts and a row of actions: **What's being shared** (name, type, and for a folder how many notes and folders it holds, with *Review contents*), **How it opens** (expiry, Link, Passcode, passcode type), and the **actions** (D-288). Shares already made for the item are listed under a *Shared before* disclosure in the same dialog. Supersedes the D-143 *Share "⟨note⟩"* dialog with audience cards and *Share this note with…* (the "in-between" screen) and *Add people*. Why: the owner's Flow 1 and the issues they listed. | Decided (owner) | Owner: "Flow 1: This should be the primary share workflow … We need to not have any screen in between". [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md#the-share-dialog) |
| D-285 | **Shares have no recipient names or name-derived public identifiers.** The owner accepts superseding D-41. Record what a share opens privately, not whom it reached. Link, random 8-character share code and every published share path/file name use opaque random identifiers only: never a slug, hash or other derivation of the note/folder name, title or path. Codes identify, not strengthen passcodes; collision-check without consulting source names. Include names/encoded variants and derivation/provenance checks in the leak specification. Preserve legacy access without emitting new name-derived aliases. Rejected: recipient labels, readable slugs, hashed filenames and passcode-only keyring searches. | **ACCEPTED by the owner on 3 November 2026** (13:35) | Owner: “Yes share without names and don't give away the name of the file in the name of the share link.” [Sharing](../design/views/sharing.md#shares-without-names), SHR-23 / MP.14 |
| D-286 | **On/off choices are switches.** A new design-system **Switch** primitive (a track and a thumb, the on state filled with the accent and marked by the thumb's position and a check, never by colour alone, 3:1 against its surroundings in every theme) replaces toggle chips wherever a choice is on or off: the share dialog's Link and Passcode, and Settings rows that are toggles. Chips stay for choosing among many (filters, presets). Why: the owner found the selected state of Link and Passcode hard to see. | Decided (owner); the primitive's rules Proposed | Owner: "The visual distinction of selected/active state of toggles like link and passcode are not distinct and obvious … something like switch toggle". [Components](../design/system/components.md) |
| D-287 | **Review contents is a dialog over the dialog.** For a folder, *Review contents* opens a second dialog over the share dialog: the folder's tree as Files shows it (expandable, scrollable, read-only, with counts), and [Done]. <kbd>Esc</kbd> closes only it. This is the one place a dialog opens over a dialog, an explicit exception to DD-IA-04. Why: the owner chose it so the share dialog stays uncluttered. Rejected: an inline tree (crowds the dialog); a side sheet (hides the share dialog's context). | Decided (owner) | Owner: "I really don't like a dialog over a dialog but for this one we will have to do a "Dialog over a dialog"". [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md#review-contents) |
| D-288 | **The share dialog's actions.** Four actions, in this order: **Share…** (primary: the system's share sheet with the complete share information, where the platform has one; Windows 10 and Linux fall back to *Copy complete share information*), **Copy link**, **Copy access code**, and **Copy share information** (the title, the link, the site's share page, the access code and the expiry, from the editable template). An action for a way in that's off is hidden. The first action creates the share; later changes in the dialog update the same share. Copies go through the main process and clear the clipboard after 90 seconds, as built. Why: the owner's four CTAs. Rejected: a Create button before the actions (one more step every time). | Decided (owner); order and creation Proposed | Owner: "Share button that opens the OS share dialog, Copy link, Copy complete share information, Copy access code". [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md#actions) |
| D-289 | **No person-first sharing (Flow 2) and no "who".** There's no flow that starts from a person and then picks notes, and no list of who a note is shared with. To give a person ongoing access, invite them to the project (D-290). Why: the owner: "Flow 2: We won't have this feature" and "we will leave this feature out". | Decided (owner) | [Scenarios](../product/spec/scenarios.md#unsupported-boundaries) |
| D-290 | **Invite on personal accounts with honest collaborator access.** Offer whatever GitHub permits: Collaborator for personal-account projects; Read, Triage, Write, Maintain and Admin for organization projects, filtered by actual caller/policy permissions. Exact personal-account copy: “This project belongs to a personal GitHub account. A collaborator can read and change everything in this project, including private notes and sharing settings. GitHub does not offer read-only access here.” Invite person / Cancel; no fictional role picker. Secrets/full-repository consequences remain in the confirmation. Rejected: disabling all personal invites or simulating app-only Read roles. | **ACCEPTED by the owner on 3 November 2026** (13:35) | Owner: “Okay invite people in personal account with the options available, the msg has to be clear.” [People](../experience/projects.md#people-in-a-project), PRJ-12 |
| D-291 | **Publishing makes a plain website with its own theme.** Website-only public pages have their own theme, independent of app Appearance; D-295/D-296 specify section/article navigation and reading controls. **Paper is the reversible design default**, not an owner blocker. Any real publication still requires the owner's informed approval. Why: the source asks for a website with its own theme; the 12:38 correction says to decide inconsequential choices. Rejected: copying personal app Appearance into the public site, or blocking the spec on a default theme. | Website direction recorded; Paper chosen design default; real publication needs owner approval | [Published website](../experience/published-site.md), [Sharing](../design/views/sharing.md#publishing-a-website) |

## Review of the 3 November direction (D-305 to D-314)

A critique of the 3 November specification found gaps in feasibility, privacy and the plan ([Next direction → Review](next-direction.md#review-3-november)). These rows settle them. All are reversible before implementation; each keeps the owner's 13:35 decisions.

| ID | Decision and reason | Status | Source and affected pages |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-305 | **The desktop app seals shares; the site never reads the private repository.** For new projects the desktop app encrypts a share when it's created or its content, opening methods or expiry change, and commits only that ciphertext, under random names, to the site repository. It never re-seals on a schedule, so the public repository doesn't grow with every build (GitHub recommends Pages source repositories stay under 1 GB). The site needs no deploy key or secret for the private repository. Expiry is inside the authenticated envelope, so the reader refuses an expired share; the next publish, or a daily site-only workflow that reads only an opaque list of expiry dates, deletes expired files. Existing pairs keep their deploy-key pipeline until WP-P.10 moves them; **Turn off sharing** removes that deploy key, the site's `PRIVATE_CONTENT_KEY` secret and its scheduled deploy. Why: the owner's "files are put in public repo in an encrypted manner" is most directly a local seal and commit; it also removes the one path by which a public repository's workflows could read private notes. Amends D-299's "fresh per-build encryption" and D-274's deploy-key step. Rejected: nightly re-sealing into Git (unbounded history growth); keeping the deploy key after Turn off. | Proposed (review) | [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#project-model), [Sharing](../design/views/sharing.md#three-levels-of-visibility), [Projects](../experience/projects.md#private-only-until-the-first-share), PRJ-10, SHR-17, R-50 |
| D-306 | **Where a project's two repositories live on disk.** A new project's folder is its private repository; when sharing is turned on, the site repository is cloned inside it at `public\`, which the private repository ignores (`/public/` in `.gitignore`), so the project stays one folder that moves as one. Existing pairs keep their current layout, where the site repository is the root and the private repository is the `private\` submodule. Either way the trusted project record names both roots, and note operations never reach the site's workflows, settings or app files. Why: the specification named two roots but not where the second one lives, and the existing layout is the opposite way round. Rejected: a sibling folder (`Book-site` beside `Book`, which breaks when the project folder moves); a folder hidden in app data (public notes the writer can't find). | Proposed (review) | [Projects](../experience/projects.md#what-a-project-is-for), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#per-project-process-boundary-planned), PRJ-03 |
| D-307 | **Share… always starts a new share; existing shares are changed where they're listed.** Shares have no names, so an item can hold several, one per audience. Share… opens a new draft; **Shared before**, open by default when the item has active shares, lists each with Copy link, Copy access code, Change… and Withdraw. Change says **"Changes apply to everyone who has this link or access code."** Why: reusing the last grant would silently shorten or cut access for people who already have it. Amends D-284 and D-288 ("repeat actions reuse it" now means repeats within one open dialog). | Proposed (review) | [Sharing](../design/views/sharing.md#the-share-dialog), SHR-16, SHR-18 |
| D-308 | **The system share sheet, platform by platform.** macOS: Electron's `ShareMenu`. Windows: Electron has no share API (its `ShareMenu` is macOS only, and Chromium's Windows share code isn't in Electron), so it needs a small native bridge to Windows' DataTransferManager share UI, proven by a one-hour spike (A-SHR-01) before the dialog is wired. Linux, and Windows until the bridge is proven: there's no sheet, so the primary button reads **Copy share information** and the duplicate copy action is hidden. The button never says Share… when nothing would open. Amends D-288 (which assumed Windows 11 had a usable sheet). | Proposed (review) | [Sharing → Actions](../design/views/sharing.md#actions), SHR-16, R-51, [Electron ShareMenu](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/share-menu) |
| D-309 | **One running app, one window per project.** The owner's "multiple instances" are windows of one Focus Notes process: the existing single-instance lock stays. A second launch, Open with or `focus-notes <path>` hands its path to the running app (`second-instance` on Windows and Linux, `open-file` on macOS), which sends it to the project window that owns it or opens a file window. Closing a project window closes that project only; closing the last window follows D-217 (tray on Windows). Each window's title is **⟨note⟩ — ⟨project⟩ — Focus Notes**; where the system shows a per-window icon (Windows Alt+Tab and taskbar previews, Linux), it's the app icon with the project's mark. Inside a project window, Projects lists projects, Local notes and Open file… but no other project's files; cross-project Recent appears only in the window shown when no project is open. Why: the owner's "should not be able to browse outside the scope of the Project". Amends D-273 and D-276. | Proposed (review) | [Projects](../experience/projects.md#the-open-view), PRJ-02, PRJ-05, PRJ-06 |
| D-310 | **Images are written as standard Markdown images.** `![description](../assets/images/q7m4k2.png "caption")`, with non-default display options in a trailing attribute block: `{display=wide rounded fitHeight=false}`. A default image is plain Markdown. GitHub and other viewers show the picture (the caption as its title, and any options as short text after it). Why: the planned `::image{…}` directive shows as text, not a picture, in a plain Markdown viewer, which breaks CP-OWN-01 ("breaks when the app writes anything a plain Markdown viewer can't show"). Galleries keep their container directive, which already holds plain images. Amends D-300 and D-301's syntax. **Amended by D-347:** the options are a hidden comment line before the image, not a trailing block. Rejected: the leaf directive; HTML `<figure>` (raw HTML is read-only in the editor). | Proposed (review) | [Images](../experience/images.md#assets-markdown-and-encrypted-sharing), [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#planned-image-attributes-and-asset-transactions), IMG-10 |
| D-311 | **A folder share covers what's in it when it's shared, unless asked for more.** A folder share includes the notes and folders the person reviewed, with their later edits. A switch, **Include notes added later**, Off by default, makes it follow new notes too. With it Off, a note added later stays private, and Shared before says **"3 newer notes aren't shared"** with **Add them**. Why: a note created in a shared folder shouldn't be published as a side effect (CP-SHARE-01); the owner fixed the scope to "the one we started with". Amends the live-folder rule in the share dialog. | Proposed (review) | [Sharing → Review contents](../design/views/sharing.md#review-contents), SHR-14 |
| D-312 | **Addresses that don't name the project.** The first-share step proposes a neutral site name, `notes-site` (then `notes-site-2`, and so on), editable, with **"This name appears in every link you share."** The repository's description is empty by default. Leak checks treat the site name like a share path: a warning, not a block, when it contains the project's or a folder's name. Published articles keep their address on rename: the build writes a redirect page at the old address from a rename map. Why: every share link starts with the site's address, and the owner's rule is that a link never reveals what it opens. Amends D-274's `⟨project⟩-site`; settles the redirect contradiction between Files and Published website. | Proposed (review) | [Projects](../experience/projects.md#private-only-until-the-first-share), [Published website](../experience/published-site.md#home-sections-and-articles), [Files](../design/views/files.html#names-and-titles), SHR-23 |
| D-313 | **Each project's colours, and whose pins they are.** A project has an accent (D-275), which also tints its title band and the Files heading, and an optional **Appearance** of its own (Follow app, Light, Dark; *replaced by D-316's colour theme, 3 November*), Follow app by default, so two windows can look different at a glance without overriding the reading theme unless asked. Folder and note pins are per account, in `.focus-notes/pins/<account>.json`, so they follow the owner across devices but aren't imposed on collaborators. Why: the owner asked for "separate color theming" per instance, which an accent alone narrowed; pins are personal. Amends D-275, D-293 and DD-PROJ-02. | Proposed (review) | [Projects → Telling projects apart](../experience/projects.md#telling-projects-apart), PRJ-04, PRJ-07 |
| D-314 | **The plan puts the smallest visible slices first.** (1) The owner's three share-dialog fixes (Share… in the menu, no screen in between, clear switches) don't wait for projects: WP-P.9 depends on WP-5.3 only and is split, with the Switch primitive as WP-P.16. (2) WP-P.4 is split into windows and scope (WP-P.4), the terminology move (WP-P.17, which goes first, as it's mostly words) and turning sharing on and off (WP-P.18, after the site work). (3) Image storage (WP-9.3, IMG-05) is apart from encrypted image sharing (WP-9.6, IMG-06), so inline images in a private note don't wait for sharing; until WP-9.6 lands, sharing a note with images says so and stops. (4) Image requirements are all P1, and SHR-23 is P1 with its package. (5) Retired browser packages are marked retired in the work graph. | Proposed (review) | [Implementation plan → MP](implementation-plan.md#mp), [Work graph](work-graph.json), [Roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| D-315 | **New project is a deliberate, stepped action.** It's reachable only from *New project…* in Projects (with its empty and first-run states) and Spotlight's *> New project…*, never as a side path of New note, New note in… or Local notes. A short stepped dialog: name (and GitHub owner), what *Private only* means, the look (accent and logo, D-275), then a confirmation that lists what *Create project* will make: a new private GitHub repository, a local folder, and no website; its folder and GitHub owner can be changed there with *Change…* (the folder through the system picker, checked at once for a non-empty folder, an existing project and write access). Nothing is created, and nothing is sent, before that final button. Why: the owner's correction; a repository is a lasting, account-level thing, so it shouldn't appear as a by-product of jotting a note. | D-321 | **Recent and Projects rank by exponential-decay frecency.** Each item keeps one score: an open adds 1 point and an editing session 2, and the score halves every 14 days (λ = ln 2 / 14 days). It's stored as the date the score would decay to 1, so decay needs no recomputation and no visit history is kept, and sorting by that date sorts by score. Pins sit above the ranking. Why: the owner asked for the researched best algorithm. Compared: **Firefox's original frecency** (visit-type buckets at 4, 14, 31 and 90 days, sampled from the last 10 visits) is non-monotonic and jumps at bucket edges, which Mozilla's own proposal replaced with exponential decay (half-life 30 days, used in Gecko's HTTP cache); **zoxide** (score +1 per visit, ×4 within an hour, ×2 within a day, ÷2 within a week, ÷4 after, with aging) is simple but also steps at its edges. Exponential decay is monotonic, continuous, private and cheap. 14 days, not 30, because notes cycle faster than web pages; it's one constant (R-55). Sources: [Mozilla, NewFrecency](https://wiki.mozilla.org/User:Jesse/NewFrecency), [Firefox Places frecency algorithm](https://developer.mozilla.org/en/The_Places_frecency_algorithm), [zoxide algorithm](https://github.com/ajeetdsouza/zoxide/wiki/Algorithm). | **Decided (researched, 3 November 2026)** | Owner, 3 November 18:01, `focus-notes-launch.md` (word for word in [Next direction](next-direction.md#the-owners-launch-notes-3-november-2026-1801)). [Projects → How Recent and Projects are ranked](../experience/projects.md#how-recent-and-projects-are-ranked), PRJ-16, R-55 |

Rejected: one shared pin set (a cross-project pin would leak into a project's Files and to collaborators). The owner explicitly replaces D-259's private-note blocking option and its assumption that an unlisted Ollama model is Cloud. Its historical row below remains a record, not current behavior; consent and key/address isolation are retained.

Old `keepPrivate` keys are ignored on read and stripped on the next ordinary write, preserving connections, sealed keys and consent. Notes and prompts are never logged or copied into public Git objects. | Accepted (owner feedback; built on fix-ai-integration-picker) | Owner, 1 November 10:16: remove the option; it is the user's judgement whether to send. More than one configured connection gets an AI integration picker; selecting one saves the existing device-local Writer, keeps the prompt, updates disclosure/consent and uses it next time. Failed saves keep the prior choice. Ollama `:cloud`/`-cloud` models, cloud-routing metadata and `ollama.com` hosts are Cloud; otherwise loopback/private-LAN endpoints are This computer, without requiring discovery. Other editable services use the same host classification; remote endpoints are Cloud. This describes configured routing, not proof that a service never forwards text. ### 1 November feedback: inline prompt and no first-send disclaimer

The owner's 11:17 feedback supersedes the modal prompt part of D-256 and the retained consent parts of D-238, D-259, D-261 and D-262. Those rows remain historical evidence, not the current contract. Key/address isolation, explicit destination disclosure and data safety remain unchanged.

| D-265 | **Rewrite is an inline menu-like popover, not a modal.** Remove the passage preview: review the selection in the scrollable document itself. Preserve visible selected fragments while prompt controls take focus. Anchor to those fragments and clamp within the editor viewport while any fragment is visible; let the popover scroll out only when all are offscreen, and recover the same draft on scrolling back. Frame-batched scroll/resize tracking uses design-system tokens and never freezes document scroll. Deselecting a zero-character prompt dismisses immediately. Any written or preset prompt, including whitespace, instead opens the existing confirmation: *Discard prompt?* / "Deselecting this text will lose your written prompt." *Keep editing* (default and Escape) restores the original selection and prompt; *Discard prompt* clears the draft and accepts deselection, without a send or write. Prompt-control focus is not deselection. Generate and the in-place stream/undo stay as D-256. | Accepted (owner); built and locally verified in WP-AI.14; candidate/owner review pending | Owner, 1 November 11:17: “We don't need to show the selected passage at all”; “inline menu popover”; document scrolling, viewport anchoring and loss warning on written-prompt deselection. (4) **One undo step:** <kbd>Ctrl+Z</kbd> gives back the exact original and <kbd>Ctrl+Y</kbd> the rewrite. (5) **Stop and errors:** <kbd>Esc</kbd> or *Stop* stops the request; any error puts the original back and says why. (5) **Where an Ollama model runs:** "On this computer" only once main has seen the model in Ollama's own list as local (Find, *Find models* or *Test connection*); otherwise the row, the form and the consent say "Cloud". | D-260 | **AI transport limits, errors and the built catalogue.** (1) **Three clocks per request**, each closing the HTTP request: the answer must start within 30 s (120 s from a server on this computer, which may be loading its model), a started answer may go quiet for at most 30 s (60 s), and the request ends at 5 minutes (10). Test: 20 s (90 s on this computer); model lists 8 s; Find 800 ms per port. Replaces RWR-82's single 90 s. (2) **Stop, a note switch or close, and a window reload or close** abort the request in main. (3) **One reason code per failure**, and only Focus Notes' own codes reach the window: `refused`, `dns`, `tls`, `redirected`, `network`, `unauthorized`, `model-missing`, `not-found`, `rate-limited` (with Retry-After's seconds), `server-error`, `bad-request`, `too-long`, `timeout`, `provider-format`, `provider-error`, `provider-empty`. **Whole answers are capped** (SEC-09 review, L1): read by their declared length and then as they arrive, at most 2 MiB for Test and Find, 4 MiB for a model list and 512 KiB for a rewrite answered in one piece (whose text, like a stream's, is at most 64 KiB); past that, `too-long`. (4) **Thinking models** write only their answer: Ollama gets `think: false`, OpenAI `max_completion_tokens`, a leading `<think>` block is left out, and Test passes on a well-formed empty answer. (5) **The catalogue is the 26 built kinds only**; Bedrock, Vertex, Foundry with Entra, Replicate, fal, MLX LM and CLI or MCP bridges are not listed (as D-257), and a test asserts it. Their NuGet package (Microsoft.Identity.Client.NativeInterop 0.20.6) is under the Microsoft Software License Terms, whose section 3(e) forbids redistribution; GCM's MIT and Git's GPL exception (D-228) don't cover them. The managed MSAL (`Microsoft.Identity.Client*.dll`, MIT) and everything else stay. (2) **Nothing Focus Notes uses needs them:** they serve only GCM's Windows broker (WAM), which is opt-in and only for Azure DevOps, never GitHub (GCM 2.9.1 `docs/windows-broker.md`); `credential.msauthUseBroker` defaults to false, except on Microsoft Dev Box (`docs/configuration.md`, `MicrosoftAuthentication.cs`), and `GCM_MSAUTH_USEBROKER` outranks every Git setting. (3) **What:** `tools/release/git-bundle.mjs` deletes both files when it prepares the bundle and writes `credential.msauthUseBroker = false` into its system settings; the smoke test fails if either file is back or the setting is gone; `bundleEnv` sets `GCM_MSAUTH_USEBROKER=false` for every Git and sign-in-helper run of the bundle, so a person's own setting or a Dev Box can't turn the broker on. Signing in to Azure DevOps through the Windows broker isn't offered in the Store build; GitHub sign-in, download and save are unchanged. (4) **Kept out:** `tools/release/store-manifest.mjs` fails a package that contains either file anywhere (`redistributionProblems`), on CI's Store package check and before every release; the real 1.1.0 package built before this fails it, so that package is never submitted; a rebuilt one is. 1.0.0 to 1.0.2 carried no Git (it arrived with D-228) and none of these files. | Accepted | [Gate evidence](gate-evidence.md#store-gcm-broker-files-d-270), [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md), D-228, PKG-13 |
| D-271 | **The desktop package's terms keep Electron's Microsoft DirectX files under Microsoft's terms.** The Windows SDK and DXC licences let us ship `d3dcompiler_47.dll`, `dxcompiler.dll` and `dxil.dll` only if end users agree to terms that protect them at least as much; plain MIT, which lets anyone copy, modify and redistribute, didn't. (1) The root `LICENSE` stays pure MIT: it covers the code. (2) `apps/desktop/build/license-terms.txt` is the MIT text plus one clause: "The MIT License covers Focus Notes' own code. The Microsoft DirectX files included with it (d3dcompiler_47.dll, dxcompiler.dll, dxil.dll) are licensed under Microsoft's terms: use them only with Focus Notes on Windows, and don't reverse engineer them (except where applicable law permits it despite this limitation), modify them or redistribute them separately. Microsoft gives no warranty for them and has no liability for them. These limits apply only to those three files. They don't restrict any other component, which keeps its own license (see THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md, LICENSES.chromium.html and LICENSE.electron.txt), or any rights applicable law gives you." So the MIT, GPL, LGPL and MPL components keep their own terms, including the LGPL's allowance to reverse engineer for debugging modifications. It ships as the package's `LICENSE.txt` (NSIS and Store) and is the NSIS installer's licence page (`nsis.license`). (3) The Store listing's copyright line and description say MIT covers Focus Notes' own code and the DirectX files are under Microsoft's terms. (4) Partner Center's "Additional license terms" stays empty, so the Store's Standard Application License Terms apply. ## D-269: unmodified Electron reciprocal runtime exception (2 November 2026)

**Status: Accepted by the owner, 2 November 13:02** ("Okay, consider the pending
stuff approved", after both pending licence decisions were explained). Amends D-228
and AGENTS.md rule 8 (Licenses).

The exception is limited to the unmodified Electron runtime: its dynamically
loaded, separately replaceable FFmpeg DLL (LGPL-2.1-or-later) and Chromium's
MPL-2.0-covered code. It does not cover GPL-enabled FFmpeg, additional codec
libraries, modifications to those components, other dependencies or a general
relaxation of the permissive-license policy. Git's separate-program exception
remains D-228.

| Obligation or boundary | How the package preserves it |
| --- | --- |
| Notices and full license texts | Ship `LICENSE.electron.txt`, the unmodified `LICENSES.chromium.html` (including full LGPL-2.1/MPL-2.0 texts), and `resources/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. The latter names the runtime, licenses, source pins and replacement/debugging rights. |
| Corresponding source | Electron v42.4.1 and its patches/build scripts; Chromium 148.0.7778.265; FFmpeg f45bab87ce4c5fafc67fd53fcde777578d01bfa0, pinned by that Chromium DEPS. Exact source links are generated in the third-party notices, not a moving main branch. Preserve MPL file-level terms and LGPL replacement/debugging rights. |
| Narrow binary scope | The Store manifest checker requires the notice files and rejects FFmpeg outside the app root or any other FFmpeg/libav codec library. The runtime DLL is not merged into the app's ASAR or modified. Negative package fixtures protect this scope. |
| Submission | Accepted by the owner on 2 November 13:02, before the rebuilt package's upload. The upload and submission themselves remain the owner's. |

Sources: [Electron DEPS](https://github.com/electron/electron/blob/v42.4.1/DEPS),
[Chromium DEPS](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/148.0.7778.265/DEPS),
[FFmpeg source](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/third_party/ffmpeg/+/f45bab87ce4c5fafc67fd53fcde777578d01bfa0/).
Affected: [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md), [gate evidence](gate-evidence.md#whole-payload-attribution-limits),
LIC-02, PKG-12 and PKG-13. The existing policy remains in effect outside this
exception.

## D-263: integration identities (1 November 2026)

> [!NOTE]
> **Accepted, built on fix-ai-icons; not merged.** Owner, 1 November 10:16: "Find and use proper icons for all the AI integrations." Extend CP-RWR-01 → SC-RWR-04 → UC-RWR-05 → IA-VIEW-REWRITESETTINGS / IA-CMP-REWRITESTATUS → DD-RWR-07 → AIS-03, in WP-AI.11 / MAI.

Reuse **Icon**, not a second brand primitive. All paths stay unmodified, monochrome (`currentColor`), in local design-system data; views name icon IDs, never inline SVG. Existing icon-size tokens cover light, dark and contrast. A mark beside the name is decorative; a standalone mark needs `label`. `aiConnectionIcon(kind): IconName` and `AI_CONNECTION_ICONS` are exported from `@focus-notes/ui/contracts` and `@focus-notes/ui`, covering every current kind and Copilot, Claude Code, Codex and Gemini CLI aliases. This does not add CLI support, hosted models, traffic, waits or status claims. Core reads only the pure data helper through a relative source import, avoiding a React/UI package dependency in core.

### Included artwork and terms

Source revision: [Simple Icons 90646e4](https://github.com/simple-icons/simple-icons/tree/90646e4bf7f3b89df6c37e65baf3e7bc759a517c). The catalogue's default is [CC0-1.0](https://github.com/simple-icons/simple-icons/blob/90646e4bf7f3b89df6c37e65baf3e7bc759a517c/LICENSE.md); Copilot explicitly carries MIT. [The disclaimer](https://github.com/simple-icons/simple-icons/blob/90646e4bf7f3b89df6c37e65baf3e7bc759a517c/DISCLAIMER.md) warns that original brand rights remain and absent per-brand licence data is not a legal assurance. No trademark permission is inferred from CC0. These are referential service identities only, never endorsement, app branding or readiness indicators. The generated `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` records each pinned SVG URL, original source and terms URL, and the MIT full text.

| Kinds | Mark | Reason |

| Docker Model Runner | code | [Docker logo guidelines](https://www.docker.com/legal/trademark-guidelines/) require authorization; informational use permits word marks, not logos |

| Cohere, Groq, Together, Fireworks, xAI, Cerebras | cloud | No verified permissive artwork obtained in this increment |

Fallbacks are existing Fluent UI System Icons (MIT, D-32), never invented facsimiles. Artwork gaps do not remove supported providers. See [Settings](../experience/ai-settings.md), [Icon catalog](../design/system/components.md#integration-icons), [Help](../help/connect-an-ai-service.md) and [wait inventory](../architecture/perceived-performance.md). Gallery fixtures and scoped tests cover all names, kinds, accessible labels and inherited colour.

## Rewrite with AI decisions (30 October 2026)

**Superseded in most part by D-256 and D-257 (31 October), above.** The owner's request on 30 October was to specify, not implement. The foundation choices below were proposed implementation contracts; each row's status now says what replaced it. Long-form [engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), [providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), [history](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#undo-and-redo) and [experience](../experience/rewrite-with-ai.md) refine them without implying delivery.

| ID | Decision and reason | Status | Source and affected pages |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-233 | **F1: desktop first, Windows first; external integration client only.** Core owns state/commands, desktop main owns API transport/key storage, ui/views own controls, editor owns mapping/apply/decorations. Focus Notes never installs, downloads, embeds, launches, hosts or executes models/model processes; local endpoints are separately managed and already running. Browser rewrite is excluded until its secure boundary is proven. | Accepted (as built under D-257) | Owner rewrite request and client-only correction, 2026-10-30; D-112, D-211; [Engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), [Providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-25, RWR-48 |
| D-234 | **F2: protected in-place preview, never an implicit save.** Canonical original is durable before Send. Hold target autosave/ordinary commits through review until Accept/Discard. Ordinary recovery contains original plus independent edits, never generated fragments; complete variations stay in isolated history. Leaving discards only the mapped target and retains outside edits/history. Accept creates one canonical undo event; projection navigation/Discard before Accept is not a canonical edit. This supersedes review autosave so unaccepted text cannot reach a remote. | Superseded by D-256 | Owner asks for inline rewriting and undo, 2026-10-30; coordinator safety clarification and canonical/projection correction, 2026-10-30; D-190; [Engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), RWR-23, RWR-24 |
| D-235 | **F3: completed variations are version-control commits in isolated local-only history storage.** Accepted text follows existing guarded save and normal commits; no amend/reset/rebase/force-push. Do not prescribe workspace side refs: external push --mirror could publish them. The history contract must prove isolation even against that operation. Commit messages contain neither prompt nor note text. | Superseded by D-256 | Owner variation/version-control request, 2026-10-30; coordinator recon safety finding, 2026-10-30; D-23, D-133; [History](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#undo-and-redo), RWR-61 to RWR-66 |
| D-236 | **F4: disabled until setup; bring your own external OpenAI-compatible API.** No Focus Notes server/account/telemetry or silent fallback. One client adapter shape supports cloud and independently running user-attested local services without installing/downloading/embedding/launching/hosting/executing models or runtimes. ai-integration is planning guidance only, no scaffold or kit installation. This recommends a contract, not a live-tested service. | Superseded by D-257 | Owner request and client-only correction, 2026-10-30; PRV-01, D-01; [ai-integration method](https://github.com/prabinpebam/ai-integration); [Providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md) |
| D-237 | **Desktop only: Mod+K rewrites; link.edit moves to Mod+Shift+J.** App registry absence is not browser availability: Chrome and Edge reserve Ctrl+Shift+J for Console on Windows/Linux. Web rewriting is excluded, so web keeps Mod+K links and must never intercept Console, including user-binding validation. Desktop Mod+Shift+K is table-row deletion and Ctrl+Alt risks AltGr. Spotlight retains scoped Mod+K actions on both platforms. Desktop device hint, menus, cheat sheet, Help and tests migrate only at feature release. | Superseded by D-256 | Owner key request and desktop-only correction, 2026-10-30; D-16; [Chrome official shortcuts](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/devtools/open), [Edge official shortcuts](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-edge/devtools/shortcuts/) verified 2026-10-30; [Shortcut catalogue](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md); KEY-08, EDT-13 |
| D-238 | **Consent names the destination, model and scope.** Private local use requires locality attestation, private remote use per-request approval; changing the profile invalidates consent. Local URLs do not prove on-device inference. No provider is silently enabled, and shared private text follows the same policy. | Built as D-259, as AIS-13 and AIS-14 specify, in main and core (31 October, for the owner's 14:45 "Production harden this stuff"): per-connection consent covering the service, address, model and key, refused in main without it; *Don't send private notes* on by default, refused in main too. The dialog's cases and the Settings switch are built too (6a945f24), proven by the `air-safeguards` journey. Locality attestation and per-request approval for private text aren't built: the switch covers private notes instead | CP-SAFE-01, PRV-01, D-236; [Providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-43 |
| D-239 | **Bound cost before sending.** Recommend 64 KiB input, 8,192 output tokens, one request at a time, 20/day/profile; confirm cloud requests above 8 KiB and configured monetary threshold. Unknown price is unknown, never free. No automatic billable retry. Owner approval is required for pricing sources and actual caps before a real adapter is enabled. | Superseded by D-256 (limits in RWR-82) | No accidental spending derived from optional BYO provider, D-236; [Providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-44 |
| D-240 | **Source-aware mapping preserves bytes outside the confirmed target.** Exact splices where provable; otherwise preview expansion to a whole top-level source block, or refuse. Hashes guard apply; front-matter ID is untouched. Model output is untrusted supported Markdown, never active HTML or a trigger for fetches. | Superseded by D-256 | EDT-10 and R-03; owner's selected-text request, 2026-10-30; [Engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), RWR-21, RWR-22, RWR-27 |
| D-241 | **Rewrite joins existing save and conflict lanes.** Checkpoint acknowledgement precedes stream. Guarded apply observes afterWrites and exact-content checks; crashes and cancellation restore safely without erasing outside edits. New files are atomic, collision-safe, new-ID and same-visibility. A history success is not a successful note save. | Superseded by D-256 | D-190, D-221, D-222; CP-SAFE-01; [Engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), RWR-23, RWR-28, RWR-29 |
| D-242 | **Fixture-first delivery, then a separate owner gate.** Engine, prompt, fresh effect and local history are visible using an offline fake before choosing a service. Real adapters depend on recorded destination/auth/privacy/consent/cost and Store/privacy-claims approval. Rollback disables new sends, not writing or history recovery. | Superseded by D-257 and D-255 | Owner says not to block spec on provider selection; D-206; [Implementation plan](implementation-plan.md#mai), RWR-45, RWR-46 |
| D-243 | **Fresh text is ephemeral, accessible decoration.** Recommend 1.5 s hold and 2.5 s ease-out fade; all frames meet 4.5:1. Selection/caret/spelling/focus band retain priority; reduced motion, forced colors and effect-off show regular text. Review status is the non-color signal. No generated style enters Markdown or clipboard. | Superseded by D-256 | Owner gradient request, 2026-10-30; D-232, A11Y-01; [Design](../design/rewrite-with-ai.md), RWR-08, FOC-07 |
| D-244 | **Bounded local history, not workspace publication.** D-247's refinement is authoritative: 20 complete variations per note across sessions, 30 days, 100 MiB/public or private domain store and 200 MiB/workspace combined; whole-session eviction cannot remove unresolved original/only recovery data. Reopening does not reset capacity. Non-Git/unborn note folders use isolated bare history only when supported Git exists; absent Git blocks generation without download or a second format. Deleting refs is not secure erasure. | Superseded by D-256 | CP-SAFE-01, D-235, D-190, D-247; [History](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#undo-and-redo), RWR-63 to RWR-66 |
| D-250 | **Inspectable presets and visible scope, no ambient prompt history.** Presets populate, never send. Collapsed-caret desktop invocation highlights the current supported whole block; expanded source targets are explicit. Up to 20 deliberately saved device-only presets use 60-code-point names and the existing 4,000-point instruction limit. No automatic history/opt-in. | Superseded by D-256 | Owner request; [Docs](https://support.google.com/docs/answer/13447609?hl=en), [Craft saved prompts](https://support.craft.do/en/ai-assistant/custom-prompts) checked 2026-10-30 (Craft's own instruction limit is 1,000, not proof of ours); [Experience](../experience/rewrite-with-ai.md#the-flow), RWR-01 to RWR-04 |
| D-251 | **Bounded companion and protected review.** Below/above selection with 8 px gap, 12 px safe inset, 420 px width cap; top-dock at 480 × 360 below chrome, reflow at the distinct 360 × 320 BrowserWindow floor. Preview stays protected; Accept first to edit, so no edited-preview destructive confirmation exists. State-scoped keys and exact focus return preserve normal editor/table keys. | Superseded by D-256 | D-111, D-232, D-234; [Word selection review](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/word/copilot/edit-with-copilot-in-word), [Craft grouped undo](https://support.craft.do/en/ai-assistant/editing) checked 2026-10-30; geometry is our hypothesis; [Design](../design/rewrite-with-ai.md#surfaces), RWR-02, RWR-05, RWR-06 |
| D-252 | **Contrast-first fresh-text hypotheses, not a measured pass.** Specify light/dark gradient stops and continuous spatial/time contrast proof, 1,500 ms hold/2,500 ms fade under D-243, semantic token aliases and ordinary-text fallbacks. Reduced motion/forced colors/contrast use ordinary text immediately; badge/announcement carries the same fact. Gallery, measurement and accessibility tests remain planned. Focus Notes is an external-app/API client, never a model host; this spec installs/runs nothing. | Superseded by D-256 | D-243; [iA Authorship](https://ia.net/writer/support/editor/authorship) establishes gradient attribution, not timing; [WCAG contrast](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-minimum.html) checked 2026-10-30; [Design](../design/rewrite-with-ai.md#states), RWR-08, FOC-07, DSY-14 |

## Rewrite engine proposals (30 October 2026)

These are specification choices, not implemented behavior. Real provider, privacy, consent and cost approval remains an owner gate. D-249's detailed [limits](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md#addresses-and-requests) supersede D-239's provisional numeric defaults; D-247's [retention](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#undo-and-redo) tightens D-244's variation cap from session to note, retaining its 100 MiB/domain store limit. Prompt input remains 4,000 code points/16 KiB to match RWR-03/RWR-04; all instruction tokens count toward context/cost rather than a hidden smaller prompt limit.

| ID | Decision and source | Status | Affects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-245 | **Source-aware target mapping and exact byte splice.** Add plain-text leaf source spans; exact partial targeting only when unambiguous and structurally safe, otherwise visibly expand to the complete top-level source block. SHA-256 and exact UTF-8 comparison prove untouched prefix/suffix bytes, BOM/EOL and front matter survive. Existing `packages/markdown/src/index.ts` preserves top-level source blocks but has no inline map; its serializer can canonicalize separators near changed blocks. Source: EDT-10, D-70 and current parser/serializer recon, 30 October 2026. | Superseded by D-256 | [Rewrite engine → Selection mapping](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#in-the-editor), RWR-21, RWR-22, EDT-15, WP-AI.1 |
| D-246 | **Hold target-note autosave and ordinary commits through review.** Sending, streaming and review render a protected projection; canonical original plus independent edits are kept for recovery, never generated fragments. Accept alone creates one ordinary undoable replacement; leaving, switching, closing and quitting default to Discard, preserve outside edits/complete variation and save canonical text through the existing guarded lane. The five-second close handshake cannot accept silently. Source: QLT-06/D-190, D-92 and existing 60-second ordinary backup cadence. Amends the initial D-234 review-autosave hypothesis. | Superseded by D-256 | [Rewrite engine → Save and checkpoints](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#failure-and-recovery), [Crash recovery](../architecture/crash-recovery.md), RWR-23, RWR-24, RWR-28 |
| D-247 | **No-remote app-local Git history, not workspace side refs.** Plumbing commits use `refs/focus-notes/rewrite/v1/<opaqueNoteKey>/<sessionUuid>` in separate userData public/private bare stores; private blobs/index are OS-encrypted. No alternates, remote, workspace parent/index or branch mutation. Undo/redo appends states; retention deletes unpublished session refs. A namespace inside the workspace could escape via external `push --mirror`; isolated stores also handle non-Git/unborn folders without initializing them. Source: [Git commit-tree](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-commit-tree), consulted 30 October 2026, PRV-01/SEC-06 and current private-first Git sync boundary. Refines D-235/D-244. | Superseded by D-256 | [Rewrite history](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#undo-and-redo), RWR-61 through RWR-66 |
| D-248 | **Untrusted note/output, not an agent.** Separate instruction/data fields; no model tools, secret access, output-triggered network, imports or executable HTML. Parse bounded Markdown, reject unsupported/unsafe constructs and require complete validated output before review/Accept. Prompt framing is not a security guarantee. Source: SEC-01/PRV-01, existing raw-Markdown/safe-link behavior, and ai-integration `security.md` at `1613a55775f3dd06f157a73a7b2db0ddc0f68ea7` (offline review, 30 October 2026). | Accepted in part (D-256): the answer is plain text, no tools | [Rewrite engine → Output validation](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#in-the-main-process), [Rewrite providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-27, SEC-09 |
| D-249 | **Narrow default-off BYO external API contract.** Client only: no model/runtime installation, download, embedding, launch, hosting or execution; local endpoints are independently running. Recommend `chat-completions-text-v1` with reviewed route capabilities, numeric loopback-only local v1 (LAN deferred), no fallback, safeStorage references, per-use private-remote consent and strict unknown-charge blocking. Proposed admission defaults: USD 0.10/operation, USD 1/day; no automatic inference retries. Explicitly entered keys may pass transiently through a cleared key-entry field; stored plaintext never returns, avoiding an unproven native-dialog dependency. Source: owner's client-only correction, 30 October 2026; D-21/PRV-01, [Electron safeStorage](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/safe-storage), consulted 30 October 2026, and pinned ai-integration offline method/proof table. No real provider/model/destination approved. | Superseded by D-257 | [Rewrite providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-41 through RWR-48, PRV-05, SEC-09 |

The final spec refinements retain these decision IDs: D-245 includes ordered eligible file runs/excluded byte spans and replacement-only completion; D-246 distinguishes destination-owned New-file undo from source replacement; D-247 disallows a Git-absent second format and binds created-note history identity. D-248's link permission is a default-off typed capability, not inferred instruction text. D-249 binds exact instruction/policy/plan/route revisions into a main-computed one-use operation/consent/reservation and distinguishes the [documented APIs and proposed support order](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md#the-catalogue) from tested adapter support. D-250/251 require closed JSON compositions, one Positioner owner, primitive-owned44px targets, nonmodal dialog/F6 precedence and deterministic transition focus; D-252 keeps all measurement/axe/VVS proof planned. All remain Proposed; real-provider/privacy/cost approval is not supplied by this spec.

## Licensing policy (D-20 details)

| Class | Licenses | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Allowed | MIT, ISC, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, 0BSD, Apache-2.0, Unlicense, CC0-1.0, and OFL-1.1 for fonts (D-29) | Can ship. Keep their notices in a generated `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`. |
| Case by case | MPL-2.0, CC-BY-4.0 (icons and other assets), CC-BY-3.0 (the EFF wordlist, D-52) | Needs a recorded decision here. |
| Not allowed | GPL, LGPL, AGPL, SSPL, BUSL, any proprietary or paid license (including the paid Tiptap bundles) | CI fails the build. |

A license check runs in CI from M0 onward. Development-only tools follow the same list, unless their output never ships. Tools the user installs themselves (Git, Git Credential Manager, GitHub CLI) aren't shipped, so they aren't covered. The one exception is the Microsoft Store package's own Git (D-228): Git and the MSYS2 runtime (GPL-2.0) ship there as separate programs the app starts, never linked into it, with their notices and the exact source of each in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.

## Changing a decision

A change starts here, then follows [the articulation chain](../product/spec/chain.md): update the affected promise, scenario, or user-can first, then the views, components, and requirements that trace to it.
